# CLAUDE.md â€” MCX (Martin Coordinate Exchange)

A Windows desktop tool that converts survey coordinate files between the three
Michigan State Plane Coordinate System of 1983 zones, and between State Plane
and geodetic positions. It is used by a licensed professional surveyor to move
job coordinates across zone boundaries. **A wrong coordinate here lands on a
sealed survey or a recorded legal description and moves a boundary.** That
sentence calibrates everything below.

**Read `docs/DESIGN.md` before any engineering change.** It is the design source
of truth; this file is the working summary. The standing method is
`docs/method/METHOD.md` â€” defaults, not law; record deviations in the DESIGN.md
amendment log.

## What it is

Reads a PNEZD coordinate file (point, northing, easting, elevation,
description â€” no header row), converts it from one Michigan zone to another, and
writes three files: a clean PNEZD export for CAD, a full-audit CSV, and a
plain-text job record. Also converts geodetic â‡„ State Plane in either direction.
Reports grid scale factor, convergence angle, elevation factor and combined
factor per point, with geoid separation from a bundled GEOID18 grid.

Since the 0.7.0 build it also converts on the **19 Michigan SPCS2022 zones**,
with NATRF2022 geodetic in and out â€” any-to-any **within** each datum.

It deliberately does **not** do UTM, the **NAD 83 â†” NATRF2022 transformation**
(held on measured evidence, `docs/DEFERRED-NATRF2022-BRIDGE.md`), **NAPGD2022 /
GEOID2022 elevations** (`docs/DEFERRED-NAPGD2022.md`), other states, NAD 27, or
two-point azimuth/distance. See DESIGN.md Â§10, #61 and #62 for why each was
deferred; the two standing markers are the live ones.

## Status (2026-08-07, 0.3.1 RELEASED)

**0.3.1 removes three tooltips and nothing else** (DESIGN.md **#34**): the
longitude sign dropdown on **both** tabs (one shared control), the angle-format
selector, and both hemisphere letter boxes. Owner's instruction. Text was
removed, not information â€” the longitude control still names the convention, the
job record still states it, and `job.run` still refuses a geodetic job without
one. Suite **1132** green in both modes; all eight gates.

**This withdraws the tooltip mitigation #33 listed** for the positive-west
preselect, under #33's own ruling that verification is the user's
responsibility. #33 is annotated in place so the record does not cite a tooltip
that no longer exists.

**Trap worth remembering, recorded in #34:** the falsification seeding used
PowerShell `Set-Content`, which on 5.1 reads ANSI and writes UTF-8 **with a
BOM** â€” it silently added a BOM and mangled every em-dash in two source files,
and the suite stayed green because Python accepts a BOM. Caught by reading the
diff stat, not by a test. TOOLING.md's warning applies to throwaway seeding
commands too, where the diff usually goes unread.

## Superseded status: the WGS 84 build in flight, W0 done (2026-10-20)

**Read `docs/PLAN-wgs84.md` and DESIGN.md #70 before touching this work.**
The owner asked for WGS 84 â‡„ the other systems and instructed the build under
the established method. His scope: **WGS 84 (G1762), (G2139), (G2296)**, plus
**ITRF2014 and ITRF2020** by name, to and from NAD 83(2011) epoch 2010.0, with
the **observation epoch entered as a calendar date**. A bare "WGS 84" stays
refused by name. WGS 84 against NATRF2022 stays refused (#62).

**The oracle is NGS's own HTDP v3.6.0**, compiled from its committed source
(`review/wgs84-w0/htdp-v3.6.0/`, `-text` in `.gitattributes` because the
files mix line endings and are SHA-pinned). W0's Python transcription
reproduces it on **45,408 of 45,408** printed records, and the lead
re-derived the composition independently to 0.2 mm. **HTDP is asymmetric**:
Helmert at the input epoch, then velocity in the output frame. A velocity
model, HTDP's region-6 grid, is therefore part of the build: Helmert-only
misses by up to 28.5 cm in reverse. **Replicate it, non-closure and all.**

**0.7.1 is still the released version.** W1 is done: `htdp.py` + `htdp_data.py`,
1,846 anchors, version `0.8.0-dev`, every frozen record and 720 fresh random
records reproduced at HTDP's printed digit. W2 is done: five frames, ten
HTDP paths, and a guard (`convert.require_a_connected_path`) that refuses
every one until W3 wires it. **Interim gate 1 APPROVED** (seven LOW fixed,
two more from the re-confirmation fixed, no wrong number in 617,164
reviewer records). **W3 is done**: HTDP applied in convert and job.run, the
observation date gated, GEODETIC_TO_GEODETIC added; transformed jobs refuse
to WRITE until W4 (the write guard). **W4 and W4b are done**: transformed
jobs write and disclose; vertical-only geodetic records no longer state a zone
they do not have (inherited from 0.4.0; the release notes must say so). **Interim gate 2 is done**: 2 HIGH, 1 MEDIUM
and 5 LOW record and pin findings, no wrong number; all fixed, plus three
from the re-confirmation. **W5 is done**: the five frames, the date control and
both positions reach both tabs (offscreen previews in `review/wgs84-w5/`).
**W6 is done**: self-test 13 checks, HTDP tripwire probe,
`docs/REFREEZE-HTDP.md`, the Windows htdp.exe checker, and DRAFT notes
`docs/RELEASE-NOTES-0.8.0.md`. **The closing gate is done and APPROVED** over fbcbf4a..HEAD (1 MEDIUM, 7 LOW, then 3 LOW and a final 2 LOW of release-notes wording; no wrong number in any gate of this build; `review/gate-wgs84-closing/`). Next is the owner's: screen review, `review/wgs84-w0/check_ngs_windows_binary.py` with NGS's htdp.exe, one real WGS 84/PPP dataset, the clean-profile install, then `py tools/build_release.py --acknowledge-ngs-beta`. Open product questions for him are in DESIGN.md #70 (the ITRF early-date warning, the screen-review items). **This container's baseline**: 6 environmental failures, the same with
and without the work (DESIGN.md #65's five plus #69's half-way-point anchor,

### The finding that changed the plan, and the one still open

**Plan Â§2.5 is superseded (Â§2.5a).** It said `.trn` is biquadratic and `.err`
bilinear, and asked for a pin that fails if the two are unified. **That pin would
have enshrined a defect.** `ngs_grid.interpolate_biquadratic` anchors its 3Ã—3
stencil at `int(row) - 1`, off-centre; both VERTCON grids want it centred on the
nearest node. Then both are biquadratic and both land at **0.47 mm** against NCAT,
20/20 exact, where the old scheme reaches 8.46 mm. **Verified bit-identical to
NOAA's own published algorithm** â€” `Vertcon.java`'s `getGridRow` transcribed
literally, max difference exactly 0.0 over 18,000 positions.

**DESIGN.md #8 is corrected.** It said NGS does not document INTG's scheme. NGS
does: NOAA TM NOS NGS-84, and `intg.f` anchors with `nint()` â€” nearest node. So
**GEOID18's anchoring is not INTG's**, a claim three docstrings and the 0.1.0
release notes carried.

**OWNER'S INSTRUCTION, 2026-08-07: replicate NOAA as closely as possible, they
are the authority.** That decides the open question â€” **GEOID18 is to be
re-anchored to nearest-node**, as its own work package, because it touches
released code and needs its own discriminating anchors (the existing 20 cannot
tell the schemes apart; 120 points sampled where they diverge give floor rms
0.715 mm against nearest-node 0.454). Cost of the current anchoring is ~4 mm in a
*reported geoid separation* and ~6e-10 in an elevation factor â€” **no coordinate
moves** â€” which is why it was not folded into a vertical-datum build.

**STILL OPEN, and it is a disclosure decision for WP-V7:** the `.err` grid
interpolates **negative** at ~0.43% of Michigan positions (956 of 223,850 sampled,
worst âˆ’0.027 m). A negative one-sigma is not a quantity. **NOAA's own published
code produces the same negatives; NOAA's live NCAT service returns a positive
value there** (+0.011 m where we compute âˆ’0.00965 m), and no rule maps one to the
other â€” not `abs()`, not clamping. So `sigma_m` **refuses**, `modeled_error_raw_m`
keeps the raw value readable under a name that cannot be mistaken for an
uncertainty, and **the shift is unaffected and still reported**. All three
paper-overs are pinned as failures.

## Superseded status (2026-08-07, 0.2.0 RELEASED)

**Closing adversarial gate is done and every finding is fixed.** Three
independent tracks ran blind to each other â€” Codex CLI, an Opus reviewer, and a
**live NGS NCAT cross-check** of every pipeline through the real file path. The
mathematics was confirmed correct by all three: 666 live comparisons, all pass,
single-leg agreement 0.5 mm, chained zone-to-zone 0.9 mm, scale factor and
convergence exact to NCAT's printed precision. Both reviewers re-derived Â§3.1
from the manual independently (80 and 60 digits) and matched to ~1e-9 m.

Seventeen defects were found, **all in the contract/record/safety-gate layer,
none in a coordinate**, and all are now fixed, pinned with the reviewer's own
counterexample, and each pin falsified. Two interim-gate fixes recorded in #11
as landed had not landed (the `constants=` seam, the longitude-convention
default) and two interim pins had never been written at all. Full account:
DESIGN.md amendment **#20**.

**Suite: 546 â†’ 855**, green in both `pytest` and `-O`, exit codes asserted
directly, run unpiped. The 13 live cross-check points are frozen as fixtures and
now drive **file â†’ job.run â†’ ZIP â†’ parsed audit CSV** for all three directions
in all three units â€” the end-to-end path that had no anchors before, and whose
absence is why the record defects survived.

**Extensibility: REWORK-REQUIRED, recorded not built** (amendment #21). Â§6's
claim that SPCS2022 arrives as data did not survive review. Michigan's published
SPCS2022 design is 19 zones on NATRF2022 (statewide oblique Mercator + 18 LDPs,
13 LC1 and 5 TM) â€” *not* the three Lambert zones Â§6 assumed. Zone parameters are
published and stable; NATRF2022 and its transformation are not released, so the
frame refusal stays and the zone layer is deliberately not built yet.

**Vertical datums sized, recorded not built** (amendment #22): NGVD 29 â†’ NAVD 88
is MEDIUM, two work packages, everything needed available today; NAPGD2022 is
blocked but seam-able via a transformation registry.

**0.2.0 IS RELEASED â€” the program is now MCX, Martin Coordinate Exchange.**
Renamed throughout (window title, shortcuts, installer, job record, executable
`mcx.exe`); publisher corrected to **DMARTIN** in Installed apps and the version
resource; the "COORD CONVERT" lettering removed from the artwork at every size.
The Python package stays `michspc` on purpose. The AppId is unchanged so this
upgrades in place, with an `[InstallDelete]` clearing the old name's leftovers.
No computation changed. DESIGN.md **#25**.

**0.1.1** fixed the icon's fake transparency: the master had a checkerboard
*painted into it* â€” all 1,048,576 pixels opaque â€” so the transparent background
three parts of the build assumed never existed (DESIGN.md **#24**).

**0.1.0** remains the release the verification record describes. Suite 898,
green in both modes; all eight build gates pass for each release.

The narrowing re-confirmation closed 10 of 11 findings, found one defect the
fixes had introduced (fixed and pinned, amendment #23), and one item is accepted
weak with reasons recorded: the final rename is not write-through, so the
failure mode is an absent archive, never a corrupt one.

### Open for the owner

1. **The install proof is human and still outstanding** (METHOD.md Â§6): install
   on a clean profile and run one real job end to end. A self-test is not a
   substitute.
2. **A real PNEZD file from an actual job** is still worth having. The reader is
   built to a documented convention, not to a real export.
3. ~~The icon's small-size lettering~~ â€” **closed at 0.2.0.** The lettering is
   out of the artwork at every size, so nothing smears at 16 and 32 px and no
   size-specific variant was needed.
4. **The `Geodetic (latitude / longitude)` dropdown entry** was a subagent
   addition to the owner-approved layout. It works, it is tested end to end, and
   he has not looked at it.

### Next build, when it comes

- **NGVD 29 â†’ NAVD 88 â€” THIS IS THE NEXT BUILD, and it is now PLANNED: see
  `docs/PLAN-vertical-datums.md` and the status section at the top of this file.**
  Owner's decision at the close of 0.3.0 (DESIGN.md **#32**); sizing is #22 and
  stands as written â€” the V0 gate confirmed #22's data claims in every
  particular. MEDIUM, every input available today. Build the **vertical-transformation
  registry keyed by (source, target)** rather than one hard-wired path, because
  that registry is how NAPGD2022 later arrives as data. Two risks, neither about
  effort: disclosing a *modeled* shift inside a number that looks exact, and the
  `trn` grid's sign/direction semantics â€” the defect class this project has
  already been burned by.
- **NAPGD2022, after it** â€” blocked on NGS, not on us (#22). **Backwards
  compatibility is a requirement, not an assumption:** it does not retire
  NAVD 88 or NGVD 29. A job converted in 2026 must still convert and still
  reproduce afterwards, so the registry keeps every pair it has carried,
  elevations stay datum-tagged, the datum in force is named in every output, and
  an unestablished datum refuses rather than assuming the newest.
- **SPCS2022 â€” not next, and DOWNSTREAM OF NAPGD2022, not parallel to it.** The
  modernized NSRS has two halves that arrive together: SPCS2022 coordinates are
  defined on **NATRF2022**, and **NAPGD2022** is the geopotential half
  (GEOID2022 replacing GEOID18 and NAVD 88). This program reports elevation and
  combined factors, so an SPCS2022 point carrying a GEOID18/NAVD 88 factor mixes
  two eras inside one number â€” and 18 of Michigan's 19 zones are LDPs designed
  at a topographic height, where the height side is load-bearing. So the order
  above is a dependency order. Spec is #21; its own blockers are unchanged â€” no
  official NAD83(2011)â†’NATRF2022 transformation, and the transverse and oblique
  Mercator engines this program does not have.
- **Rename durability** (#23) â€” if it is ever worth it, `MoveFileEx` with
  `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH` via `ctypes`.

## Repo layout

```
michspc/spc/      computation core â€” stdlib only; no Qt, no file I/O, no network
michspc/fileio/   readers and writers; the ONLY layer that touches csv or the
                  NGS binary grids. Named fileio, never io (shadows stdlib).
michspc/gui/      PySide6; never computes a domain result
tests/            suite; every expected value hand-derived in a comment
data/             four NGS grids, unmodified, each SHA-256 pinned: g2018u3.bin,
                  g2012bu3.bin, and the VERTCON 3.0 .trn/.err pair
review/           committed review harnesses and captured NGS truth (#36)
docs/             DESIGN.md (authority), method/, the NOAA manual, reference/
```

## Non-negotiable conventions (enforce in review)

- Units explicit at every boundary; International feet is the default because
  Michigan legislated it, and the unit in force is stated in every output file.
- Longitude sign convention: **no default in the core** â€” `JobSettings`
  requires it and `job.run` refuses a geodetic job without it. The GUI dropdown
  opens on positive-west (DESIGN.md #29, owner's instruction); that is a
  decision, not a regression, and #29 says why.
- Fail closed, never fabricate; refusals name the offending item.
- Missing elevation writes `N/A` in factor columns â€” never `1.0`.
- One authoritative representation per fact; derived values are never stored.
- One entry point per data path; loaders validate as strictly as the UI.
- No uncited constants â€” document and page, in an adjacent comment.
- Exports never silently clobber; atomic stage-and-rename.
- No load-bearing asserts in production code; the suite runs under `-O`.
- Cross-frame conversion refuses loudly. Never a silent pass-through.
- The rigorous Â§3.1 Lambert equations are the only computation path. What
  verifies them is external and lives in the suite: the frozen NGS NCAT anchors
  and the published Appendix C constants (DESIGN.md #14).

## Development process

Work packages by subagents â†’ session lead independently re-derives load-bearing
math before accepting â†’ independent adversarial review (Codex CLI) at the build
midpoint AND at closing â†’ fix at the root, pin each finding with the reviewer's
own counterexample, falsify the pin, then narrowing re-confirmation until
approved. Commit per gated milestone; push after gates. Suite green in both
`pytest` and `-O` modes before any commit.

freezes a script, not `-m`.
# PLAN: Options menu, Auto zone, and the observation date in Options

**Status: PROPOSAL, written by the session lead 2026-10-22.** Ships in 0.8.0
together with the WGS 84 build (`docs/PLAN-wgs84.md`). DESIGN.md #71 opens it.
Recon: `review/autozone-r0/RECON.md` (zone boundaries, NCAT as truth) and the
lead's GUI/job map (summarised in Â§2).

## 1. Scope

### Settled by the owner (2026-10-22)

1. **An Options menu** that switches groups on and off in every system
   dropdown: SPCS 83 zones, SPCS2022 zones, NAD83(2011) geodetic, NATRF2022
   geodetic, and each of the five WGS 84 / ITRF frames. All on by default
   except the five, which are off. **Remembered between runs.**
2. **Auto zone.** An "Auto" entry at the top of the State Plane zone section
   of the list, on both tabs. It picks the zone from each point's
   latitude/longitude, **per point**. On NATRF2022 it picks the **local
   low-distortion zone**, never the statewide one.
3. **The Auto frame is an Options setting** (NAD83(2011) or NATRF2022). A
   lat/long in the other frame is refused for now (no published NAD 83 â‡„
   NATRF2022 transformation, #62); when that bridge exists it converts.
4. **A Multi point file that spans zones writes one CAD (clean PNEZD) file
   per zone.** The audit CSV and the job record name each point's zone.
5. **The observation date leaves both tabs** and moves into Options: "today"
   by default, or a date the user enters. The job record says which.
6. ITRF early-date warning: not added (as built).

### Settled by the session lead, open to the owner's review

- **L1. Auto is an output-side entry only.** A northing/easting does not say
  which zone it is in (the zones overlap in grid space), so Auto is offered
  only as the TO system when the FROM system is geodetic. On the FROM combo it
  is hidden.
- **L2. Auto follows the existing cross-frame rule (#64).** When the Auto
  frame and the input lat/long's frame have no published path, the Auto entry
  is grayed and, if selected, cleared with `FRAME_RESET_STATUS`; `job.run`
  refuses the same pair by name. WGS 84 / ITRF input works with Auto on
  NAD83 (the zone is chosen from the NAD 83 pivot position after HTDP); with
  Auto on NATRF2022 it is refused, as WGS 84 â†’ SPCS2022 already is.
- **L3. The zone is chosen by county, as the law defines it.** Both systems
  are county unions, Great Lakes waters included (MCL 54.231(3)â€“(5) for
  SPCS 83; NGS's SPCS2022 zones measured as county unions to 0.026 m, with
  Isle Royale split from Keweenaw). MCX bundles **83 water-extended county
  polygons, unsimplified**, plus two tables: county â†’ SPCS 83 zone
  (transcribed from the statute, cited) and county â†’ SPCS2022 LDP zone
  (measured from NGS's beta polygons, `NGS beta` token, REFREEZE-NSRS row),
  and the Isle Royale rule. NGS's own SPCS2022 polygons are land-only and
  would refuse Great Lakes points NCAT accepts, so they are evidence, not
  the bundled data.
- **L4. Point-in-polygon on the job's own geodetic position** (NAD 83(2011)
  for Auto-NAD83, including the HTDP pivot; NATRF2022 for Auto-NATRF). The
  ~1.2 m between the two is inside every boundary band below.
- **L5. A point in no county is refused by name**, never given the nearest
  zone. Outside Michigan, and in Canadian water, is refused.
- **L6.** Superseded by O2.
- **L7. Options are window-level, shared by both tabs.** This reverses
  #26/#43's "tabs share no state" for the Options only; every other control
  stays per tab. Changing any Option clears every displayed Single point
  result (the #26 stale-result rule) and reapplies the dropdown filter.
- **L8. Hiding a group that holds a current selection clears that
  selection** with a status line, exactly as #64's strand rule does. At
  least one geodetic group and one zone group must stay on; the dialog
  refuses an all-off state rather than leaving an empty dropdown.
- **L9. Persistence through `QSettings`** (per user,
  `HKCU\Software\DMARTIN\MCX - Martin Coordinate Exchange`), behind a seam so
  the suite never reads or writes the owner's real registry. A stored value
  MCX does not recognise falls back to the default **and says so** on the
  status line; it never crashes and never guesses.
- **L10. The observation date in Options.** "Today" is the **local** date the
  job runs (Michigan), not the later-of-local-and-UTC used to avoid false
  future refusals. The core keeps refusing a missing date: the GUI now always
  sends one, plus a required `ObservationDateSource` (TODAY or ENTERED) which
  the record prints ("2026-10-22, the date the job ran (Options default)" or
  "â€¦, entered in Options"). A stray source on a job with no WGS 84/ITRF frame
  is refused, as a stray date is. **This reverses PLAN-wgs84 D10's no-default
  rule, on the owner's instruction**, for the "today" choice only; O3.
- **L11. Non-Auto jobs are byte-identical** to the WGS 84 build's output, held
  by the existing digest pins (v0.5.0, pre-W4, W4 disclosure). Every change
  in the outputs is gated on Auto.

### Owner decisions on the lead's questions (2026-10-22)

- **O1. County lines: US Census TIGER 2025** (what NGS built SPCS2022 from,
  and what NCAT uses for it). Measured against the State of Michigan
  Geographic Framework: on land 0.04 m median, 40 m worst; in the lakes
  116 m median, 444 m worst; the North/Central water line up to 11.8 km
  where it meets the international line. NCAT's SPCS 83 land line is 3-17 m
  off TIGER in places; the record names TIGER as the source.
- **O2. Near a zone line: assign only.** A point takes the zone of the
  county it falls in, with no warning and no refusal (MCL 54.234 permits
  either zone where named; the record names each point's zone and county as
  facts). L6 is superseded by this.
- **O3. The observation date.** Options holds a choice: **today** (default)
  or **enter a date**. The choice is remembered; the date is not. With
  "enter a date", the existing date box appears on the main tab, opening
  unanswered ("â€” choose â€”") every time MCX starts, and Convert waits for it
  (the WGS 84 build's widget and gating, kept). With "today", no date box is
  shown and the job uses the local date it runs.

## 2. What the recon found that shapes the build

- One function builds all four combos (`controls.zone_combo`); rows can be
  hidden (`setRowHidden`) without rebuilding, so every `findData` and index
  pin stays valid. `direction_for` fails closed on an unknown item type, so
  the Auto item is refused until each consumer handles it.
- The core already converts per point (`project_point(..., target_zone)`,
  `PointConversion.target_zone`), but every output reads
  `settings.target_zone`: `exports.output_stem`, the audit "Target zone"
  cell, `report._job_zones`, the TO block, the "Both zones â€¦ exact
  re-projection" sentence, the panel's Zone row, the unit and frame gates.
  With Auto, `target_zone` is None, and each of these crashes, prints a false
  sentence, or **skips a gate silently** (US survey feet on SPCS2022). Every
  one is a work item and a pin.
- `verify_round_trip` checks one clean file against all points; with one file
  per zone it runs per file on that file's points. `_verify_archive` refuses
  an empty member, so a zone with no points gets no file.
- Gate 6 refuses any bundled file byte-identical to one under `review/`, so
  the county file is derived (re-serialised, digest-pinned), never copied.
- NCAT returns the zone for a lat/long in both systems (production REST for
  SPCS 83; the beta web form for SPCS2022). It disagrees with itself near
  the Osceola/Mecosta line because its two systems use different county
  data, so anchors are frozen **outside** the boundary bands only.

## 3. Architecture

- **`michspc/spc/autozone.py`** (core, stdlib): `zone_for(lat, lon, frame)`
  â†’ `ZoneAssignment(zone, county, distance_to_line_m | None)` or
  `AutoZoneRefusal` naming the point. Pure point-in-polygon (even-odd, with a
  documented on-edge rule) over polygons handed in; the county tables are
  core constants with their citations.
- **`michspc/fileio/counties.py`**: the only reader of
  `data/mi_counties_<source>.json` (derived, SHA-256 pinned, geometry
  checks on load as the NGS grids have).
- **`job.py`**: `JobSettings.target_zone` may be `AUTO` (a sentinel object,
  not None) with `auto_frame`; per point the zone is chosen after any HTDP
  pivot, then projected. Unit gate checks the unit against EVERY zone the
  Auto frame can choose; frame gate against the Auto frame.
- **Outputs**: one clean member per zone used, `<stem>_<zone>.csv`; the audit
  CSV's Target zone cell names the point's zone; the record's TO block lists
  each zone used with its point count, the county rule, the data source and
  digest; the audit CSV names each point's county (O2).
- **GUI**: `Options` menu â†’ dialog (toggles, Auto frame, observation date);
  window-level `Options` object read by both tabs; Auto row at the top of
  the zone section; date widgets removed from both tabs.

## 4. Work packages (Opus builders to the lead's brief; lead reviews every diff)

- **Z0** derive the county file and both tables from the recon captures;
  freeze NCAT zone anchors (both systems, outside the bands, including
  Great Lakes, Isle Royale, out-of-state refusals).
- **Z1** `autozone.py` + `counties.py`, pinned to the anchors.
- **Z2** `job.run` Auto: per-point zone, refusals, both gates. **Interim
  Codex gate 1 over Z0â€“Z2.**
- **Z3** outputs: per-zone clean files, verifiers, audit, record (Auto only;
  every non-Auto digest unchanged).
- **Z4** Options dialog, persistence seam, toggles, Auto entry, the date
  box shown only under "enter a date", both tabs; offscreen previews for the owner.
- **Z5** packaging (spec datas, data pin, LAZY_IMPORTS, gate 6), self-test
  check for Auto, REFREEZE rows, release notes 0.8.0 updated. **Closing
  Codex gate over Z0â€“Z5**, fixes, narrowing re-confirmation.

Each package: hand-derived expected values, every pin falsified by seeding
(snapshot restore, never `git checkout --`), suite green in `pytest` and
`-O` before commit.

271660a Z0-Z1: the county data and the Auto zone lookup

data/mi_counties_tiger2025.json: Michigan's 83 water-extended TIGER 2025
counties, unsimplified, derived by a stdlib script (sha256 53664dd8...).
County -> SPCS 83 zone transcribed from MCL 54.231 and re-checked against
the captured statute; county -> SPCS2022 local zone from NGS's beta
polygons (NGS beta, REFREEZE-NSRS rows), Isle Royale split at 47.65 N.
zone_for() is even-odd point-in-polygon with a deterministic on-edge rule
proven on all 30,871 shared TIGER edges; a point in no county, or two, is
refused. 247 NCAT anchors outside the boundary bands, all agreeing. Twelve
seeds caught. Lead checked the 83-county SPCS 83 table against an
independent list and spot-checked known places. Suite 5,551 both modes.

Not yet bundled: michspc.spec gains the data file in Z5 (gate 5 refuses a
release build until then).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

a425a2f Z2: Auto zone in job.run, written only once Z3 discloses it

JobSettings.target_zone may be AUTO_ZONE, a typed singleton, with a
required auto_frame (NAD83(2011) -> SPCS 83 zones, NATRF2022 -> the 18
local SPCS2022 zones). Each point's zone is chosen by county from its
position in the auto frame (after HTDP for WGS 84 / ITRF input) and
projected by the unchanged fixed-zone call: every ConvertedPoint field is
identical to the fixed-zone job's, the one difference being that an Auto
point carries no OUTSIDE_ZONE_EXTENT warning (its zone is the statute's).
A point in no county, or two, refuses the whole job naming every such
point. Cross-frame pairs refuse through require_frame_path; the unit gate
checks every zone Auto can choose. ObservationDateSource (TODAY uses the
local date) is transitional until Z4. A write guard refuses to write an
Auto job until Z3. 25 seeds caught; lead re-ran a three-zone job against
fixed-zone jobs. Suite 5,611 in both modes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2e06514 Interim gate 1 (Codex): the SPCS2022 table is tested on NGS's native geometry

Codex over Z0-Z2 returned FINDINGS: one HIGH, one MEDIUM, no wrong zone
(review/gate-autozone-1/). This commit fixes the MEDIUM: the table test
and re-freeze harness read mi_4269.json, which NGS's server had shifted
~0.9 m, while the cited 0.026 m agreement was measured on the native Web
Mercator capture. They now read mi_native.json (SHA-pinned, EPSG 1024
inverse, no datum shift): worst NGS-to-TIGER 0.025 m over 11,875 edges.
The reviewer's Livingston counterexample is pinned, with the shifted file
as a negative control; two seeds caught. Table unchanged.

The HIGH (public renderers bypassing the write guard) is fixed in Z3,
which replaces the guard. capture_anchors.py still picks anchor positions
on the shifted file; immaterial at the anchors' >= 1,084 m clearance.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

26e2d9c Z3: Auto jobs write one CAD file per zone, and the record states the rule

An Auto job's archive is <input>_AUTO-SPCS83 / _AUTO-SPCS2022: one clean
PNEZD file per zone used (<stem>_<zone>.csv, that zone's points in file
order, none for an unused zone), each verified on its own points; the
audit CSV names each point's zone, county and FIPS; the record's TO block
lists the zones used with counts and files, the county rule (MCL 54.231
for SPCS 83, measured NGS polygons and the Isle Royale rule for SPCS2022),
the TIGER data and its digest, the on-line rule, and MCL 54.234 (SPCS 83
only). The write guard is gone; interim gate 1's HIGH is fixed at its
root: clean_points() requires a zone on an Auto job and refuses one the
job did not use, so cross-zone rows reach no writer. The record states an
observation date's source when one is given. 223 non-Auto configurations
byte-identical to a425a2f (667 members); 13 seeds caught. Suite 5,666 in
both modes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

18de4b3 Z4: the Options menu, the Auto entry, and the observation date in Options

An "Options..." menu opens a dialog: which groups the system lists show
(the five WGS 84 / ITRF frames off by default), the Auto zone frame
(NAD83(2011) -> SPCS 83, NATRF2022 -> SPCS2022 local zones), and the
observation date (today, or a date entered on the tab). Remembered per
user in QSettings behind a seam the suite never lets reach the registry;
never a date value; an unrecognised stored value resets and says so.
Hidden rows are also disabled (Qt's keys and wheel reach hidden rows);
a hidden or stranded selection is cleared with a status line, and any
Options change discards a displayed Single point result. Auto sits in its
own block at the top of the zone section of the TO lists, grayed per #64
when its frame has no path from the input. Results show each point's own
zone and county on both tabs. The GUI always sends a date source; the
core keeps it optional (a frozen record digest and ~180 core tests state
none, and a record without one says nothing false). Previews in
review/autozone-z4/. 19 seeds caught. Suite 5,739 in both modes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

207645f Z5: the county file ships, self-test check 14, notes and status updated

michspc.spec bundles data/mi_counties_tiger2025.json beside the NGS grids;
seven modules declared in LAZY_IMPORTS. Self-test check 14 runs an Auto
job through job.run at two production-NCAT anchors (Kent, MI-S; Marquette,
MI-N): NCAT's zone and county, coordinates identical to the fixed-zone
job and within 0.28 mm of NCAT, and a Toledo point refusing the job. A
trial bundle passed gates 5-7 with "SELF-TEST PASSED - 14 checks". The
beta acknowledgement count is now 7. The core's optional date source is
documented as decided; a sweep pins that both tabs send one whenever they
send a date. Release notes (DRAFT) cover Options, Auto zone and the date;
CLAUDE.md gains the current state. 9 seeds caught. Suite 5,754 both modes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

271660a Z0-Z1: the county data and the Auto zone lookup

data/mi_counties_tiger2025.json: Michigan's 83 water-extended TIGER 2025
counties, unsimplified, derived by a stdlib script (sha256 53664dd8...).
County -> SPCS 83 zone transcribed from MCL 54.231 and re-checked against
the captured statute; county -> SPCS2022 local zone from NGS's beta
polygons (NGS beta, REFREEZE-NSRS rows), Isle Royale split at 47.65 N.
zone_for() is even-odd point-in-polygon with a deterministic on-edge rule
proven on all 30,871 shared TIGER edges; a point in no county, or two, is
refused. 247 NCAT anchors outside the boundary bands, all agreeing. Twelve
seeds caught. Lead checked the 83-county SPCS 83 table against an
independent list and spot-checked known places. Suite 5,551 both modes.

Not yet bundled: michspc.spec gains the data file in Z5 (gate 5 refuses a
release build until then).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

a425a2f Z2: Auto zone in job.run, written only once Z3 discloses it

JobSettings.target_zone may be AUTO_ZONE, a typed singleton, with a
required auto_frame (NAD83(2011) -> SPCS 83 zones, NATRF2022 -> the 18
local SPCS2022 zones). Each point's zone is chosen by county from its
position in the auto frame (after HTDP for WGS 84 / ITRF input) and
projected by the unchanged fixed-zone call: every ConvertedPoint field is
identical to the fixed-zone job's, the one difference being that an Auto
point carries no OUTSIDE_ZONE_EXTENT warning (its zone is the statute's).
A point in no county, or two, refuses the whole job naming every such
point. Cross-frame pairs refuse through require_frame_path; the unit gate
checks every zone Auto can choose. ObservationDateSource (TODAY uses the
local date) is transitional until Z4. A write guard refuses to write an
Auto job until Z3. 25 seeds caught; lead re-ran a three-zone job against
fixed-zone jobs. Suite 5,611 in both modes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2e06514 Interim gate 1 (Codex): the SPCS2022 table is tested on NGS's native geometry

Codex over Z0-Z2 returned FINDINGS: one HIGH, one MEDIUM, no wrong zone
(review/gate-autozone-1/). This commit fixes the MEDIUM: the table test
and re-freeze harness read mi_4269.json, which NGS's server had shifted
~0.9 m, while the cited 0.026 m agreement was measured on the native Web
Mercator capture. They now read mi_native.json (SHA-pinned, EPSG 1024
inverse, no datum shift): worst NGS-to-TIGER 0.025 m over 11,875 edges.
The reviewer's Livingston counterexample is pinned, with the shifted file
as a negative control; two seeds caught. Table unchanged.

The HIGH (public renderers bypassing the write guard) is fixed in Z3,
which replaces the guard. capture_anchors.py still picks anchor positions
on the shifted file; immaterial at the anchors' >= 1,084 m clearance.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

26e2d9c Z3: Auto jobs write one CAD file per zone, and the record states the rule

An Auto job's archive is <input>_AUTO-SPCS83 / _AUTO-SPCS2022: one clean
PNEZD file per zone used (<stem>_<zone>.csv, that zone's points in file
order, none for an unused zone), each verified on its own points; the
audit CSV names each point's zone, county and FIPS; the record's TO block
lists the zones used with counts and files, the county rule (MCL 54.231
for SPCS 83, measured NGS polygons and the Isle Royale rule for SPCS2022),
the TIGER data and its digest, the on-line rule, and MCL 54.234 (SPCS 83
only). The write guard is gone; interim gate 1's HIGH is fixed at its
root: clean_points() requires a zone on an Auto job and refuses one the
job did not use, so cross-zone rows reach no writer. The record states an
observation date's source when one is given. 223 non-Auto configurations
byte-identical to a425a2f (667 members); 13 seeds caught. Suite 5,666 in
both modes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

18de4b3 Z4: the Options menu, the Auto entry, and the observation date in Options

An "Options..." menu opens a dialog: which groups the system lists show
(the five WGS 84 / ITRF frames off by default), the Auto zone frame
(NAD83(2011) -> SPCS 83, NATRF2022 -> SPCS2022 local zones), and the
observation date (today, or a date entered on the tab). Remembered per
user in QSettings behind a seam the suite never lets reach the registry;
never a date value; an unrecognised stored value resets and says so.
Hidden rows are also disabled (Qt's keys and wheel reach hidden rows);
a hidden or stranded selection is cleared with a status line, and any
Options change discards a displayed Single point result. Auto sits in its
own block at the top of the zone section of the TO lists, grayed per #64
when its frame has no path from the input. Results show each point's own
zone and county on both tabs. The GUI always sends a date source; the
core keeps it optional (a frozen record digest and ~180 core tests state
none, and a record without one says nothing false). Previews in
review/autozone-z4/. 19 seeds caught. Suite 5,739 in both modes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

207645f Z5: the county file ships, self-test check 14, notes and status updated

michspc.spec bundles data/mi_counties_tiger2025.json beside the NGS grids;
seven modules declared in LAZY_IMPORTS. Self-test check 14 runs an Auto
job through job.run at two production-NCAT anchors (Kent, MI-S; Marquette,
MI-N): NCAT's zone and county, coordinates identical to the fixed-zone
job and within 0.28 mm of NCAT, and a Toledo point refusing the job. A
trial bundle passed gates 5-7 with "SELF-TEST PASSED - 14 checks". The
beta acknowledgement count is now 7. The core's optional date source is
documented as decided; a sweep pins that both tabs send one whenever they
send a date. Release notes (DRAFT) cover Options, Auto zone and the date;
CLAUDE.md gains the current state. 9 seeds caught. Suite 5,754 both modes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

C:\claude-projects\coord-convert-gate-azc\review\gate-autozone-1\prompt.txt
# PLAN: Options menu, Auto zone, and the observation date in Options

**Status: PROPOSAL, written by the session lead 2026-10-22.** Ships in 0.8.0
together with the WGS 84 build (`docs/PLAN-wgs84.md`). DESIGN.md #71 opens it.
Recon: `review/autozone-r0/RECON.md` (zone boundaries, NCAT as truth) and the
lead's GUI/job map (summarised in §2).

## 1. Scope

### Settled by the owner (2026-10-22)

1. **An Options menu** that switches groups on and off in every system
   dropdown: SPCS 83 zones, SPCS2022 zones, NAD83(2011) geodetic, NATRF2022
   geodetic, and each of the five WGS 84 / ITRF frames. All on by default
   except the five, which are off. **Remembered between runs.**
2. **Auto zone.** An "Auto" entry at the top of the State Plane zone section
   of the list, on both tabs. It picks the zone from each point's
   latitude/longitude, **per point**. On NATRF2022 it picks the **local
   low-distortion zone**, never the statewide one.
3. **The Auto frame is an Options setting** (NAD83(2011) or NATRF2022). A
   lat/long in the other frame is refused for now (no published NAD 83 ⇄
   NATRF2022 transformation, #62); when that bridge exists it converts.
4. **A Multi point file that spans zones writes one CAD (clean PNEZD) file
   per zone.** The audit CSV and the job record name each point's zone.
5. **The observation date leaves both tabs** and moves into Options: "today"
   by default, or a date the user enters. The job record says which.
6. ITRF early-date warning: not added (as built).

### Settled by the session lead, open to the owner's review

- **L1. Auto is an output-side entry only.** A northing/easting does not say
  which zone it is in (the zones overlap in grid space), so Auto is offered
  only as the TO system when the FROM system is geodetic. On the FROM combo it
  is hidden.
- **L2. Auto follows the existing cross-frame rule (#64).** When the Auto
  frame and the input lat/long's frame have no published path, the Auto entry
  is grayed and, if selected, cleared with `FRAME_RESET_STATUS`; `job.run`
  refuses the same pair by name. WGS 84 / ITRF input works with Auto on
  NAD83 (the zone is chosen from the NAD 83 pivot position after HTDP); with
  Auto on NATRF2022 it is refused, as WGS 84 → SPCS2022 already is.
- **L3. The zone is chosen by county, as the law defines it.** Both systems
  are county unions, Great Lakes waters included (MCL 54.231(3)–(5) for
  SPCS 83; NGS's SPCS2022 zones measured as county unions to 0.026 m, with
  Isle Royale split from Keweenaw). MCX bundles **83 water-extended county
  polygons, unsimplified**, plus two tables: county → SPCS 83 zone
  (transcribed from the statute, cited) and county → SPCS2022 LDP zone
  (measured from NGS's beta polygons, `NGS beta` token, REFREEZE-NSRS row),
  and the Isle Royale rule. NGS's own SPCS2022 polygons are land-only and
  would refuse Great Lakes points NCAT accepts, so they are evidence, not
  the bundled data.
- **L4. Point-in-polygon on the job's own geodetic position** (NAD 83(2011)
  for Auto-NAD83, including the HTDP pivot; NATRF2022 for Auto-NATRF). The
  ~1.2 m between the two is inside every boundary band below.
- **L5. A point in no county is refused by name**, never given the nearest
  zone. Outside Michigan, and in Canadian water, is refused.
- **L6.** Superseded by O2.
- **L7. Options are window-level, shared by both tabs.** This reverses
  #26/#43's "tabs share no state" for the Options only; every other control
  stays per tab. Changing any Option clears every displayed Single point
  result (the #26 stale-result rule) and reapplies the dropdown filter.
- **L8. Hiding a group that holds a current selection clears that
  selection** with a status line, exactly as #64's strand rule does. At
  least one geodetic group and one zone group must stay on; the dialog
  refuses an all-off state rather than leaving an empty dropdown.
- **L9. Persistence through `QSettings`** (per user,
  `HKCU\Software\DMARTIN\MCX - Martin Coordinate Exchange`), behind a seam so
  the suite never reads or writes the owner's real registry. A stored value
  MCX does not recognise falls back to the default **and says so** on the
  status line; it never crashes and never guesses.
- **L10. The observation date in Options.** "Today" is the **local** date the
  job runs (Michigan), not the later-of-local-and-UTC used to avoid false
  future refusals. The core keeps refusing a missing date: the GUI now always
  sends one, plus a required `ObservationDateSource` (TODAY or ENTERED) which
  the record prints ("2026-10-22, the date the job ran (Options default)" or
  "…, entered in Options"). A stray source on a job with no WGS 84/ITRF frame
  is refused, as a stray date is. **This reverses PLAN-wgs84 D10's no-default
  rule, on the owner's instruction**, for the "today" choice only; O3.
- **L11. Non-Auto jobs are byte-identical** to the WGS 84 build's output, held
  by the existing digest pins (v0.5.0, pre-W4, W4 disclosure). Every change
  in the outputs is gated on Auto.

### Owner decisions on the lead's questions (2026-10-22)

- **O1. County lines: US Census TIGER 2025** (what NGS built SPCS2022 from,
  and what NCAT uses for it). Measured against the State of Michigan
  Geographic Framework: on land 0.04 m median, 40 m worst; in the lakes
  116 m median, 444 m worst; the North/Central water line up to 11.8 km
  where it meets the international line. NCAT's SPCS 83 land line is 3-17 m
  off TIGER in places; the record names TIGER as the source.
- **O2. Near a zone line: assign only.** A point takes the zone of the
  county it falls in, with no warning and no refusal (MCL 54.234 permits
  either zone where named; the record names each point's zone and county as
  facts). L6 is superseded by this.
- **O3. The observation date.** Options holds a choice: **today** (default)
  or **enter a date**. The choice is remembered; the date is not. With
  "enter a date", the existing date box appears on the main tab, opening
  unanswered ("— choose —") every time MCX starts, and Convert waits for it
  (the WGS 84 build's widget and gating, kept). With "today", no date box is
  shown and the job uses the local date it runs.

## 2. What the recon found that shapes the build

- One function builds all four combos (`controls.zone_combo`); rows can be
  hidden (`setRowHidden`) without rebuilding, so every `findData` and index
  pin stays valid. `direction_for` fails closed on an unknown item type, so
  the Auto item is refused until each consumer handles it.
- The core already converts per point (`project_point(..., target_zone)`,
  `PointConversion.target_zone`), but every output reads
  `settings.target_zone`: `exports.output_stem`, the audit "Target zone"
  cell, `report._job_zones`, the TO block, the "Both zones … exact
  re-projection" sentence, the panel's Zone row, the unit and frame gates.
  With Auto, `target_zone` is None, and each of these crashes, prints a false
  sentence, or **skips a gate silently** (US survey feet on SPCS2022). Every
  one is a work item and a pin.
- `verify_round_trip` checks one clean file against all points; with one file
  per zone it runs per file on that file's points. `_verify_archive` refuses
  an empty member, so a zone with no points gets no file.
- Gate 6 refuses any bundled file byte-identical to one under `review/`, so
  the county file is derived (re-serialised, digest-pinned), never copied.
- NCAT returns the zone for a lat/long in both systems (production REST for
  SPCS 83; the beta web form for SPCS2022). It disagrees with itself near
  the Osceola/Mecosta line because its two systems use different county
  data, so anchors are frozen **outside** the boundary bands only.

## 3. Architecture

- **`michspc/spc/autozone.py`** (core, stdlib): `zone_for(lat, lon, frame)`
  → `ZoneAssignment(zone, county, distance_to_line_m | None)` or
  `AutoZoneRefusal` naming the point. Pure point-in-polygon (even-odd, with a
  documented on-edge rule) over polygons handed in; the county tables are
  core constants with their citations.
- **`michspc/fileio/counties.py`**: the only reader of
  `data/mi_counties_<source>.json` (derived, SHA-256 pinned, geometry
  checks on load as the NGS grids have).
- **`job.py`**: `JobSettings.target_zone` may be `AUTO` (a sentinel object,
  not None) with `auto_frame`; per point the zone is chosen after any HTDP
  pivot, then projected. Unit gate checks the unit against EVERY zone the
  Auto frame can choose; frame gate against the Auto frame.
- **Outputs**: one clean member per zone used, `<stem>_<zone>.csv`; the audit
  CSV's Target zone cell names the point's zone; the record's TO block lists
  each zone used with its point count, the county rule, the data source and
  digest; the audit CSV names each point's county (O2).
- **GUI**: `Options` menu → dialog (toggles, Auto frame, observation date);
  window-level `Options` object read by both tabs; Auto row at the top of
  the zone section; date widgets removed from both tabs.

## 4. Work packages (Opus builders to the lead's brief; lead reviews every diff)

- **Z0** derive the county file and both tables from the recon captures;
  freeze NCAT zone anchors (both systems, outside the bands, including
  Great Lakes, Isle Royale, out-of-state refusals).
- **Z1** `autozone.py` + `counties.py`, pinned to the anchors.
- **Z2** `job.run` Auto: per-point zone, refusals, both gates. **Interim
  Codex gate 1 over Z0–Z2.**
- **Z3** outputs: per-zone clean files, verifiers, audit, record (Auto only;
  every non-Auto digest unchanged).
- **Z4** Options dialog, persistence seam, toggles, Auto entry, the date
  box shown only under "enter a date", both tabs; offscreen previews for the owner.
- **Z5** packaging (spec datas, data pin, LAZY_IMPORTS, gate 6), self-test
  check for Auto, REFREEZE rows, release notes 0.8.0 updated. **Closing
  Codex gate over Z0–Z5**, fixes, narrowing re-confirmation.

Each package: hand-derived expected values, every pin falsified by seeding
(snapshot restore, never `git checkout --`), suite green in `pytest` and
`-O` before commit.

- One real WGS 84 / PPP dataset end to end.
- The clean-profile install proof (METHOD.md §6).

615:### #71 — 2026-10-22 — Options menu, Auto zone, the observation date in Options; the release machine's suite
"""The Options: window-level choices both tabs read (DESIGN.md #71, plan L7-L10).

Three questions, answered once for the window rather than per tab - the one
recorded reversal of #26/#43's "tabs share no state", on the owner's
instruction and for the Options only (plan L7):

1. **Which groups the four system lists show** - each zone system, and each
   geodetic frame's latitude/longitude entry. On by default except the
   time-dependent frames (the WGS 84 realizations and the ITRFs), decided by
   the frame record's own ``is_time_dependent``, never by a list of names.
2. **The Auto zone frame** - NAD83(2011) (SPCS 83 zones) or NATRF2022 (the
   SPCS2022 local zones), from ``job.AUTO_FRAMES``.
3. **Where the observation date comes from** - "today" (the default) or a
   date entered on the tab (plan O3).

``Options`` is one immutable value. ``visible_rows`` is the pure function
from it to which rows of a zone dropdown are shown; both tabs call it, so
they cannot filter differently. ``load_options`` / ``save_options`` are the
persistence, over the store seam in ``settings_store``.

**This module computes no domain value.** It decides what is offered, never
what a coordinate is; ``job.run`` stays the wall for every combination a
caller could still assemble by hand.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from michspc.gui.controls import (
    GEODETIC_CHOICES,
    UNCHOSEN,
    is_auto,
    is_geodetic,
)
from michspc.job import AUTO_FRAMES, ObservationDateSource
from michspc.spc.frames import ReferenceFrame
from michspc.spc.zones import ALL_ZONES, Zone


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ListGroup:
    """One group of entries the Options switch on or off as a whole.

    Exactly one of ``system`` (a zone system, ``Zone.system``) and ``frame``
    (a geodetic entry's frame) is set. ``label`` and ``key`` are derived from
    that record, so no name is typed twice.
    """

    system: str | None
    frame: ReferenceFrame | None

    @property
    def is_zone_group(self) -> bool:
        return self.system is not None

    @property
    def label(self) -> str:
        """What the Options dialog calls this group. Derived from the record."""
        if self.is_zone_group:
            return f"{self.system} zones"
        return f"{self.frame.code} latitude/longitude"

    @property
    def key(self) -> str:
        """The persistence key: the record's own string, under ``lists/``."""
        if self.is_zone_group:
            return f"lists/zones/{self.system}"
        return f"lists/geodetic/{self.frame.code}"

    @property
    def default_shown(self) -> bool:
        """On, except a time-dependent frame (plan section 1 item 1).

        Asked of the frame record (``ReferenceFrame.is_time_dependent``): the
        five WGS 84 / ITRF entries are off by default because a position in
        one of them means nothing until its observation date is stated, and
        the commonest slip is one row off the NAD83(2011) entry above them.
        """
        if self.is_zone_group:
            return True
        return not self.frame.is_time_dependent

    def holds(self, data) -> bool:
        """Whether a dropdown item belongs to this group."""
        if self.is_zone_group:
            if isinstance(data, Zone):
                return data.system == self.system
            if is_auto(data):
                return data.system.value == self.system
            return False
        return is_geodetic(data) and data.frame is self.frame


def _zone_systems() -> tuple[str, ...]:
    systems: list[str] = []
    for zone in ALL_ZONES:
        if zone.system not in systems:
            systems.append(zone.system)
    return tuple(systems)


LIST_GROUPS: tuple[ListGroup, ...] = (
    *(ListGroup(system=system, frame=None) for system in _zone_systems()),
    *(ListGroup(system=None, frame=choice.frame) for choice in GEODETIC_CHOICES),
)
"""Every group, in the dialog's order: the zone systems in registry order
(SPCS 83, then SPCS2022), then one geodetic group per offered frame in
``ALL_FRAMES`` declaration order. Derived from the registries, so a frame or
a zone system that arrives there arrives here."""

_GROUPS_BY_KEY = {group.key: group for group in LIST_GROUPS}


def group_of(data) -> ListGroup | None:
    """The group a dropdown item belongs to; None for the placeholder or a separator."""
    for group in LIST_GROUPS:
        if group.holds(data):
            return group
    return None


class OptionsError(ValueError):
    """An Options value that cannot exist: names the field and why."""


def options_problem(shown) -> str | None:
    """Why a set of shown groups cannot be accepted, or None (plan L8).

    At least one zone group and one geodetic group must stay on: with none,
    a dropdown would offer only its placeholder, and a list with nothing to
    choose looks answerable and is not.
    """
    groups = [_GROUPS_BY_KEY[key] for key in shown if key in _GROUPS_BY_KEY]
    if not any(group.is_zone_group for group in groups):
        return (
            "Switch on at least one group of State Plane zones: with none, the "
            "system lists would hold no zone to choose."
        )
    if not any(not group.is_zone_group for group in groups):
        return (
            "Switch on at least one latitude/longitude group: with none, the "
            "system lists would hold no geodetic position to choose."
        )
    return None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Options:
    """The window's Options. Immutable; replaced whole, never edited in place."""

    shown: frozenset[str]
    """The ``ListGroup.key`` of every group switched on."""

    auto_frame: ReferenceFrame
    """The frame Auto chooses zones in - one of ``job.AUTO_FRAMES``, by identity."""

    date_source: ObservationDateSource
    """Where a time-dependent job's observation date comes from (plan O3)."""

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        # if/raise, never assert: the suite runs under -O.
        if not isinstance(self.shown, frozenset):
            raise OptionsError(
                f"Options.shown must be a frozenset of group keys; got "
                f"{type(self.shown).__name__}."
            )
        unknown = sorted(key for key in self.shown if key not in _GROUPS_BY_KEY)
        if unknown:
            raise OptionsError(
                f"Options.shown names groups that do not exist: {unknown}. "
                f"The groups are: {[group.key for group in LIST_GROUPS]}."
            )
        problem = options_problem(self.shown)
        if problem is not None:
            raise OptionsError(problem)
        if not any(self.auto_frame is frame for frame, _system in AUTO_FRAMES):
            raise OptionsError(
                f"Options.auto_frame must be one of job.AUTO_FRAMES "
                f"({', '.join(frame.code for frame, _ in AUTO_FRAMES)}), the "
                f"registry's own record; got {self.auto_frame!r}."
            )
        if not isinstance(self.date_source, ObservationDateSource):
            raise OptionsError(
                f"Options.date_source must be an ObservationDateSource (TODAY "
                f"or ENTERED); got {self.date_source!r}."
            )

    def is_group_shown(self, group: ListGroup) -> bool:
        return group.key in self.shown

    def with_groups(self, *groups: ListGroup, shown: bool = True) -> "Options":
        """A copy with these groups switched on (or off). For callers and tests."""
        keys = {group.key for group in groups}
        new = self.shown | keys if shown else self.shown - keys
        return Options(
            shown=frozenset(new),
            auto_frame=self.auto_frame,
            date_source=self.date_source,
        )


DEFAULT_SHOWN = frozenset(group.key for group in LIST_GROUPS if group.default_shown)

DEFAULT_AUTO_FRAME = AUTO_FRAMES[0][0]
"""NAD83(2011) - the first ``job.AUTO_FRAMES`` record: today's zones."""

DEFAULT_DATE_SOURCE = ObservationDateSource.TODAY
"""Plan O3, the owner's decision: "today" unless he chooses to enter a date.
The reversal of PLAN-wgs84 D10's no-default rule, for this choice only
(DESIGN.md #71); an entered date is still never remembered."""

DEFAULT_OPTIONS = Options(
    shown=DEFAULT_SHOWN,
    auto_frame=DEFAULT_AUTO_FRAME,
    date_source=DEFAULT_DATE_SOURCE,
)


def is_shown(options: Options, data, *, is_source: bool) -> bool:
    """Whether a dropdown item is shown under these Options.

    * the placeholder - always;
    * an Auto entry - only on a TO combo (plan L1: a northing/easting does
      not say which zone it is in), only for the Options' Auto frame, and
      only while that system's zone group is shown ("shown only if that
      system's zone group is shown");
    * a zone or a geodetic entry - while its group is on.

    Anything else is refused: a dropdown item this rule cannot place is a
    wiring defect, and guessing "shown" would put it in front of the user.
    """
    if data == UNCHOSEN:
        return True
    group = group_of(data)
    if group is None:
        raise ValueError(
            f"options.is_shown cannot place the dropdown item {data!r} in any "
            f"Options group; every item zone_combo builds belongs to one."
        )
    if is_auto(data):
        if is_source or data.frame is not options.auto_frame:
            return False
    return options.is_group_shown(group)


def visible_rows(options: Options, items, *, is_source: bool) -> tuple[bool, ...]:
    """One shown/hidden flag per dropdown row, separators included.

    ``items`` is the combo's data in index order (``controls.combo_items``),
    a separator being None. A separator is shown exactly when it would sit
    BETWEEN two shown entries - placed before the next shown entry, provided
    an entry other than the placeholder is shown above it - so switching a
    group off never leaves two rules together, a rule at the top, or a rule
    at the bottom.
    """
    flags = [False] * len(items)
    pending: int | None = None
    seen_entry = False
    for index, data in enumerate(items):
        if data is None:
            if seen_entry:
                pending = index
            continue
        shown = is_shown(options, data, is_source=is_source)
        flags[index] = shown
        if shown and data != UNCHOSEN:
            if pending is not None:
                flags[pending] = True
                pending = None
            seen_entry = True
    return tuple(flags)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Persistence (plan L9). Three kinds of key, each read strictly.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

AUTO_FRAME_KEY = "auto_zone/frame"
DATE_SOURCE_KEY = "observation_date/source"
_TRUE = "true"
_FALSE = "false"


def _reset_message(name: str, raw, default_text: str) -> str:
    return (
        f"Options: the remembered value of \"{name}\" ({raw!r}) is not one MCX "
        f"recognises, so it was reset to its default ({default_text})."
    )


def load_options(store) -> tuple[Options, tuple[str, ...]]:
    """The remembered Options, and one message per value that had to be reset.

    A key that is absent is the default, silently - a first run is not an
    error. A key that is present and not exactly a value this program writes
    is reset to that key's default **and says so**, never crashes and never
    guesses (plan L9): ``"True"``, ``"1"`` or a frame code with a typo are all
    refused alike. A set of groups that would leave a list empty (L8) is reset
    to the default groups, also said. Every reset is written back, so the
    message is shown once rather than at every start.
    """
    messages: list[str] = []
    shown: set[str] = set()
    for group in LIST_GROUPS:
        raw = store.get(group.key)
        if raw is None:
            on = group.default_shown
        elif raw == _TRUE:
            on = True
        elif raw == _FALSE:
            on = False
        else:
            on = group.default_shown
            messages.append(
                _reset_message(
                    f"Show {group.label}", raw, "on" if on else "off"
                )
            )
            store.put(group.key, _TRUE if on else _FALSE)
        if on:
            shown.add(group.key)
    if options_problem(shown) is not None:
        messages.append(
            "Options: the remembered system lists would have left a list "
            "with nothing to choose, so every group was reset to its default."
        )
        shown = set(DEFAULT_SHOWN)
        for group in LIST_GROUPS:
            store.put(group.key, _TRUE if group.default_shown else _FALSE)

    raw = store.get(AUTO_FRAME_KEY)
    auto_frame = DEFAULT_AUTO_FRAME
    if raw is not None:
        matched = [frame for frame, _system in AUTO_FRAMES if frame.code == raw]
        if matched:
            auto_frame = matched[0]
        else:
            messages.append(
                _reset_message("Auto zone frame", raw, DEFAULT_AUTO_FRAME.code)
            )
            store.put(AUTO_FRAME_KEY, DEFAULT_AUTO_FRAME.code)

    raw = store.get(DATE_SOURCE_KEY)
    date_source = DEFAULT_DATE_SOURCE
    if raw is not None:
        matched = [source for source in ObservationDateSource if source.value == raw]
        if matched:
            date_source = matched[0]
        else:
            messages.append(
                _reset_message("Observation date", raw, DEFAULT_DATE_SOURCE.value)
            )
            store.put(DATE_SOURCE_KEY, DEFAULT_DATE_SOURCE.value)

    return (
        Options(
            shown=frozenset(shown), auto_frame=auto_frame, date_source=date_source
        ),
        tuple(messages),
    )


def save_options(store, options: Options) -> None:
    """Remember the three choices - and nothing else. Never a date (plan O3)."""
    for group in LIST_GROUPS:
        store.put(group.key, _TRUE if options.is_group_shown(group) else _FALSE)
    store.put(AUTO_FRAME_KEY, options.auto_frame.code)
    store.put(DATE_SOURCE_KEY, options.date_source.value)
"""Where the Options are remembered between runs, behind a seam (plan L9, O3).

**The one place this program persists anything**, and it persists exactly
three facts: which groups the system lists show, the Auto zone frame, and
WHERE the observation date comes from. Never a date itself (plan O3: "the
choice is remembered; the date is not"), never a zone, a unit, a file or a
folder - those are answered per job, in words on the screen, before Convert.

In production the store is ``QSettings`` in its native format, per user:
``HKCU\\Software\\DMARTIN\\MCX - Martin Coordinate Exchange`` on Windows
(organisation ``APP_PUBLISHER``, application the window title). The suite
must never read or write the owner's real registry - a test that passed or
failed on his saved options would be measuring his machine, not the program
(recon section 5) - so every window asks ``default_store()``, and the suite's
conftest installs ``MemoryStore`` as the factory before any test runs. A test
pins that the production factory is not the one installed, and that it was
never called (``PRODUCTION_STORES_BUILT``).

**No Qt import at module level**, deliberately: tests/conftest.py imports
this module for every test in the suite, most of which never touch Qt, and
``QSettings`` is imported only when a production store is actually built.

The stored values are strings, read back strictly by ``options.load_options``
(the "loaders validate as strictly as the UI" rule): anything it does not
recognise falls back to that key's default and says so on the status line.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from michspc import APP_FULL_NAME, APP_NAME, APP_PUBLISHER

ORGANIZATION = APP_PUBLISHER
APPLICATION = f"{APP_NAME} - {APP_FULL_NAME}"
"""The two names ``QSettings`` keys the native store by. ``APPLICATION`` is
the window title's own derivation (``window.WINDOW_TITLE``; a test pins the
two equal), which is also what ``app.build_application`` names the
application, so the key is the one the plan states (L9)."""


class MemoryStore:
    """A store that lives in a dict: the suite's, and the offscreen previews'."""

    def __init__(self, values: dict | None = None) -> None:
        self.values: dict[str, object] = dict(values or {})

    def get(self, key: str):
        return self.values.get(key)

    def put(self, key: str, value: str) -> None:
        self.values[key] = value


class QSettingsStore:
    """``QSettings`` behind the two-method store interface.

    ``ini_path`` None is the production store (native format, user scope);
    a path makes an INI-file store, which is how the suite tests this adapter
    without going near the registry.
    """

    def __init__(self, ini_path=None) -> None:
        from PySide6.QtCore import QSettings

        if ini_path is None:
            self._settings = QSettings(
                QSettings.Format.NativeFormat,
                QSettings.Scope.UserScope,
                ORGANIZATION,
                APPLICATION,
            )
        else:
            self._settings = QSettings(str(ini_path), QSettings.Format.IniFormat)

    def get(self, key: str):
        return self._settings.value(key)

    def put(self, key: str, value: str) -> None:
        self._settings.setValue(key, value)
        self._settings.sync()


PRODUCTION_STORES_BUILT = 0
"""How many times ``production_store`` has run in this process. The suite pins
it at zero: a nonzero count under pytest means a test reached the owner's
real registry."""


def production_store() -> QSettingsStore:
    """The owner's real, per-user store. Never built under the suite."""
    global PRODUCTION_STORES_BUILT
    PRODUCTION_STORES_BUILT += 1
    return QSettingsStore()


_factory = production_store


def store_factory():
    """The factory ``default_store`` calls - ``production_store`` outside tests."""
    return _factory


def set_store_factory(factory):
    """Install ``factory`` as what ``default_store`` calls; returns the previous one."""
    global _factory
    previous = _factory
    _factory = factory
    return previous


def default_store():
    """The store a window reads its Options from when none is handed in."""
    return _factory()
"""Shared test helpers.

A job's deliverable is a single ZIP archive (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #17), so
tests that want to inspect what was actually written read members out of it
rather than opening loose files.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import zipfile
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from michspc.gui import settings_store

# The Options store (plan L9, DESIGN.md #71): EVERY window this suite builds
# reads and writes an in-memory store, never the owner's real per-user
# registry key. Installed at import, before any test module is collected, so
# a window built at module level is covered too; each call returns a fresh
# empty store, so every window opens on the defaults. Pinned by
# tests/test_gui_options.py::test_the_suite_never_builds_the_real_store.
settings_store.set_store_factory(settings_store.MemoryStore)


def wgs84_build_store() -> settings_store.MemoryStore:
    """A store holding the Options the WGS 84 build's GUI tests were written under.

    Those tests (W5, DESIGN.md #70) pin the five WGS 84 / ITRF entries in the
    lists and the observation date box on the tab. Since the Options build
    (DESIGN.md #71) both are Options - the five groups off by default and
    "Today" as the date source - so their fixtures switch the five ON and the
    date to "Enter a date on the tab" through the Options seam itself (a
    remembered store, read by the window as the owner's would be), and every
    assertion they make stands unweakened. Imported lazily: the options module
    imports Qt, and most of the suite never does.
    """
    from michspc.gui import options
    from michspc.job import ObservationDateSource

    time_dependent = [
        group
        for group in options.LIST_GROUPS
        if not group.is_zone_group and group.frame.is_time_dependent
    ]
    chosen = options.Options(
        shown=options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS.with_groups(*time_dependent).shown,
        auto_frame=options.DEFAULT_AUTO_FRAME,
        date_source=ObservationDateSource.ENTERED,
    )
    store = settings_store.MemoryStore()
    options.save_options(store, chosen)
    return store


def archive_members(archive: Path) -> dict[str, str]:
    """Every member of a written export, decoded, keyed by name.

    Reads through zipfile rather than trusting a path convention, so a test
    asserting "the export contains X" is asserting about the file on disk and
    not about how the test thinks the file was named.
    """
    with zipfile.ZipFile(archive) as handle:
        return {
            name: handle.read(name).decode("utf-8") for name in handle.namelist()
        }


def member_text(archive: Path, suffix: str) -> str:
    """The one member whose name ends with ``suffix``.

    Fails loudly on zero or several matches rather than silently picking one,
    since a test that reads the wrong member would still pass for the wrong
    reason.
    """
    members = archive_members(archive)
    matches = [name for name in members if name.endswith(suffix)]
    if len(matches) != 1:
        raise AssertionError(
            f"expected exactly one member ending {suffix!r} in {archive.name}, "
            f"found {matches or 'none'} among {sorted(members)}"
        )
    return members[matches[0]]


def extract_member(archive: Path, suffix: str, into: Path) -> Path:
    """Write one member out to a real file and return its path.

    This is what the surveyor actually does with the export - unzip it, then
    hand the PNEZD file to CAD - so tests that check the export round-trips
    through this program's own reader go the same way rather than parsing the
    text in memory.
    """
    members = archive_members(archive)
    matches = [name for name in members if name.endswith(suffix)]
    if len(matches) != 1:
        raise AssertionError(
            f"expected exactly one member ending {suffix!r} in {archive.name}, "
            f"found {matches or 'none'} among {sorted(members)}"
        )
    into.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    destination = into / matches[0]
    destination.write_text(members[matches[0]], encoding="utf-8", newline="")
    return destination


@pytest.fixture
def read_member():
    """Fixture form of ``member_text``, for readability in test bodies."""
    return member_text


# The two record lines that carry an absolute path (michspc/fileio/report.py,
# "File" and "Folder"). Their 19-character label column is the report's own.
_PATH_LINE_LABELS = ("File               ", "Folder             ")


def record_root_normalised(text: str, root: Path) -> str:
    r"""``text`` with the temporary ``root`` replaced by ``<ROOT>``, and the
    remaining separators on the File and Folder lines written as ``/``.

    The record digests in the suite were frozen on Linux, where the path
    below the root is ``<ROOT>/job/in.csv``; on Windows the same record says
    ``<ROOT>\job\in.csv``. That difference is the platform's path spelling,
    not the program's output, so it is normalised - but ONLY on the two lines
    that carry a path, and only on lines that begin with the label followed
    by ``<ROOT>``: a backslash anywhere else in a record is still compared
    exactly. On Linux this is a no-op (a POSIX path has no backslash), so the
    frozen digests are compared there exactly as before.
    """
    text = text.replace(str(root), "<ROOT>")
    lines = text.split("\n")
    for index, line in enumerate(lines):
        if line.startswith(tuple(label + "<ROOT>" for label in _PATH_LINE_LABELS)):
            lines[index] = line.replace("\\", "/")
    return "\n".join(lines)
