_ZONE_CONSTANT_BLOCKS = {
    LambertTwoParallelDef: _lambert_two_parallel_constants,
    LambertOneParallelDef: _lambert_one_parallel_constants,
    TransverseMercatorDef: _transverse_mercator_constants,
    ObliqueMercatorCenterDef: _oblique_mercator_constants,
}
"""THE table that turns a definition record into the lines describing it.

Keyed on the record's type, exactly as ``michspc.spc.projection``'s engine
table is, and for the same reason: a fifth projection that can be COMPUTED but
not DESCRIBED must refuse rather than be described with another projection's
field names. Every field name below belongs to one projection only - a
transverse Mercator has no standard parallel, an oblique Mercator's false
coordinates are not at a grid origin - so a wrong block here is a record that
misdescribes a sealed job in plausible words.
"""


_SOURCE_PREFIX = "  Source: "

_UNWRAPPED_SOURCE_DEFINITIONS = (LambertTwoParallelDef,)
"""Definition types whose citation is printed whole, on one line.

FROZEN HISTORY, not a style choice. The SPCS 83 citation is 78 characters and
its line is 88, over the record's 78-column width, and it has been printed that
way in every sealed job since 0.1.0 - so it stays, byte for byte.

Every other kind wraps. A 2022 citation names two NGS captures with their URLs,
byte counts, dates and SHA-256 digests: about 700 characters, which on one line
is a horizontal scroll in the Notepad this document is written to be read in.
The line is per kind because the block already is; nothing shared changes.
"""


def _source_lines(zone) -> list[str]:
    """The zone's citation, whole or wrapped under itself.

    ``break_on_hyphens=False`` and ``break_long_words=False`` for the reason
    ``_labelled_paragraph`` carries them: a citation holds URLs and repository
    paths (``review/nsrs-n0/raw/zoneBounds.json``), and a URL or a path broken
    across a line in a sealed record is a wrong URL or a wrong path.
    """
    if type(zone.definition) in _UNWRAPPED_SOURCE_DEFINITIONS:
        return [f"{_SOURCE_PREFIX}{zone.citation}"]
    return textwrap.wrap(
        zone.citation,
        width=78,
        initial_indent=_SOURCE_PREFIX,
        subsequent_indent=" " * len(_SOURCE_PREFIX),
        break_long_words=False,
        break_on_hyphens=False,
    ) or [_SOURCE_PREFIX.rstrip()]


class ZoneBlockUnavailableError(ValueError):
    """No description is registered for this kind of zone definition.

    The ``ProjectionUnavailableError`` voice, in the document layer. This
    record is what a surveyor defends a sealed survey with; a zone it cannot
    describe is a zone it must not write about.
    """


def _zone_block(zone, label: str) -> list[str]:
    """A zone's full defining and derived constants, with citations.

    Per projection kind, dispatched on the definition record's type. The two
    heading lines and the citation are common to every kind; what a zone's
    constants ARE is not, and this block used to print the two-parallel Lambert
    field names over whatever it was handed - which raised ``AttributeError``
    on a one-parallel zone AFTER the coordinates had been computed, and would
    have printed a plausible, wrong block for any definition that happened to
    carry the same field names (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #21).
    """
    render = _ZONE_CONSTANT_BLOCKS.get(type(zone.definition))
    if render is None:
        described = ", ".join(
            record.__name__ for record in _ZONE_CONSTANT_BLOCKS
        )
        raise ZoneBlockUnavailableError(
            f"This job record cannot describe {zone.name} (zone {zone.code}, "
            f"{zone.system}): no block is registered for a zone definition of "
            f"type {type(zone.definition).__name__}. Every projection is "
            f"defined by different constants - a transverse Mercator has no "
            f"standard parallel, and an oblique Mercator's false coordinates "
            f"are at its centre rather than at a grid origin - so describing "
            f"this zone with another projection's field names would put a "
            f"plausible falsehood into a sealed record. The definitions this "
            f"record can describe are: {described}."
        )
    lines = [
        f"{label}: {zone.name}, zone {zone.code} ({zone.system})",
        _zone_heading("Reference frame", zone.frame.code),
    ]
    lines.extend(_projection_lines(zone.projection_kind))
    lines.extend(render(zone))
    lines.extend(_source_lines(zone))
    return lines


_LABEL_WIDTH = 19
"""The record's label column: "File               " is 19 characters, and every
labelled line in this document starts the same way."""


def _labelled_paragraph(label: str, text: str) -> list[str]:
    """A labelled line whose text wraps under itself, record-style.

    The registry's ``direction_statement``, ``uncertainty_citation`` and
    ``caveat`` are single authoritative sentences longer than a line, and they
    must be QUOTED - re-drafting them here would create a second account of
    what was done to a height, which is the drift ``direction_statement``
    exists to make impossible. So the sentence is wrapped, never reworded:
    the words and their order are the record's exactly.
    """
    # break_long_words=False: a URL in a quoted citation must never be
    # snapped mid-token by the wrapper - a broken URL in a sealed record is
    # a wrong URL (WP-V7 review gate, LOW 3).
    body = (
        textwrap.wrap(
            text,
            width=78 - _LABEL_WIDTH,
            break_long_words=False,
            break_on_hyphens=False,
        )
        or [""]
    )
    lines = [f"{label:<{_LABEL_WIDTH}}{body[0]}"]
    lines.extend(f"{'':<{_LABEL_WIDTH}}{line}" for line in body[1:])
    return lines


def _vertical_method_block(
    settings, transformation, result, *, zoneless: bool
) -> list[str]:
    """The METHOD section's account of the vertical transformation.

    ``zoneless`` is ``build_report``'s one flag for a job with no State Plane
    zone at either end, passed in rather than re-derived, so the Factor height
    paragraph cannot name a combined factor on a job that has none by a rule
    of its own (interim gate 2 of the WGS 84 build, finding H2).

    Everything quotable is quoted from the registry record (``spc/vertical``),
    which derives its words from the same ``sign`` the computation multiplies
    by - so this document and the arithmetic cannot disagree about direction.
    The grid filenames and digests are the ``fileio/vertcon`` constants the
    loader itself authenticates against, for the same reason.
    """
    lines: list[str] = []
    add = lines.append

    add("VERTICAL DATUM TRANSFORMATION")
    add("")
    lines.extend(_labelled_paragraph("Direction", transformation.direction_statement))
    if not transformation.is_identity:
        add(
            f"Model              {transformation.model} "
            f"release {transformation.release}"
        )
        # Both grids, each with its own digest: the shift and its uncertainty
        # come from two different files, and a record that named one would
        # certify half of what was read (plan section 5.2).
        add(f"Grids              {vertcon.VERTCON3_TRN_FILENAME}")
        add(f"                   SHA-256 {vertcon.VERTCON3_TRN_SHA256}")
        add(f"                   {vertcon.VERTCON3_ERR_FILENAME}")
        add(f"                   SHA-256 {vertcon.VERTCON3_ERR_SHA256}")
    lines.extend(
        _labelled_paragraph("Uncertainty", transformation.uncertainty_citation)
    )
    lines.extend(_labelled_paragraph("Caveat", transformation.caveat))
    if not transformation.is_identity:
        # The half-cell discontinuity, DESIGN.md #38: a property of NOAA's own
        # algorithm, carried in deliberately because NOAA is the authority,
        # and disclosed here because a job whose points straddle such a line
        # shows the step with nothing else to explain it.
        add("")
        lines.extend(
            _labelled_paragraph(
                "Interpolation",
                "The grid is interpolated the way NOAA's own published "
                "VERTCON software interpolates it: a 3x3 stencil centred on "
                "the nearest grid node. That scheme steps at half-cell lines "
                "- odd multiples of 0.025 degrees, exactly the round "
                "coordinate values surveyors type - and the modeled shift "
                "jumps there, by up to about 76 mm in Michigan. NGS NCAT "
                "reproduces the same steps, because it runs the same "
                "algorithm. Two points a fraction of a millimetre apart "
                "across such a line can therefore carry visibly different "
                "shifts, and that is the model's own behaviour, not an "
                "error in either point.",
            )
        )
    if (
        # The one shared statement of the era rule - job.factors_use_source_era
        # is what the computation itself branches on, so this sentence and the
        # arithmetic cannot drift apart (WP-V7 review gate, LOW 2).
        factors_use_source_era(settings, transformation)
        # ...and only when at least one point actually HAS factors: "were
        # computed from" is a claim about work done, and a job whose every
        # point was blank-Z or coverage-refused did none of it - the record
        # asserted it anyway (WP-V7 review gate, MEDIUM 3).
        and any(p.factors.elevation_factor is not None for p in result.points)
    ):
        # The #41 rule - now the per-side pairing's consequence - said out
        # loud: this is the one configuration where the factors do NOT use
        # the height the Z column carries, and a record that did not say
        # which height the factors came from would leave the audit CSV's
        # "how was this derived" question half answered. The model named is
        # the one the factors actually read (factors_geoid_model) - for
        # every #41-era call shape that is settings.geoid_model itself, and
        # for the per-side shape it is the input-side model, which is the
        # only model such a job has.
        factor_model = factors_geoid_model(settings, transformation)
        # A job with no zone has no grid scale factor and so no combined
        # factor - its SCALE FACTOR SUMMARY says exactly that - so the
        # paragraph names only the factor that exists, in the words W4b gave
        # the ELLIPSOID HEIGHT block and ELEVATIONS. Until interim gate 2
        # (H2) it said "The elevation and combined factors were computed"
        # here on every job, zone-less ones included, since #41.
        factors = (
            "The elevation factor was"
            if zoneless
            else "The elevation and combined factors were"
        )
        add("")
        lines.extend(
            _labelled_paragraph(
                "Factor height",
                f"{factors} computed from the "
                f"SOURCE-datum ({transformation.source.code}) height, not "
                f"from the shifted height the Z column carries. The "
                f"{factor_model.name} separations are defined "
                f"against {factor_model.vertical_datum.code} "
                f"heights, and combining a separation with a height from a "
                f"different era would mix two eras inside one number "
                f"(DESIGN.md #32, #41). The Z column carries the shifted, "
                f"{transformation.target.code} height wherever one was "
                f"written.",
            )
        )
    add("")
    return lines


def _ellipsoid_height_method_block(settings, model_name, path=None) -> list[str]:
    """The METHOD section's account of an ellipsoid-height input.

    ``path`` is the job's frame transformation, or None (``job.
    applied_frame_transformation``). Across one, three sentences of the
    same-frame account stop being true, and each is restated from the path's
    own frames (work package W4 of the WGS 84 build, plan decision D6): the
    supplied heights are above THEIR frame's ellipsoid, which for a WGS 84
    realization is not GRS 80; the h in H = h - N is the NAD 83 height, the
    supplied one carried there by the transformation when it was supplied in
    another frame, because the geoid models publish N against NAD 83; and a
    horizontal job's Z column carries the height transformed with the
    position, not the height exactly as supplied. On a same-frame job every
    line is byte-identical to what it always printed.

    Facts only, in ``_geoid_swap_method_block``'s shape and under the same
    standing instruction (the owner's, 2026-08-09, #33/#34/#45 extended to the
    written record): the model with its tile filename and digest resolved from
    the registry, the arithmetic, and the datum the derived heights are in.

    The mode paragraph is the load-bearing one, because the two modes do
    different things with the same conversion and the difference is invisible
    in the numbers alone: horizontal mode writes the Z column back exactly as
    supplied and uses the derived height only for the factors, while the
    vertical modes write the derived height. A reader holding only the clean
    export needs the record to say which of those produced it.
    """
    record = geoid_model_by_name(model_name)
    lines: list[str] = []
    add = lines.append

    add("ELLIPSOID HEIGHT CONVERSION")
    add("")
    if path is None:
        add(
            f"Input heights      stated as ELLIPSOID heights (above the GRS 80 "
            f"ellipsoid),"
        )
        add("                   as a GNSS receiver produces them.")
    else:
        lines.extend(
            _labelled_paragraph(
                "Input heights",
                f"stated as ELLIPSOID heights in {path.source.code} (above the "
                f"{path.source.ellipsoid_name} ellipsoid), as a GNSS receiver "
                f"produces them.",
            )
        )
    add(
        f"Geoid model        {record.name}, NGS grid tile "
        f"{record.tile_filename}"
    )
    add(f"                   SHA-256 {record.sha256}")
    if path is None:
        which_h = "h is the height the Z column supplied"
    else:
        # The NAD 83 h, always (plan D6, PointConversion.pivot_ellipsoid_height):
        # the supplied height itself when it was supplied in NAD 83, else the
        # supplied height carried there with the position.
        pivot = _pivot_frame(path).code
        which_h = (
            f"h is the {pivot} ellipsoid height (the height the Z column "
            f"supplied in {path.source.code}, transformed with the position "
            f"to {pivot}: {record.name} publishes N against {pivot})"
            if path.source.is_time_dependent
            else f"h is the {pivot} ellipsoid height the Z column supplied"
        )
    lines.extend(
        _labelled_paragraph(
            "Arithmetic",
            f"The orthometric height is derived at each point's own "
            f"horizontal position: H = h - N, where {which_h} and N is the "
            f"{record.name} geoid height there, "
            f"in metres. N is negative throughout Michigan, so H is the "

def direction_for(source_data, target_data) -> Direction | None:
    """The job this pair of dropdowns describes, or None if it is not one.

    Returns None while either side is unanswered, and for the geodetic pairs
    that are not a conversion: geodetic to geodetic with no registered
    transformation between the two frames, which has no zone at either end and
    nothing to project through.

    A zone to ITSELF is deliberately not in that list. It returns
    ``ZONE_TO_ZONE`` and runs, because it is a real job: the units are
    selected independently of the zones, so Michigan South in feet to
    Michigan South in metres is a conversion the surveyor asked for, and
    even the identity case produces the per-point scale, convergence and
    combined factors that the audit CSV and the job record exist to report.

    **Geodetic to geodetic is ``GEODETIC_TO_GEODETIC`` exactly when the frame
    registry holds a NON-identity transformation between the two frames**
    (docs/PLAN-wgs84.md decision D7, work package W5) - the ten NGS HTDP paths
    between NAD83(2011) and the five WGS 84 and ITRF frames, which is HTDP's
    own core use and the conversion most people asking for "WGS 84" need. Asked
    of the registry (``_has_a_transformation``), never of a list here.

    SUPERSEDES H6's "geodetic to geodetic returns None whatever the two frames
    are", which was true while no transformation existed. It still returns
    None for every other geodetic pair, and each for the reason it always had:

    * the same frame at both ends - an identity converts nothing;
    * NAD83(2011) against NATRF2022 - reads like a datum transformation and is
      not one this program has; the frame bridge is unpublished (DESIGN.md
      #62);
    * a WGS 84 or ITRF frame against NATRF2022 (plan decision D3) - HTDP does
      not carry NATRF2022;
    * one global frame against another (plan decision D4) - no zone lives on
      either, and refusing that neighbouring problem is a recorded decision.

    None there means the pair describes no job and Convert is not offered; the
    graying (``selection_is_compatible``) already keeps the user from choosing
    it, from the same registry.

    **A cross-frame pair that IS a direction - a NAD83(2011) geodetic entry
    against an SPCS2022 zone - is answered here rather than refused.** The rule
    this function owns is what SHAPE of job the two dropdowns describe; whether
    that job's frames have a published path between them is the frame
    registry's question, and ``job.run`` asks it before a file is read. Two
    layers answering it would eventually disagree, and the one that would be
    wrong is this one, which has no registry.

    Anything else in a dropdown is refused by name. Since H6 the list contains
    one item that is not a selection at all - the separator between the two
    eras - and Qt gives it no selectable flags, so no user action reaches this
    with it. A programmatic ``setCurrentIndex`` onto it, or onto -1, would
    otherwise fall through to ``ZONE_TO_ZONE`` and hand ``job.run`` a job with
    no source zone.
    """
    if source_data == UNCHOSEN or target_data == UNCHOSEN:
        return None
    if is_auto(source_data):
        # Plan L1: a northing and easting do not say which zone they are in,
        # so Auto is never an input. Hidden on the FROM combos; reachable only
        # programmatically, and then it describes no job.
        return None
    if is_auto(target_data):
        # Auto chooses a zone from a latitude and longitude, so only a
        # geodetic FROM makes it a job (the entry is grayed otherwise).
        return Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE if is_geodetic(source_data) else None
    if is_geodetic(source_data) and is_geodetic(target_data):
        if _has_a_transformation(source_data.frame, target_data.frame):
            return Direction.GEODETIC_TO_GEODETIC
        return None
    if is_geodetic(source_data):
        return Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE
    if is_geodetic(target_data):
        return Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC
    if isinstance(source_data, Zone) and isinstance(target_data, Zone):
        return Direction.ZONE_TO_ZONE
    raise ValueError(
        f"A zone dropdown holds {source_data!r} / {target_data!r}, and at "
        f"least one of those is neither the unanswered placeholder, nor a "
        f"geodetic entry, nor an Auto entry, nor a zone. Every item zone_combo "
        f"offers is one of those four; the separators are not selectable and "
        f"carries no data. Refusing rather than reading it as a zone-to-zone "
        f"job, which would reach job.run with no zone."
    )


FRAME_RESET_STATUS = (
    "Cleared: no published transformation between those reference frames."
)
"""Shown when a selection is cleared because the other end moved.

One sentence, the owner's instruction after reading ``UNITS_SNAPPED_STATUS`` on
screen and finding three too many. It says the two things that cannot be
guessed from a suddenly-empty dropdown: that the program cleared it, and why.

It lives here rather than on either tab because both tabs say it, and one
wording in both surfaces is the standing rule (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #17).
"""

AUTO_RESET_STATUS = "Cleared: Auto zone needs a latitude/longitude input."
"""Shown when an Auto TO selection is cleared because the FROM end moved to
something that is not a latitude/longitude (plan L1). One sentence, in
``FRAME_RESET_STATUS``'s form: that the program cleared it, and why."""

OPTIONS_RESET_STATUS = "Cleared: that entry is switched off in Options."
"""Shown when an Options change hides the entry a dropdown held (plan L8) -
including an Auto entry whose frame the Options no longer choose. The
``FRAME_RESET_STATUS`` form again: cleared by the program, and why."""


def frame_of(data) -> ReferenceFrame | None:
    """The reference frame a zone dropdown's item is expressed in.

    A zone carries its own; a geodetic entry carries the frame it names. The
    placeholder and the separators carry none, and ``None`` means exactly that -
    "this item states no frame" - which is what makes the compatibility rule
    below able to leave them alone rather than needing to know what they are.
    """
    if isinstance(data, Zone):
        return data.frame
    if is_geodetic(data):
        return data.frame
    if is_auto(data):
        # Every zone an Auto entry can choose is defined on its frame
        # (job._check_auto_frames refuses to import otherwise), so the frame
        # IS what that TO end is expressed in.
        return data.frame
    return None


def frames_have_a_path(source: ReferenceFrame, target: ReferenceFrame) -> bool:
    """Whether this program can carry a position from one frame to the other.

    **Derived from the frame registry, by asking it.** Not an era test, and
    that is the whole point of writing it this way: ``require_frame_path`` is
    the same function ``job.run`` refuses on, so the interface and the gate
    cannot disagree about which pairs are convertible - and the day NGS
    publishes the NAD83(2011) <-> NATRF2022 transformation and it is registered
    in ``FRAME_TRANSFORMATIONS``, this answers True with no change to any line
    of interface code and the graying dissolves by itself. A hard-coded
    "1983 zones do not mix with 2022 zones" would still be graying them out
    that day, and somebody would have to remember it was there.

    Directional, because the registry is keyed by (source, target) and a future
    transformation need not be registered both ways.

    **And through the same point ``job.run`` applies or refuses a path**
    (``convert.require_an_applicable_path``, docs/PLAN-wgs84.md work package
    W3): the registry returns NGS HTDP records for NAD83(2011) against the
    WGS 84 and ITRF frames, and since W3 the conversion pipeline applies them,
    so this answers True for those pairs exactly as ``job.run`` accepts them
    (a WGS 84 position into an SPCS 83 zone, an SPCS 83 zone out to WGS 84) -
    and False for a registered path whose method the pipeline could not run.
    Under W2 it answered False for all ten, because the guard then refused
    them. Since work package W5 the five frames are offered in the dropdowns
    (``frames_offered``), so this answer is now what grays - or leaves
    choosable - every pairing of a WGS 84 or ITRF entry.
    """
    try:
        require_an_applicable_path(require_frame_path(source, target))
    except FrameMismatchError:
        return False
    return True


def selection_is_compatible(candidate, other, *, candidate_is_source: bool) -> bool:
    """Whether ``candidate`` can be chosen while the other end holds ``other``.

    True whenever the question does not arise:

    * the other end is unanswered - nothing is grayed against a placeholder,
      because the user has not said anything for the candidate to disagree
      with;
    * either item states no frame at all (the placeholder itself, a separator).

    The first of those is covered by the second - ``frame_of(UNCHOSEN)`` is
    already ``None`` - and is written out anyway, measured rather than assumed:
    a falsification that deleted it changed no behaviour at all. It stays
    because the rule it states is the one a reader needs first, and because the
    day ``UNCHOSEN`` stops being frameless by accident is the day the silent
    version would start graying against a placeholder.

    Otherwise it is the registry's answer for the pair, in the direction the
    job would run: ``candidate_is_source`` says which end the candidate is, so
    a one-way transformation would gray the right list.

    **Geodetic-to-geodetic is judged by the same rule**, deliberately. It is
    not a conversion at all (``direction_for`` returns None), so nothing would
    break by exempting it - but exempting it would mean two rules where one
    does, and it would offer a NATRF2022 position against a NAD83(2011) one as
    though the pair meant something. One mechanism, no special case.
    """
    return (
        incompatibility(candidate, other, candidate_is_source=candidate_is_source)
        is None
    )


def incompatibility(candidate, other, *, candidate_is_source: bool) -> str | None:
    """Why ``candidate`` cannot be paired with ``other``, as its status line, or None.

    ``selection_is_compatible``'s rule with its reason, so a cleared selection
    says the true one of two (DESIGN.md #71):

    * ``AUTO_RESET_STATUS`` - an Auto entry at the TO end with a FROM end that
      is not a latitude/longitude, or an Auto entry at the FROM end at all
      (plan L1: Auto chooses a zone from a latitude/longitude, and a
      northing/easting does not say which zone it is in);
    * ``FRAME_RESET_STATUS`` - the frame registry has no path between the two
      frames (#64). An Auto entry's frame is its Auto frame, so NAD83(2011)
      input against Auto on NATRF2022 grays and clears exactly as it does
      against an SPCS2022 zone (plan L2) - and dissolves by itself the same
      way when a bridge is registered.

    Nothing is incompatible with the unanswered placeholder or a separator,
    and the placeholder is incompatible with nothing.
    """
    if other == UNCHOSEN or candidate == UNCHOSEN:
        return None
    if candidate is None or other is None:
        return None
    source, target = (candidate, other) if candidate_is_source else (other, candidate)
    if is_auto(source):
        return AUTO_RESET_STATUS
    if is_auto(target) and not is_geodetic(source):
        return AUTO_RESET_STATUS
    candidate_frame = frame_of(candidate)
    other_frame = frame_of(other)
    if candidate_frame is None or other_frame is None:
        return None
    if candidate_is_source:
        allowed = frames_have_a_path(candidate_frame, other_frame)
    else:
        allowed = frames_have_a_path(other_frame, candidate_frame)
    return None if allowed else FRAME_RESET_STATUS


def refresh_zone_graying(combo, other, *, is_source: bool, visible=None) -> None:
    """Gray every item in ``combo`` that cannot be paired with ``other``.

    **Grayed - disabled in place - never removed**, the owner's word and the
    #50 vocabulary: a question that applies and cannot be answered stays
    visible and gray, where a question that does not apply at all is hidden.
    A surveyor who cannot find Michigan Grand Rapids in the list learns
    nothing; one who sees it grayed learns that his other selection is why.

    Both tabs call this through their own one owner method, so all four combos
    are grayed by one rule. The separators are never touched - they are already
    flagless, and re-enabling one would make it selectable.

    **What Qt actually does with a disabled item, measured rather than
    assumed:** the arrow keys skip it and the mouse cannot land on it, but
    ``setCurrentIndex`` selects it perfectly happily from code. So this is a
    user-interface courtesy and NOT a safety barrier, and nothing downstream
    may be simplified on the strength of it: ``job.run``'s frame gate stays the
    wall, and it is pinned as the wall.

    **``visible`` - the Options' row visibility (``options.visible_rows``),
    one flag per index; None means every row is shown.** A hidden row is
    DISABLED here as well as hidden by ``apply_row_visibility``, and that is
    measured Qt behaviour, not belt and braces: ``QListView.setRowHidden``
    hides a row from the popup only, while the closed combo's arrow keys and
    mouse wheel step through every ENABLED item, hidden or not - so a
    switched-off WGS 84 entry would be one Down-arrow away without this
    (pinned in tests/test_gui_options.py). Folded into this one owner of the
    flags (the #57 rule) rather than set by a second method.
    """
    model = combo.model()
    for index in range(combo.count()):
        data = combo.itemData(index)
        if data is None:
            # A separator. It carries no data, no flags and no meaning.
            continue
        item = model.item(index)
        allowed = (visible is None or visible[index]) and selection_is_compatible(
            data, other, candidate_is_source=is_source
        )
        flags = item.flags()
        if allowed:
            item.setFlags(flags | Qt.ItemFlag.ItemIsEnabled)
        else:
            item.setFlags(flags & ~Qt.ItemFlag.ItemIsEnabled)


def combo_items(combo) -> tuple:
    """Every item's data in a combo, in index order - separators as None."""
    return tuple(combo.itemData(index) for index in range(combo.count()))


def apply_row_visibility(combo, visible) -> None:
    """Hide the popup rows the Options switch off; one flag per index.

    ``setRowHidden`` on the combo's own list view, never a rebuild: the items
    and their indexes stay exactly where ``zone_combo`` put them, so every
    ``findData`` and every index a method holds stays valid, and a hidden
    selection cannot silently turn into a different item. The flags that keep
    a hidden row out of keyboard and wheel stepping are
    ``refresh_zone_graying``'s (its ``visible``). Each tab calls this from one
    method only (the #57 rule, AST-pinned).
    """
    visible = tuple(visible)
    if len(visible) != combo.count():
        raise ValueError(
            f"Row visibility for {len(visible)} rows was applied to a dropdown "
            f"holding {combo.count()}; the flags must come from this combo's "
            f"own items (combo_items)."
        )
    view = combo.view()
    for index, shown in enumerate(visible):
        view.setRowHidden(index, not shown)


def geodetic_frame_for(*sides) -> ReferenceFrame | None:
    """The frame this job's geodetic END is in, or None if it has none.

    What ``JobSettings.geodetic_frame`` is threaded from, on both tabs. The
    sides are passed in the order the tab reads them - source then target,
    or source alone in vertical-only mode - and the first geodetic one
    answers.

    First-wins is the rule, and since work package W5 it is load-bearing
    rather than incidental: the only pair with two geodetic sides is
    ``GEODETIC_TO_GEODETIC``, whose ``geodetic_frame`` is the INPUT frame - the
        f"{SPCS2022_DEFINITIONS_CAPTURED}"
    )
    add(f"{'':<{_LABEL_WIDTH}}SHA-256 {SPCS2022_DEFINITIONS_SHA256}")
    add(
        f"{'':<{_LABEL_WIDTH}}{SPCS2022_BOUNDS_FILENAME}, captured "
        f"{SPCS2022_BOUNDS_CAPTURED}"
    )
    add(f"{'':<{_LABEL_WIDTH}}SHA-256 {SPCS2022_BOUNDS_SHA256}")
    add("")

    lines.extend(
        _labelled_paragraph(
            "Equations",
            "The rigorous mapping equations of NOAA Manual NOS NGS 5, one set "
            "per projection this job uses:",
        )
    )
    lines.extend(_equation_lines(zones))
    add("")
    lines.extend(
        _labelled_paragraph(
            "Ellipsoid",
            "GRS 80 - the ellipsoid of NAD 83 and of NATRF2022 alike (NOAA "
            "Technical Report NOS NGS 62). Only the semimajor axis and the "
            "flattening are stored; every other constant is derived from "
            "those two.",
        )
    )
    add("")
    add("HOW THIS SOFTWARE IS VERIFIED")
    add("")
    lines.extend(
        textwrap.wrap(
            f"The zone constants used above are not taken on trust. This "
            f"software stores only the DEFINING constants NGS publishes - the "
            f"origin, the central meridian, the origin scale factor, the false "
            f"coordinates - and derives the rest. Its test suite reads NGS's "
            f"own captured files back and compares every stored field against "
            f"them, zone by zone: the defining constants against "
            f"{SPCS2022_DEFINITIONS_FILENAME} (SHA-256 "
            f"{SPCS2022_DEFINITIONS_SHA256}), and the published zone bounds "
            f"this program's warnings are measured against, against "
            f"{SPCS2022_BOUNDS_FILENAME} (SHA-256 {SPCS2022_BOUNDS_SHA256}).",
            width=78,
            break_long_words=False,
        )
    )
    add("")
    lines.extend(
        textwrap.wrap(
            f"The conversion itself is checked against the National Geodetic "
            f"Survey's own Coordinate Conversion and Transformation Tool "
            f"(NCAT). Sixty-three positions spanning all nineteen Michigan "
            f"SPCS2022 zones were converted by beta NCAT (captured "
            f"{SPCS2022_DEFINITIONS_CAPTURED}), and those results are held in "
            f"the test suite as fixed reference values. This software "
            f"reproduces them within the precision NCAT prints. The inverse is "
            f"checked at the same positions: each captured coordinate is "
            f"converted back, and the convergence angle and grid scale factor "
            f"reported there are held against the same printed NCAT values.",
            width=78,
            break_long_words=False,
        )
    )
    add("")
    lines.extend(
        textwrap.wrap(
            "Those anchors, and the zone parameters above, come from NGS's "
            "beta products, published ahead of the official SPCS2022 release "
            "for implementation. They are to be re-frozen against NGS's "
            "official release when it is published, and the difference "
            "measured (docs/REFREEZE-NSRS.md).",
            width=78,
            break_long_words=False,
        )
    )
    add("")
    add("Both checks compare this software against NGS. Neither compares it")
    add("against itself.")
    add("")
    return lines


def _method_and_verification(settings, result=None) -> list[str]:
    """The METHOD section, in the era of the zones this job actually uses.

    **A job cannot mix eras.** The 1983 zones are on NAD83(2011) and the 2022
    zones are on NATRF2022; ``frames.require_frame_path`` refuses that pair
    before any coordinate is converted, and ``job.run`` refuses it before the
    file is read (docs/DESIGN.md #62). This function does not rely on that
    assumption silently - it checks it, because a settings record can be built
    by hand and a document that quietly described half a job in the wrong era's
    words would be worse than a refusal.

    A job with NO zone at all is not described here: since plan W4b
    (DESIGN.md #70) ``build_report`` gives it ``_method_without_a_zone``,
    because the 1983 arm it used to take describes a projection such a job
    never runs. Called with no zone, this still returns the 1983 arm, as it
    always did - it is only no longer asked.
    """
    zones = _job_zones(settings, result)
    if is_auto_zone_job(settings):
        # The era is the Auto system's, and every zone used must be of it -
        # checked, not assumed, so an Auto SPCS2022 job can never fall through
        # to the 1983 arm below (which is what a job with no modern zone gets).
        system = auto_system(settings)
        stray = [zone for zone in zones if zone.system != system.value]
        if stray or not zones:
            raise ValueError(
                f"This Auto zone job chose among the {system.value} zones, but "
                f"its points name "
                f"{', '.join(f'{z.name} ({z.code}, {z.system})' for z in zones) or 'no zone'}."
            )
        if system is AutoZoneSystem.SPCS2022:
            return _method_spcs2022(list(zones))
        return _method_spcs83()
    modern = [zone for zone in zones if zone.code in _SPCS2022_ZONE_CODES]
    legacy = [zone for zone in zones if zone.code not in _SPCS2022_ZONE_CODES]
    if modern and legacy:
        raise ValueError(
            f"This job names zones from two eras - "
            f"{', '.join(f'{z.name} ({z.code}, {z.system})' for z in legacy)} "
            f"and "
            f"{', '.join(f'{z.name} ({z.code}, {z.system})' for z in modern)} "
            f"- and they are on different reference frames, so no such "
            f"conversion exists to describe. NGS has not published the "
            f"NAD83(2011) to NATRF2022 transformation "
            f"(docs/DEFERRED-NATRF2022-BRIDGE.md); a conversion between the "
            f"eras is refused before any coordinate is computed."
        )
    if modern:
        return _method_spcs2022(modern)
    return _method_spcs83()


def build_report(result: JobResult) -> str:
    """Render the job record."""
    # The W3 -> W4 write guard (require_a_disclosable_job) was called here
    # until this record could state a frame transformation; it can now, in
    # the FRAME TRANSFORMATION block below, and the guard is gone. The Auto
    # zone build's guard (require_a_disclosed_zone, Z2 -> Z3) stood here too,
    # until the Auto TO block below could state each zone used.
    settings = result.settings
    if settings.input_path is None or settings.output_directory is None:
        # The record's INPUT block opens with "File" and its OUTPUT block opens
        # with "Folder". Neither line can be written from None, and neither may
        # be filled in with a plausible substitute: this record is what a sealed
        # survey is defended with, so a file name it never read or a folder it
        # never wrote to would be a falsehood in the one document that exists to
        # say what happened (docs/DESIGN.md section 1, amendment #26).
        # Name which one is actually missing. Saying "neither" when only one is
        # absent is a false diagnostic about the half-pathless state, which is
        # a state this program explicitly supports (closing review gate).
        missing = []
        if settings.input_path is None:
            missing.append("no input file")
        if settings.output_directory is None:
            missing.append("no output folder")
        raise ValueError(
            f"A job record names the input file it read and the folder it "
            f"wrote to, and this job states it had {' and '.join(missing)} "
            f"(input_path={settings.input_path!r}, "
            f"output_directory={settings.output_directory!r}). A job with no "
            f"file, such as a single typed point, is displayed rather than "
            f"recorded."
        )
    generated = datetime.now(timezone.utc).astimezone()

    # The vertical disclosure switch (WP-V7). Everything it gates below is
    # ADDITIVE and appears only on a job that converts elevations
    # (HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL, or vertical-only): a horizontal job's record
    # is byte-identical to what this program has always written, which is the
    # WP-V6 gate's hard constraint on this package. The transformation is
    # resolved through the same registry lookup job.run performed, so the
    # record and the computation quote one record.
    vertical = settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations
    transformation = (
        require_vertical_pair(
            settings.source_vertical_datum, settings.target_vertical_datum
        )
        if vertical
        else None
    )
    # (input model, output model) when this job converts BETWEEN geoid
    # models, else None - job.geoid_swap_models' one rule, so the record and
    # the computation cannot disagree about whether a swap ran. Every block
    # this gates is additive and swap-only: a job without a swap keeps every
    # byte it wrote before the feature.
    swap_models = geoid_swap_models(settings, transformation)
    # The frame transformation the rows applied, or None: job's one rule, so
    # every block this gates - all of them additive or reworded only on a
    # transformed job - and the computation cannot disagree about whether
    # HTDP ran. A same-frame record is byte-identical to what it always was.
    frame_path = applied_frame_transformation(settings)
    geodetic_to_geodetic = settings.direction is Direction.GEODETIC_TO_GEODETIC
    # Every job with no State Plane zone at either end: geodetic to geodetic,
    # and a vertical-only job on geodetic positions. The one condition every
    # zone-only sentence below is withheld on (plan W4b, DESIGN.md #70 - the
    # #42 finding-3 class: until W4b the vertical-only geodetic record printed
    # the SPCS 83 projection prose, "inside its zone" and "moved between
    # zones" about a job with no zone).
    # ``job.is_zoneless`` is the one statement of it, shared with the
    # interface's elevation hints (work package W5).
    zoneless = is_zoneless(settings.direction, settings.source_zone)
    # An Auto zone job (docs/PLAN-options-autozone.md): its points are in the
    # zones of their counties, one clean file per zone used. Every block this
    # gates is Auto-only, so no other job's record moves by a byte.
    auto = is_auto_zone_job(settings)
    auto_members = clean_members(result) if auto else ()
    # The model that derived H from h, or None when the Z column already held
    # elevations. Resolved through the same job-layer rule the computation
    # used, so the record cannot name a model the conversion did not read.
    ellipsoid_model_name = (
        factors_geoid_model(settings, transformation).name
        if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID
        else None
    )

    lines: list[str] = []
    add = lines.append

    add(_RULE)
    add(f"  {APP_NAME.upper()}")
    add("  COORDINATE CONVERSION JOB RECORD")
    add(_RULE)
    add("")
    add(f"Generated          {generated.strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S %Z')}")
    add(f"Software           {APP_NAME} version {__version__}")
    add("")

def check_autozone() -> str:
    """Auto zone chooses NCAT's zone from the bundled county file, and refuses outside Michigan.

    Auto zone (docs/PLAN-options-autozone.md, DESIGN.md #71) picks each
    point's State Plane zone by the county it lies in, from
    ``data/mi_counties_tiger2025.json`` - a data file this bundle must carry,
    read by ``michspc.fileio.counties``, which authenticates it against its
    pinned SHA-256 before a byte of it is parsed. Nothing else in this
    self-test reads that file, so a bundle missing it, or carrying an altered
    one, would pass every other check and refuse (or misplace) the first Auto
    job a surveyor ran.

    Through ``job.run``, the public path both tabs take, on an in-memory
    PNEZD file of the two production-NCAT anchors above (NAD83(2011), Auto
    on NAD83(2011), metres):

    * each point lands in the zone NCAT chose for it, in the county the
      fixture names - two different zones in one job;
    * each Auto point's northing and easting are EXACTLY (==) those of a
      fixed-zone job into NCAT's zone: Auto chooses a zone and nothing else;
    * and those coordinates agree with NCAT's own printed figures, so the
      fixed-zone side is not this program agreeing with itself;
    * a third point at Toledo, Ohio, in no Michigan county, refuses the WHOLE
      job, naming that point and only that point - never the nearest zone.
    """
    from michspc.fileio import counties as county_file
    from michspc.fileio import pnezd
    from michspc.job import (
        AUTO_ZONE,
        AutoZoneUnplacedPointsError,
        Direction,
        JobSettings,
        LongitudeConvention,
        run,
    )
    from michspc.spc.frames import NAD83_2011
    from michspc.spc.units import METERS
    from michspc.spc.zones import zone_by_code

    def settings(target_zone):
        return JobSettings(
            input_path=None,
            output_directory=None,
            direction=Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE,
            source_zone=None,
            target_zone=target_zone,
            input_unit=METERS,
            output_unit=METERS,
            longitude_convention=LongitudeConvention.NEGATIVE_WEST,
            auto_frame=NAD83_2011 if target_zone is AUTO_ZONE else None,
        )

    def parsed(rows):
        return pnezd.parse_lines(
            [f"{point_id},{lat!r},{lon!r},,selftest\n" for point_id, lat, lon in rows]
        )

    rows = [(point_id, lat, lon) for point_id, lat, lon, *_rest in AUTOZONE_ANCHORS]
    try:
        result = run(settings(AUTO_ZONE), parsed(rows))
    except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 — any failure is fatal here
        raise SelfTestError(
            f"an Auto zone job that converts in the source tree failed in this "
            f"bundle: {type(error).__name__}: {error}. Auto reads the bundled "
            f"county file {county_file.COUNTIES_FILENAME}; michspc.spec must "
            f"carry it under data/."
        ) from error

    if len(result.points) != len(AUTOZONE_ANCHORS):
        raise SelfTestError(
            f"the Auto job returned {len(result.points)} points for "
            f"{len(AUTOZONE_ANCHORS)} rows."
        )

    worst_m = 0.0
    for point, anchor in zip(result.points, AUTOZONE_ANCHORS):
        point_id, lat, lon, zone_code, county, ncat_n, ncat_e = anchor
        assignment = point.auto_zone
        chosen = None if assignment is None else assignment.zone.code
        if chosen != zone_code or assignment.county_name != county:
            raise SelfTestError(
                f"this bundle's Auto zone placed {lat}, {lon} (NAD83(2011)) in "
                f"zone {chosen}"
                + ("" if assignment is None else f", {assignment.county_name} County")
                + f", where NGS NCAT chooses zone {zone_code}, {county} County. A "
                f"point in the wrong zone gets a coordinate that is entirely "
                f"ordinary and entirely wrong."
            )
        fixed = run(settings(zone_by_code(zone_code)), parsed([(point_id, lat, lon)]))
        fixed_point = fixed.points[0]
        if (point.output_northing, point.output_easting) != (
            fixed_point.output_northing,
            fixed_point.output_easting,
        ):
            raise SelfTestError(
                f"this bundle's Auto job put {point_id} at N "
                f"{point.output_northing!r}, E {point.output_easting!r} m in "
                f"zone {zone_code}, where the fixed-zone job into that zone "
                f"computes N {fixed_point.output_northing!r}, E "
                f"{fixed_point.output_easting!r} m. Auto chooses a zone and "
                f"must not change a single bit of the projection."
            )
        for label, produced, expected in (
            ("northing", point.output_northing, ncat_n),
            ("easting", point.output_easting, ncat_e),
        ):
            difference = abs(produced - expected)
            worst_m = max(worst_m, difference)
            if difference > LINEAR_TOLERANCE_M:
                raise SelfTestError(
                    f"this bundle's Auto job produced a {label} of "
                    f"{produced:,.4f} m for {point_id} in zone {zone_code}, "
                    f"where NGS NCAT prints {expected:,.3f} m - out by "
                    f"{difference:.4f} m, against {LINEAR_TOLERANCE_M} m."
                )

    outside_id, outside_lat, outside_lon = AUTOZONE_OUTSIDE
    try:
        refused = run(settings(AUTO_ZONE), parsed(rows + [AUTOZONE_OUTSIDE]))
    except AutoZoneUnplacedPointsError as error:
        if error.points != ((outside_id, outside_lat, outside_lon),):
            raise SelfTestError(
                f"this bundle refused the Auto job holding a point at Toledo, "
                f"Ohio, but named {error.points!r} where only "
                f"{AUTOZONE_OUTSIDE!r} is in no Michigan county."
            ) from error
    except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 — any other outcome is a failure
        raise SelfTestError(
            f"an Auto job holding a point at Toledo, Ohio raised "
            f"{type(error).__name__} rather than AutoZoneUnplacedPointsError: "
            f"{error}"
        ) from error
    else:
        placed = [
            f"{p.row.point_id} in "
            f"{'no zone' if p.auto_zone is None else p.auto_zone.zone.code}"
            for p in refused.points
        ]
        raise SelfTestError(
            f"this bundle CONVERTED an Auto job holding a point at Toledo, "
            f"Ohio ({'; '.join(placed)}). A point in no Michigan county must "
            f"refuse the whole job by name, never take the nearest zone."
        )

    return (
        f"{len(AUTOZONE_ANCHORS)} points placed in NCAT's zones "
        f"({', '.join(a[3] for a in AUTOZONE_ANCHORS)}) from "
        f"{county_file.COUNTIES_FILENAME} (SHA-256 "
        f"{county_file.COUNTIES_SHA256[:12]}...), identical to the fixed-zone "
        f"jobs and within {worst_m:.4f} m of NCAT; a Toledo, Ohio point "
        f"refused the job by name"
    )


CHECKS: tuple[tuple[str, object], ...] = (
    ("version and application name", check_version),
    ("bundled GEOID18 grid", check_geoid_grid),
    ("bundled VERTCON 3.0 grid pair", check_vertcon_grids),
    ("vertical conversion against NGS NCAT", check_vertical_conversion),
    ("bundled GEOID12B tile", check_geoid12b_tile),
    ("ellipsoid height conversion", check_ellipsoid_height_conversion),
    ("lazily imported dependencies", check_lazy_imports),
    ("Qt startup and bundled icon", check_icon_resource),
    ("end-to-end conversion against NGS NCAT", check_end_to_end_conversion),
    ("SPCS2022 conversion against beta NGS NCAT", check_spcs2022_conversion),
    ("cross-frame refusal", check_frame_refusal),
    ("WGS 84 (G2296) conversion against NGS HTDP", check_wgs84_conversion),
    ("WGS 84 refusals", check_wgs84_refusals),
    ("Auto zone against NGS NCAT", check_autozone),
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Driver
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def run_selftest(log=None) -> list[str]:
    """Run every check in order. Raises ``SelfTestError`` on the first failure.

# MCX 0.8.0 (DRAFT)

**DRAFT. Not released.** The version literal still carries its `-dev` marker
(`0.8.0-dev`), nothing is tagged, and the owner has not seen the new
controls on a real screen. The release has two parts. The WGS 84 and ITRF
part has had its closing review gate, and its findings are fixed (below,
under Verified). The Options, Auto zone and observation date part has had
its interim gate, and its findings are fixed; **its closing gate has not run
yet.** The release build is made on the owner's machine. This file is
written so that what the release claims can be checked against what was
actually verified.

---

**MCX now converts WGS 84 and ITRF positions, and can pick the State Plane
zone for you.** It moves survey coordinates between Michigan's three State
Plane zones and the 19 SPCS2022 zones, between State Plane and geodetic
positions, converts elevations between vertical datums and between geoid
models, accepts GNSS ellipsoid heights, and documents every job well enough
to defend it. It is in production use by a licensed professional surveyor.

This release adds **five global reference frames**, carried to and from
NAD 83(2011) by NGS's own HTDP; an **Options menu**; an **Auto zone** entry
that chooses each point's zone from its county; and moves the **observation
date** into Options. **Nothing about an existing job's numbers changed**:
every coordinate, elevation and factor an SPCS 83, SPCS2022 or vertical job
produces is what 0.7.1 produced, and a job with a fixed zone writes the same
files it wrote before the Auto zone work.

## WGS 84 and ITRF, to and from NAD 83(2011)

Five frames, each a separate entry in every system dropdown, grouped below
the two NAD 83 and NATRF2022 geodetic entries:

- **WGS 84 (G1762)**, **WGS 84 (G2139)** and **WGS 84 (G2296)**: the three
  realizations in force since 16 October 2013;
- **ITRF2014** and **ITRF2020** by name, for precise point positioning
  results (CSRS-PPP, OPUS's ITRF output), so a PPP report's frame is selected
  as it is printed rather than translated into a G-number.

The five are **switched off in the dropdowns by default** (see Options
below): turn them on in Options to use them.

Each converts to and from **NAD 83(2011) at epoch 2010.0** (the epoch NGS,
OPUS, NCAT and the CORS networks publish NAD 83(2011) at), and through it to
and from **every SPCS 83 zone**, in every unit those zones offer.

- **Geodetic to geodetic** is a new direction: WGS 84 or ITRF latitude and
  longitude in, NAD 83(2011) latitude and longitude out, and the reverse. It
  exists only across one of these paths; NAD 83(2011) to NAD 83(2011) is still
  not a conversion.
- **Vertical modes work with the new frames.** A GNSS ellipsoid height in
  WGS 84 or ITRF goes to its NAD 83(2011) ellipsoid height and then to a
  NAVD 88 elevation through the selected geoid model, so a WGS 84 GNSS height
  becomes an elevation in one job.
- **Both tabs.** The Single point tab shows both positions, each labelled
  with its frame, when a transformation ran.

## The Options menu

A new **Options...** menu opens one dialog with three parts. The choices
apply to both tabs at once.

- **Show in the system lists.** One check box per group of entries in every
  FROM and TO dropdown: SPCS 83 zones, SPCS2022 zones, NAD83(2011)
  latitude/longitude, NATRF2022 latitude/longitude, and each of the five
  WGS 84 and ITRF frames. All are on by default except the five WGS 84 and
  ITRF frames, which are off. At least one group of zones and one group of
  latitude/longitude must stay on; the dialog will not accept a choice that
  leaves a dropdown with nothing to pick.
- **Auto zone frame**: NAD83(2011) (SPCS 83 zones, the default) or
  NATRF2022 (SPCS2022 local zones). See Auto zone below.
- **Observation date**: "Today (the date the job runs)", the default, or
  "Enter a date on the tab". See below.

If a change in Options hides an entry that is selected on a tab, that
selection is cleared and the status line says so. Any change in Options
clears a result shown on the Single point tab, so a result on screen always
matches the choices in force.

**What is remembered, and where.** MCX remembers three things between runs:
which groups are shown, the Auto zone frame, and which observation date
choice is in force. They are stored per Windows user, in the registry at
`HKCU\Software\DMARTIN\MCX - Martin Coordinate Exchange`. **No date is ever
stored**, and nothing else is: zones, units, files and folders are chosen
for each job. If a stored value is one MCX does not recognise, that setting
goes back to its default and the status line says so.

## Auto zone

**Auto** is a new entry at the top of the State Plane zone section of the TO
dropdown on both tabs. It chooses the zone **for each point**, from the
point's latitude and longitude, **by the county the point is in**, which is
how the law defines the zones.

- It is offered only as the TO system, and only when the FROM system is a
  latitude/longitude. A northing and easting do not say which zone they are
  in, because the zones overlap in grid coordinates.
- **On NAD83(2011)** it chooses among the three SPCS 83 zones. Each zone's
  counties are the ones MCL 54.231 names: subsection (3) the north zone, (4)
  the central zone, (5) the south zone.
- **On NATRF2022** it chooses among the **18 SPCS2022 low-distortion zones**,
  never the statewide zone 260001. NGS publishes no county list for these
  zones, so MCX measured one from NGS's beta zone polygons: each zone is a
  union of whole counties. The one exception is Keweenaw County, which is two
  zones. **The Isle Royale rule:** a Keweenaw County position at or north of
  47.65 degrees N is placed in Michigan Isle Royale (261018, MI_U61K); south
  of it, in Michigan Houghton (261016, MI_U41H). NGS publishes no text stating
  this rule; NGS's zone bounds give the two zones a shared bound at 47.65 N,
  and NGS's beta NCAT switches between them there to within about 100 m.
- **County lines are the US Census TIGER/Line 2025 counties**, all 83 of
  Michigan's, unsimplified, with their lines extended into the Great Lakes,
  so a point on the water is in a county just as a point on land is. This is
  the county data NGS built SPCS2022 from. MCX ships it as
  `mi_counties_tiger2025.json` and checks its SHA-256 every time it is read.
- **A point exactly on a county line** is placed in the county east of it,
  or, where the line runs exactly east-west, in the county north of it.
- **A point near a zone line** is placed by its county like every other
  point, with no warning.
- **WGS 84 and ITRF input works with Auto on NAD83(2011).** The zone is
  chosen from the point's NAD 83(2011) position after the HTDP
  transformation.
- **Each point's coordinates are exactly what a job with that zone fixed
  would produce**, to the last digit. Auto chooses a zone and changes nothing
  else.

**What an Auto job writes.** The archive is named `<input>_AUTO-SPCS83.zip`
or `<input>_AUTO-SPCS2022.zip`. It holds **one CAD (clean PNEZD) file per
zone the job used**, named `<input>_AUTO-SPCS83_<zone>.csv`, for example
`pts_AUTO-SPCS83_MI-C.csv` or `pts_AUTO-SPCS2022_MI_L45G.csv`, holding that
zone's points in file order. A zone no point fell in gets no file. Each file
is checked on its own points before the archive is kept. The audit CSV names
each point's zone, county and county FIPS code. The job record lists each
zone used with its point count and file, the county rule and where it comes
from, the county data and its SHA-256, the on-line rule, and, for SPCS 83,
MCL 54.234. On screen, the Single point result and the Multi point table show
each point's zone and county.

**What Auto refuses.** None of these ever passes coordinates through
unchanged.

- **A point in no Michigan county**, outside the state or in Canadian water,
  or a point two county polygons both claim: the **whole job** is refused,
  and the message names every such point with the position tested. A point
  is never given the nearest zone.
- **An Auto frame the input cannot reach**: NAD83(2011) or WGS 84 or ITRF
  input with Auto on NATRF2022, and NATRF2022 input with Auto on
  NAD83(2011). There is no published NAD 83 to NATRF2022 transformation
  (`docs/DEFERRED-NATRF2022-BRIDGE.md`). The Auto entry is grayed for such a
  pair, cleared with the same status line as any other frame mismatch, and a
  job that states one is refused by name.
- **A unit one of the possible zones does not define.** Auto offers only the
  units every zone it can choose publishes, so Auto on NATRF2022 offers no US
  survey foot, which NGS publishes for no SPCS2022 zone.

## The observation date

A WGS 84 or ITRF position means nothing until the date it was observed is
stated: Michigan moves about 17 mm a year in those frames against NAD 83. So
every job on one of the five frames carries an **observation date**, a
calendar date. **Where it comes from is now an Options choice:**

- **Today (the default).** The job uses **the local calendar date on the
  computer running MCX, read when Convert is clicked**, and no date box is
  shown on the tabs. **This reverses the earlier rule that the observation
  date has no default, at the owner's instruction**, for this choice only.
- **Enter a date on the tab.** The date box appears on both tabs. It opens
  unanswered every time MCX starts, because a typed date is never
  remembered, and Convert stays unavailable until it is answered. The box is
  enabled only when one of the five frames is selected.

Either way:

- the job record states the date and where it came from: "the date the job
  ran (Options default)" or "entered by the user";
- an entered date must be a real date (there is no 30 February) and not
  after the day the job runs;
- it is converted to a decimal year exactly the way HTDP converts a calendar
  date, and the job record states both;
- one date covers the whole job: a PNEZD file has no column for it, so every
  point in the file is taken as observed on that date, and the record says
  so;
- a date outside the selected WGS 84 realization's period of use (G1762 from
  2013-10-16, G2139 from 2021-01-03, G2296 from 2024-01-07, each ending the
  day before its successor) raises a warning naming the period. It does not
  refuse: reprocessed data can carry a later realization. The ITRF frames
  have no such period.

## NGS HTDP v3.6.0 is the authority

The transformation is **NGS's Horizontal Time-Dependent Positioning program,
HTDP v3.6.0** (`github.com/noaa-ngs/HTDP`, commit
`59fdbe6b7cf5fabf79f901801ecdc33e7ca2095c`, 7 April 2025), transcribed into
MCX and held to HTDP's own output. It is not a reimplementation of the
geodesy: it is HTDP's arithmetic, including the parts a cleaner design would
change.

- **The Helmert transformation and the velocity model, as HTDP applies
  them.** HTDP evaluates the Helmert at the input epoch and then moves the
  position in the output frame with that frame's velocity, from its region-6
  velocity grid for the eastern United States (ITRF2008 velocities on a
  0.5-degree grid, interpolated bilinearly). All of Michigan, Isle Royale and
  the lakes lie in that region.
- **The ellipsoid follows the frame's name, as in HTDP**: WGS 84 (...)
  positions are on the WGS 84 ellipsoid, ITRF and NAD 83 on GRS 80.
- **Forward and reverse are not exact inverses, in HTDP.** Measured on HTDP's
  own arithmetic, for observation dates from 1990-01-01 to 2035-12-31 and
  heights handed to HTDP from -250 m to 610 m, a Michigan position carried out
  and back returns within 9.2e-6 m north, 6.4e-5 m east and 4.3e-6 m up. MCX
  reproduces that, and every job record states it.
- **The job record** gains a FRAME TRANSFORMATION block on transformed jobs:
  both frames and their ellipsoids, the observation date and decimal year,
  HTDP's version and commit, the composition in words, both Helmert sets'
  parameters, the velocity model, the height rule used and the round-trip
  figure above. The audit CSV gains the external position and the NAD 83
  position, each in columns named by frame, on transformed jobs only. The
  clean PNEZD export keeps its five fields and carries the target frame's
  values; a geodetic target writes its decimal and DMS pair as 0.7.1 does.

## Heights

- **An ellipsoid (GNSS) height is part of the 3-D position and is transformed
  with it.** The ellipsoid height out is the target frame's, above the target
  frame's ellipsoid. A WGS 84 height kept unchanged in a NAD 83 job would be
  about a metre wrong under the right label.
- **An elevation is in no frame, so it passes unchanged.** HTDP needs an
  ellipsoid height for the horizontal transformation and is handed the
  elevation in its place. Measured: the horizontal result moves 1.2e-5 m per
  40 m of height difference, so at most 0.012 mm in Michigan.
- **A blank height is transformed at an ellipsoid height of 0 m** and the Z
  stays blank. Measured: at 600 m of real height, Michigan's highest ground,
  the horizontal result moves at most 0.18 mm north and 0.13 mm east.

The job record states which of the three each job used and how many points
used it.

does (Auto zone's own refusals are listed under Auto zone above):
