def output_stem(result: JobResult) -> str:
    """``<input stem>_<zone abbreviation>``, e.g. ``24-118-topo_MI-C``.

    An Auto job has no one zone to abbreviate: its stem is
    ``<input stem>_AUTO-SPCS83`` or ``<input stem>_AUTO-SPCS2022``
    (``auto_stem_suffix``), and each of its clean exports adds its own
    zone's abbreviation after that (``clean_members``).
    """
    settings = result.settings
    if settings.input_path is None:
        # The stem of every member of the archive is built from the input file's
        # name, so a job that came from no file cannot be named. Refused rather
        # than substituting something like "typed" or "untitled": the members
        # would then claim to describe a file that does not exist, in the one
        # deliverable a sealed survey is supported by (amendment #26).
        raise WriteError(
            "This job states it came from no input file "
            "(JobSettings.input_path is None), so its export cannot be named - "
            "every file inside the archive is named after the input file. A "
            "job with no file, such as a single typed point, is displayed "
            "rather than written."
        )
    if settings.direction in (
        Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC,
        Direction.GEODETIC_TO_GEODETIC,
    ):
        # Both write latitudes and longitudes; the record names the frame.
        suffix = "GEODETIC"
    elif settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY:
        # No target zone exists in this mode, so no abbreviation could name
        # the export; what happened to the file is the vertical conversion.
        suffix = "VERTICAL"
    elif is_auto_zone_job(settings):
        suffix = auto_stem_suffix(settings)
    else:
        suffix = settings.target_zone.abbrev
    return f"{settings.input_path.stem}_{suffix}"


def clean_points(result: JobResult, zone: Zone | None = None) -> tuple[ConvertedPoint, ...]:
    """The points ONE clean PNEZD file of this job holds, in file order.

    The single rule ``clean_pnezd_rows`` and ``verify_round_trip`` both
    resolve their file through, so the file written and the file verified
    are the same points by construction.

    * **Every job but Auto**: ``zone`` must be None, and the file holds every
      point - the job's one clean file, exactly as always.
    * **An Auto job**: ``zone`` is REQUIRED, and must be a zone the job's
      points were actually put in (``clean_members``); the file holds that
      zone's points only. Called without one, or with a zone the job did not
      use, it refuses by name. No public function returns or renders an Auto
      job's clean rows across zones: one headerless PNEZD file of northings
      and eastings from two zones looks like one zone's file and is not (the
      interim gate over Z0-Z2, HIGH 1 - before Z3 only the write guard stood
      between ``clean_pnezd_rows`` and ``writers.write_csv_rows``, and the
      guard was in ``write_all``, not here).
    """
    if not is_auto_zone_job(result.settings):
        if zone is not None:
            raise WriteError(
                f"This job is not an Auto zone job, so it has one clean export "
                f"holding every point; it was asked for the file of "
                f"{getattr(zone, 'name', zone)!r} alone. Nothing was rendered."
            )
        return tuple(result.points)
    members = clean_members(result)
    if zone is None:
        used = ", ".join(f"{m.zone.name} ({m.zone.code})" for m in members)
        raise WriteError(
            f"This is an Auto zone job: its points are in {len(members)} "
            f"zone(s) ({used}), and each zone's points go in a clean export of "
            f"their own. Clean PNEZD rows of an Auto job are rendered one zone "
            f"at a time - name the zone - and never across zones, because one "
            f"file of northings and eastings from different zones reads as one "
            f"zone's file. Nothing was rendered."
        )
    for member in members:
        if member.zone is zone:
            return member.points
    raise WriteError(
        f"This Auto zone job put no point in {getattr(zone, 'name', zone)} "
        f"({getattr(zone, 'code', '?')}), so it has no clean export for that "
        f"zone. Nothing was rendered."
    )


def clean_pnezd_rows(result: JobResult, zone: Zone | None = None) -> list[list[str]]:
    """The CAD-bound export: point, northing, easting, elevation, description.

    One file's rows: every point on every job but Auto, and on an Auto job
    the points of ``zone``, which is then required (``clean_points``).
    """
    settings = result.settings
    rows: list[list[str]] = []

    for point in clean_points(result, zone):
        if _geodetic_coordinate_columns(settings):
            # For a vertical-only geodetic job these are the INPUT positions
            # re-rendered through the standard formatters - the longitude
            # exactly as the file wrote it, in its own convention - so the
            # export mirrors the import, formatting-normalized.
            northing = fmt.latitude(point.output_northing)
            easting = fmt.longitude(point.output_easting)
            # The OUTPUT unit, exactly as in every other direction. The columns
            # either side of it are degrees, but the Z column is still linear
            # and the job record's "Units out" and "Precision written" lines
            # both describe it in the output unit (WP-R2 fix A).
            elevation = fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, settings.output_unit)
        else:
            northing = fmt.coordinate(point.output_northing, settings.output_unit)
            easting = fmt.coordinate(point.output_easting, settings.output_unit)
            elevation = fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, settings.output_unit)

        rows.append(
            [point.point_id, northing, easting, elevation, point.row.description]
        )

    return rows


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CleanMember:
    """One clean PNEZD file in the archive: its name and exactly the points it holds.

    ``zone`` is the zone every one of those points was projected into on an
    Auto job, and None on every other job - whose one clean file holds every
    point, in whatever system the job wrote. ``points`` are in file order.
    """

    name: str
    zone: Zone | None
    points: tuple[ConvertedPoint, ...]


def clean_members(result: JobResult) -> tuple[CleanMember, ...]:
    """Every clean PNEZD file this job writes, in archive order.

    **One on every job but Auto**, named by ``member_names`` exactly as
    before. **On an Auto job, one per zone actually used** (the owner's
    decision, plan section 1 item 4): ``<stem>_<zone abbreviation>.csv`` -
    ``pts_AUTO-SPCS83_MI-C.csv`` - holding exactly the points Auto put in
    that zone, in file order, and NO file for a zone no point was put in
    (``_verify_archive`` refuses an empty member, and an empty CAD file
    would be a statement about a zone this job never wrote to). The zones
    are in registry order (``autozone.zones_choosable``), so the files read
    the same way from job to job whatever order the points came in.

    The zone abbreviation follows the archive's stem rather than replacing
    it, so every member of the archive still starts with the archive's own
    name, and an Auto file never takes the name a fixed-zone job of the same
    input gives its clean file (``pts_MI-C.csv``) while holding a different
    set of points.

    Fail closed on a result that does not say where each point went - a
    point with no ``auto_zone``, one whose zone is not the zone it was
    projected into, or a zone Auto cannot choose in this job's system: a
    point written into a file named for a zone it is not in is the error
    this function exists to make impossible.
    """
    settings = result.settings
    if not is_auto_zone_job(settings):
        return (CleanMember(member_names(result)["pnezd"], None, tuple(result.points)),)
    stem = output_stem(result)
    system = auto_system(settings)
    choosable = zones_choosable(system)
    for point in result.points:
        assignment = point.auto_zone
        if assignment is None:
            raise WriteError(
                f"Point {point.point_id} of this Auto zone job carries no zone "
                f"assignment, so no clean export can say which zone it is in. "
                f"Nothing was written."
            )
        if assignment.zone is not point.conversion.target_zone:
            raise WriteError(
                f"Point {point.point_id} of this Auto zone job was assigned "
                f"{assignment.zone.name} ({assignment.zone.code}) but projected "
                f"into {point.conversion.target_zone.name} "
                f"({point.conversion.target_zone.code}). Nothing was written: "
                f"its coordinates would be filed under a zone they are not in."
            )
        if assignment.zone not in choosable:
            raise WriteError(
                f"Point {point.point_id} of this Auto zone job was assigned "
                f"{assignment.zone.name} ({assignment.zone.code}), which Auto "
                f"does not choose in {system.value}. Nothing was written."
            )
    members = []
    for zone in choosable:
        held = tuple(p for p in result.points if p.auto_zone.zone is zone)
        if held:
            members.append(CleanMember(f"{stem}_{zone.abbrev}.csv", zone, held))
    if not members:
        raise WriteError(
            "This Auto zone job converted no points, so there is no zone to "
            "write a clean export for. Nothing was written."
        )
    return tuple(members)


def _require_members_partition_the_job(
    result: JobResult, members: tuple[CleanMember, ...]
) -> None:
    """Refuse clean files that are not, together, exactly the job's points.

    Every point in exactly one file, none in two, none missing, each file in
    the job's own order, and on an Auto job every point of a zone's file
    both assigned to and projected into that zone. Checked by identity against
    ``result.points``, beside the per-file round trip, so that the union of
    the files is what the job converted: a point dropped between two
    zones' files, or written twice, would pass every per-file check.
    """
    order = {id(point): index for index, point in enumerate(result.points)}
    seen: dict[int, str] = {}
    for member in members:
        if not member.points:
            raise WriteError(
                f"The clean export {member.name} would hold no points. Nothing "
                f"was written."
            )
        indexes = []
        for point in member.points:
            key = id(point)
            if key not in order:
                raise WriteError(
                    f"The clean export {member.name} holds point "
                    f"{point.point_id}, which this job did not convert. "
                    f"Nothing was written."
                )
            if key in seen:
                raise WriteError(
                    f"Point {point.point_id} would be written to both "
                    f"{seen[key]} and {member.name}. Nothing was written."
                )
            if member.zone is not None and not (
                point.conversion.target_zone is member.zone
                and point.auto_zone is not None
                and point.auto_zone.zone is member.zone
        advice = (
            f"Check that the point is in Michigan, and that the northing and "
            f"easting are {settings.source_zone.name} coordinates in "
            f"{settings.input_unit.code}."
        )
    return htdp.HtdpVelocityGridError(
        f"{context}: cannot transform {origin} - latitude "
        f"{error.latitude:.6f}, longitude {error.longitude:.6f} (signed, "
        f"negative west), in {frame.code} - to {frame_path.target.code}: "
        f"{error.reason} {advice}",
        latitude=error.latitude,
        longitude=error.longitude,
        position_frame=error.position_frame,
        reason=error.reason,
    )


def _unplaced_points_refusal(
    unplaced: list[_UnplacedPoint], county_set: CountySet
) -> AutoZoneUnplacedPointsError:
    """The whole-job refusal naming every row Auto could not place."""
    frame = unplaced[0].frame
    carried = unplaced[0].transformed_from
    via = (
        f" (each {carried.code} position as HTDP carried it there)"
        if carried is not None
        else ""
    )
    listed = "; ".join(
        f"point {p.point_id} at latitude {p.latitude:.8f}, longitude "
        f"{p.longitude:.8f} - {p.reason}"
        for p in unplaced
    )
    return AutoZoneUnplacedPointsError(
        f"Auto could not choose a zone for {len(unplaced)} point(s) of this "
        f"job, so nothing was converted. Positions tested, in "
        f"{frame.code}{via}: {listed}. No zone is assigned to a point in no "
        f"Michigan county ({county_set.source}, Great Lakes waters included), "
        f"and Auto never assigns the nearest zone or picks between two "
        f"counties that both claim a point. Check these coordinates, remove "
        f"the points from the file, or choose the zone yourself.",
        points=tuple((p.point_id, p.latitude, p.longitude) for p in unplaced),
        frame=frame,
    )


def _auto_zone_for(
    row: pnezd.PnezdRow,
    latitude: float,
    longitude: float,
    settings: JobSettings,
    county_set: CountySet | None,
    context: str,
    transform_arguments: dict,
) -> tuple[ZoneAssignment, tuple[float, float]]:
    """The zone Auto chooses for one point, and the position it was chosen at.

    Plan L4: the position is the point's own in the Auto frame - the input
    itself for NAD83(2011) or NATRF2022 input, and the NAD83(2011) pivot
    HTDP carries a WGS 84 or ITRF position to - found by
    ``convert.geodetic_position``, the function the vertical-only job reads
    its pivot with, through the same ``pivot_path``. Its frame is then
    required to BE the Auto frame (a second gate behind the settings' frame
    gate). A position in no county, or claimed by two, is signalled to
    ``run`` (``_AutoZoneUnplaced``), which collects every such row and then
    refuses the whole job; every other failure refuses the job at once.
    """
    if not isinstance(county_set, CountySet):
        raise TypeError(
            f"{context}: an Auto job converts only against the loaded county "
            f"set; got {type(county_set).__name__}."
        )
    position = geodetic_position(
        latitude, longitude, settings.geodetic_frame, context, **transform_arguments
    )
    if position.frame is not settings.auto_frame:
        raise FrameTransformationUnavailableError(
            f"{context}: Auto chooses zones in {settings.auto_frame.code}, but "
            f"this point's position is in {position.frame.code}, and no zone "
            f"is chosen from a position in another frame."
        )
    system = auto_zone_system(settings.auto_frame)
    try:
        assignment = zone_for(position.latitude, position.longitude, system, county_set)
    except (AutoZoneOutsideError, AutoZoneAmbiguousError) as error:
        if isinstance(error, AutoZoneAmbiguousError):
            claimed = ", ".join(
                f"{c.name} ({c.fips})"
                for c in county_set.containing(position.longitude, position.latitude)
            )
            reason = f"inside more than one county polygon ({claimed})"
        else:
            reason = "in no Michigan county"
        raise _AutoZoneUnplaced(
            _UnplacedPoint(
                point_id=row.point_id,
                latitude=position.latitude,
                longitude=position.longitude,
                frame=position.frame,
                reason=reason,
                transformed_from=(
                    settings.geodetic_frame if position.is_transformed else None
                ),
            )
        ) from error
    return assignment, (position.latitude, position.longitude)


def _convert_row(
    row: pnezd.PnezdRow,
    settings: JobSettings,
    grid,
    transformation: VerticalTransformation | None,
    vertcon_grids: vertcon.VertconGridPair | None,
    swap_grids: _GeoidSwapGrids | None = None,
    *,
    frame_path: FrameTransformation | None = None,
    epoch: htdp.ObservationEpoch | None = None,
    county_set: CountySet | None = None,
) -> ConvertedPoint:
    context = f"point {row.point_id}"
    warnings: list[ConversionWarning] = []
    auto_assignment: ZoneAssignment | None = None

    geodetic_input = settings.direction in (
        Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE,
        Direction.GEODETIC_TO_GEODETIC,
    ) or (
        settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
        and settings.source_zone is None
    )

    # The Z in metres, as the file states it - computed here, before the
    # conversion, because a frame transformation needs the height it is
    # handed (plan decision D6). Only its unit is touched; every rule below
    # about what the Z becomes is unchanged.
    elevation_m = (
        settings.input_unit.to_meters(row.elevation)
        if row.elevation is not None
        else None
    )
    transform_height = _transform_height(settings, frame_path, elevation_m)
    # Keyword arguments for the conversion call: an epoch and a height only
    # on a path that runs HTDP, and nothing at all on a same-frame one, whose
    # call is then exactly the call it always was.
    transform_arguments = (
        dict(epoch=epoch, height=transform_height)
        if transform_height is not None
        else {}
    )

    # An HTDP refusal names the point and the position's own frame, and
    # advises only what applies to where that position came from (interim
    # gate 2 of the WGS 84 build, finding L4): HTDP itself knows neither
    # the row nor whether its input was typed into the file or derived from
    # a grid coordinate.
    try:
        if geodetic_input:
            # The file's "northing" and "easting" columns hold latitude and
            # longitude. The longitude is normalised to the program's signed
            # convention here, at the boundary, and nowhere else.
            latitude = row.northing
            longitude = settings.longitude_convention.to_signed(row.easting)
            _require_geodetic_in_range(
                latitude, longitude, row.easting, settings, context
            )
            if settings.direction is Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE:
                if settings.target_zone is AUTO_ZONE:
                    # Work package Z2: the zone is chosen from the position
                    # in the Auto frame - AFTER any HTDP transformation - and
                    # the point is then projected into it by exactly the call
                    # a fixed-zone job makes, so an Auto point is the
                    # fixed-zone point of its zone, field for field.
                    auto_assignment, chosen_at = _auto_zone_for(
                        row,
                        latitude,
                        longitude,
                        settings,
                        county_set,
                        context,
                        transform_arguments,
                    )
                    target_zone = auto_assignment.zone
                else:
                    target_zone = settings.target_zone
                conversion = project_point(
                    latitude,
                    longitude,
                    settings.geodetic_frame,
                    target_zone,
                    context,
                    **transform_arguments,
                )
                if auto_assignment is not None:
                    # The lead's ruling on Z2 finding 6: no OUTSIDE_ZONE_EXTENT
                    # on an Auto point. Its zone was chosen by the statute's
                    # county rule, so a warning from the zone's approximate
                    # extent box would be false on it. Removed from the
                    # conversion record too, so no surface can reach it.
                    # Fixed-zone jobs are untouched.
                    conversion = replace(
                        conversion,
                        warnings=tuple(
                            w
                            for w in conversion.warnings
                            if w.code is not WarningCode.OUTSIDE_ZONE_EXTENT
                        ),
                    )
                if auto_assignment is not None and (
                    conversion.latitude,
                    conversion.longitude,
                ) != chosen_at:
                    # if/raise, never assert (-O). The zone must have been
                    # chosen at exactly the position that was projected; a
                    # difference would mean the two calls took different
                    # frame paths, and the zone would be the zone of some
                    # other position.
                    raise FrameTransformationUnavailableError(
                        f"{context}: the Auto zone was chosen at latitude "
                        f"{chosen_at[0]!r}, longitude {chosen_at[1]!r}, but "
                        f"the position projected into {target_zone.name} was "
                        f"latitude {conversion.latitude!r}, longitude "
                        f"{conversion.longitude!r}. Refused: the zone must be "
                        f"the zone of the position converted."
                    )
                output_unit = settings.output_unit
                output_northing = output_unit.from_meters(conversion.target_northing)
                output_easting = output_unit.from_meters(conversion.target_easting)
            elif settings.direction is Direction.GEODETIC_TO_GEODETIC:
                # Plan decision D7. No zone: the output columns are the position
                # in the TARGET frame, in the file's own longitude convention.
                conversion = geodetic_to_geodetic(
                    latitude,
                    longitude,
                    settings.geodetic_frame,
                    settings.target_geodetic_frame,
                    context,
                    **transform_arguments,
                )
                out_latitude, out_longitude = written_geodetic_position(conversion)
            self.to_zone, self._visible_rows(self.to_zone, is_source=False)
        )

    @property
    def options(self) -> Options:
        """The Options this tab currently reads."""
        return self._options

    def apply_options(self, options: Options) -> None:
        """Take a new Options value from the window (plan L7, L8; DESIGN.md #71).

        Every Options change discards a displayed result - the #26 stale
        result rule: the lists, the Auto frame and the date source all change
        what a Convert would do, and numbers left on screen would describe
        Options that no longer hold. Then:

        * the rows are re-hidden, and a selection whose row is now hidden is
          CLEARED to the placeholder with ``OPTIONS_RESET_STATUS`` (L8) - an
          Auto entry whose frame the Options no longer choose included;
        * the pair is reconciled and re-grayed, the units re-offered and
          Convert re-gated, by the path a dropdown change takes;
        * leaving "Enter a date" empties the date box, so a date typed earlier
          can never reappear later and be sent unseen (O3: the date is not
          remembered).
        """
        if not isinstance(options, Options):
            raise TypeError(
                f"apply_options needs an options.Options; got "
                f"{type(options).__name__} ({options!r})."
            )
        self._options = options
        self._update_list_rows()
        cleared = False
        self._reconciling = True
        try:
            for combo, is_source in ((self.from_zone, True), (self.to_zone, False)):
                index = combo.currentIndex()
                shown = self._visible_rows(combo, is_source=is_source)
                if index >= 0 and not shown[index]:
                    combo.setCurrentIndex(combo.findData(UNCHOSEN))
                    cleared = True
        finally:
            self._reconciling = False
        if options.date_source is ObservationDateSource.TODAY:
            self.observation_date_edit.setDate(OBSERVATION_DATE_UNANSWERED)
        self._reconcile_zone_frames(clear=self.to_zone)
        self._on_direction_changed()
        self._invalidate_result()
        if cleared:
            self._set_status(OPTIONS_RESET_STATUS)

    def _update_elevation_hint(self) -> None:
        """The one owner of the elevation box's placeholder (#57's rule:
        one method per property).

        Empty in the two vertical modes (#51: the elevation is the value
        being converted, so "optional" would be false). In Horizontal, the
        owner's sentence - unless the selections describe a job with no zone,
        whose combined factor does not exist
        (``controls.combined_factor_is_reported``, asked of the core's own
        zoneless rule). Called on every mode change and every direction
        change, the two things it depends on.
        """
        if self.vertical_mode().converts_elevations:
            text = ""

"""NCAT zone anchors for Auto zone - frozen NGS truth, NOT derived here.

NGS beta: the SPCS2022 rows are beta NCAT's own answers (the web app at
https://beta.ngs.noaa.gov/NCAT/, NATRF2022 epoch 2020.00 in and out, zone
list on 'Auto Pick (default zone)'); re-freeze obligation in
docs/REFREEZE-NSRS.md. The SPCS 83 rows are production NCAT
(https://geodesy.noaa.gov/api/ncat/llh, NAD83(2011) in and out, no spcZone,
so NCAT picks the zone itself; RECON.md section 5.1).

Captured 2026-10-22: the autozone-r0 recon (review/autozone-r0/raw/ncat_probe/)
and work package Z0 (review/autozone-z0/raw/, harness
review/autozone-z0/capture_anchors.py). Written by
review/autozone-z0/freeze_anchors.py; every row names its raw capture and
that file's SHA-256, and tests/test_autozone_anchors.py re-hashes the file and
re-reads NCAT's zone out of it rather than trusting the ``ncat_zone`` text.

Each anchor is OUTSIDE the boundary bands where NCAT disagrees with itself
and with the county sources (RECON.md section 5.3): >= 50 m from any zone
line (or Michigan's outline) on land, >= 1 km in water and outside
Michigan. ``distance_m`` is the generator's measurement, capped at 20,000
(read: at least 20 km); the suite re-measures it. ``expected`` is the zone code NCAT gave, or None where NCAT
gave another state's zone or N/A - Auto must refuse those.

Categories: land / water (in Michigan, land by NGS's own land-only
polygons), outside (not in any Michigan county).
"""

    expected: str | None  # zone code, or None: Auto refuses
    capture: str  # repository path of the raw NCAT response
    sha256: str
    distance_m: float

@pytest.mark.parametrize("which", ["single", "multi"])
def test_hiding_a_selected_entry_clears_it_and_says_so(window, which, tmp_path):
    """L8: a group switched off while it holds a selection clears that
    selection to the placeholder, with one sentence on the status line - the
    #64 strand rule's form. On the Multi point tab the table goes with it."""
    page = pages(window)[which]
    window.apply_options(with_time_dependent())
    choose(page.from_zone, G2296)
    choose(page.to_zone, MI_SOUTH)
    assert page.from_zone.currentData() is G2296

    window.apply_options(options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS)

    assert page.from_zone.currentData() == controls.UNCHOSEN
    assert page.to_zone.currentData() is MI_SOUTH
    assert page.status_label.text() == controls.OPTIONS_RESET_STATUS
    assert controls.OPTIONS_RESET_STATUS.count(".") == 1
    assert page.settings() is None


def test_hiding_a_selection_clears_the_multi_point_table(window, tmp_path):
    window.apply_options(with_time_dependent())
    file_job(window, tmp_path, f"1,{CALHOUN[0]},{CALHOUN[1]},,A\n")
    choose(window.from_zone, G2296)
    choose(window.to_zone, MI_SOUTH)
    assert window.convert() is True, window.shown_failures
    assert window.model.rowCount() == 1

    window.apply_options(options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS)
    assert window.model.rowCount() == 0
    assert window.result is None
    assert window.status_label.text() == controls.OPTIONS_RESET_STATUS


def test_an_options_change_that_hides_nothing_leaves_the_multi_point_table(
    window, tmp_path
):
    """#65: the table describes the archive a run wrote, so an Options change
    that clears no selection leaves it - and re-gates Convert."""
    file_job(window, tmp_path, f"1,{CALHOUN[0]},{CALHOUN[1]},,A\n")
    choose(window.from_zone, NAD)
    choose(window.to_zone, MI_SOUTH)
    assert window.convert() is True, window.shown_failures
    window.apply_options(with_time_dependent())
    assert window.model.rowCount() == 1
    assert window.convert_button.isEnabled() is True


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "change",
    ["lists", "auto frame", "date source"],
)
def test_every_options_change_discards_a_displayed_single_point_result(
    window, change
):
    """L7 + #26: whatever the Options change - even one that hides nothing
    the tab holds - the displayed result goes, both copy paths with it."""
    tab = window.single_point
    converted_single_point(tab)
    new = {
        "lists": with_time_dependent(),
        "auto frame": replaced(options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS, auto_frame=NATRF2022),
        "date source": replaced(
            options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS, date_source=ObservationDateSource.ENTERED
        ),
    }[change]
    assert window.apply_options(new) is True
    assert tab.result is None
    assert tab.sections is None
    assert tab.copy_all_button.isEnabled() is False
    # The selections themselves were not hidden, so they stand.
    assert tab.from_zone.currentData() is NAD
    assert tab.to_zone.currentData() is MI_SOUTH


def test_unchanged_options_change_nothing(window):
    converted_single_point(window.single_point)
    assert window.apply_options(options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS) is False
    assert window.single_point.sections is not None

_OBSERVATION_DATE_SOURCE_TEXT = {
    ObservationDateSource.TODAY: "the date the job ran (Options default), ",
    ObservationDateSource.ENTERED: "entered by the user, ",
}
"""How the record's Observation date line says where the date came from
(docs/PLAN-options-autozone.md L10 and O3). TODAY is held by ``job.run`` to
the LOCAL date the job ran, so "the date the job ran" is a checked fact, not
a description of a setting."""


def _observation_date_source_text(source) -> str:
    """The words after the date, or nothing when the job stated no source.

    None stays accepted by decision (``JobSettings.observation_date_source``,
    the lead's ruling at Z4): a job that states no source prints the line
    exactly as before the Auto build, so every frozen record digest holds. A source with no sentence
    registered refuses rather than printing a date of unstated origin.
    """
    if source is None:
        return ""
    text = _OBSERVATION_DATE_SOURCE_TEXT.get(source)
    if text is None:
        raise ValueError(
            f"This job record cannot state where the observation date came "
            f"from: no sentence is registered for {source!r}."
        )
    return text


_MANUAL_SECTIONS = {
    ProjectionKind.LAMBERT_CONIC_2SP: "3.1",
    ProjectionKind.LAMBERT_CONIC_1SP: "3.1",
    ProjectionKind.TRANSVERSE_MERCATOR: "3.2",
    ProjectionKind.OBLIQUE_MERCATOR: "3.3",
}
"""Which chapter of the manual defines each projection's equations.

Every kind must be here. A kind missing from this table would be silently left
out of the Authority section, so the record would name fewer sets of equations
than the job actually used - a sealed document understating its own method -
and ``_equation_lines`` refuses instead.
"""

_SPCS2022_ZONE_CODES = frozenset(zone.code for zone in SPCS2022_ZONES)
"""The 2022 era, derived from the registry's own tuple rather than restated.

A zone added to ``SPCS2022_ZONES`` is in this set the moment it is added, so a
new 2022 zone cannot be described by the 1983 era's METHOD section.
"""


def _job_zones(settings, result=None) -> tuple:
    """Every zone this record DESCRIBES, source first.

    On an Auto job, the zones its points were actually put in, in registry
    order (``exports.clean_members``, the same zones the clean files and
    the COORDINATE SYSTEMS section name) - never ``AUTO_ZONE`` itself, which
    is not a zone and has no era. So an Auto SPCS2022 job's METHOD is the
    SPCS2022 arm with exactly the projections its zones use. ``result`` is
    required for that and only for that.

    The same rule the COORDINATE SYSTEMS section applies a few lines above -
    including the target zone unless the job converts TO geodetic, where there
    is no target zone to describe and any value left in the field was not used.
    Deriving both sections from one rule means METHOD states the equations for
    exactly the zones the record set out, and cannot name a projection the
    document never showed the reader.

    A geodetic job has one zone; a zone-to-zone job has two; a vertical-only
    job on geodetic positions has none.
    """
    zones = []
    if settings.source_zone is not None:
        zones.append(settings.source_zone)
    if is_auto_zone_job(settings):
        if result is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "An Auto zone job's zones are the ones its points were put in; "
                "pass the JobResult."
            )
        zones.extend(member.zone for member in clean_members(result))
    elif (
        settings.target_zone is not None
        and settings.direction is not Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC
    ):
        zones.append(settings.target_zone)
    return tuple(zones)

"""Work package Z3: what an Auto zone job WRITES (docs/PLAN-options-autozone.md).

Every expectation here comes from outside the code under test:

* **Which zone, which county**: the frozen NCAT anchors
  (``tests/fixtures/autozone_anchors.py``) - NGS's own zone answer for each
  position, and the county named in the anchor's tag. The files are built
  from those positions and the expected file of every point is NCAT's zone.
* **What a zone's clean file holds**: the fixed-zone job of that zone over
  the SAME input, which this program has written since 0.1.0 and the digest
  pins hold - each Auto clean file must be exactly that job's lines for the
  points NCAT put in the zone, byte for byte, in file order.
* **The record's Auto TO block**: written out below by hand, line for line,
  for one mixed-zone job of each system.

Pinned:

1. **Members.** One clean PNEZD file per zone used, ``<stem>_<abbrev>.csv``,
   stem ``<input>_AUTO-SPCS83`` / ``<input>_AUTO-SPCS2022``, zones in
   registry order; no file for an unused zone; audit and record after them.
2. **No cross-zone clean rows** (interim gate over Z0-Z2, HIGH 1):
   ``clean_pnezd_rows`` and ``verify_round_trip`` refuse an Auto result
   without a zone, or with a zone it did not use, at the reviewer's input -
   so mixed rows can never reach a writer as one file.
3. **Verifiers** run per file on exactly that file's points; the partition
   check refuses a point missing, doubled, out of order or in the wrong
   zone's file.
4. **Audit CSV**: Target zone is the point's own zone, County and County
   FIPS follow it; every other cell is the fixed-zone job's.
5. **Record**: the TO block, the era, FILES WRITTEN, and a sweep of every
   sentence that names a zone, a target or the files, on Auto jobs of both
   systems, single- and mixed-zone, NAD83 and WGS 84 input.
6. **Observation date source** wording (TODAY / ENTERED / None).

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from michspc import job

    Direction,
    JobSettings,
    LongitudeConvention,
    ObservationDateSource,
    VerticalMode,
    run,

from michspc.spc.frames import ITRF2020, NAD83_2011, NATRF2022, WGS84_G2296

from tests.fixtures.autozone_anchors import ANCHORS

NEG = LongitudeConvention.NEGATIVE_WEST


def anchor(tag: str, system: str):
    (found,) = [a for a in ANCHORS if a.tag == tag and a.system == system]
    return found


def county_of(tag: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
    """'COUNTY 26001 Alcona' -> ('Alcona', '26001'): the county the anchor's
    capture was taken in, from the fixture, not from the program."""
    _, fips, name = tag.split(" ", 2)
    return name, fips


# The mixed-zone files. File order deliberately interleaves the zones.
TAGS_83 = (
    "COUNTY 26001 Alcona",  # NCAT 2112
    "COUNTY 26025 Calhoun",  # NCAT 2113
    "COUNTY 26003 Alger",  # NCAT 2111
    "COUNTY 26019 Benzie",  # NCAT 2112
    "COUNTY 26005 Allegan",  # NCAT 2113
)
TAGS_2022 = (
    "COUNTY 26001 Alcona",  # NCAT 261006
    "ISLE_ROYALE_LAND",  # NCAT 261018
    "COUNTY 26025 Calhoun",  # NCAT 261007
    "COUNTY 26083 Keweenaw",  # NCAT 261016 (mainland)
    "COUNTY 26003 Alger",  # NCAT 261014
    "COUNTY 26019 Benzie",  # NCAT 261011
    "COUNTY 26005 Allegan",  # NCAT 261008
)
ELEVATIONS = (250.0, None, 190.5, 300.25, None, 210.0, 275.5)


def write_input(tmp_path, tags, system, name="pts"):
    path = tmp_path / f"{name}.csv"
    lines = []
    for index, tag in enumerate(tags):
        a = anchor(tag, system)
        z = ELEVATIONS[index]
        lines.append(f"{index + 1},{a.latitude},{a.longitude},{'' if z is None else z},P{index + 1}\r\n")
    path.write_bytes("".join(lines).encode("ascii"))
    return path


def auto_settings(path, frame, **fields):
    base = dict(
        input_path=path,
        output_directory=path.parent,
        direction=Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE,
        source_zone=None,
        target_zone=AUTO_ZONE,
        input_unit=METERS,
        output_unit=INTERNATIONAL_FEET,
        longitude_convention=NEG,
        geodetic_frame=frame,
        auto_frame=frame,
    )
    base.update(fields)
    return JobSettings(**base)


def members_of(archive) -> dict[str, str]:
    with zipfile.ZipFile(archive) as opened:
        return {name: opened.read(name).decode("utf-8") for name in opened.namelist()}


def expected_zones(tags, system) -> list[str]:
    return [anchor(tag, system).expected for tag in tags]


def auto_job(tmp_path, tags, system, frame, **fields):
    path = write_input(tmp_path, tags, system)
    result = run(auto_settings(path, frame, **fields))
    return result, exports.write_all(result)["archive"]


SYSTEMS = [
    pytest.param(TAGS_83, "SPCS 83", NAD83_2011, "AUTO-SPCS83", id="spcs83"),
    pytest.param(TAGS_2022, "SPCS2022", NATRF2022, "AUTO-SPCS2022", id="spcs2022"),
]


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 1. Members.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


@pytest.mark.parametrize("tags, system, frame, suffix", SYSTEMS)
def test_one_clean_file_per_zone_used_named_for_the_zone(tmp_path, tags, system, frame, suffix):
    result, archive = auto_job(tmp_path, tags, system, frame)
    assert archive.name == f"pts_{suffix}.zip"
    # NCAT's zones, in the registry's order (the code numbers ascend in both
    # registries), one file each and no other.
    used = sorted(set(expected_zones(tags, system)), key=int)
    expected = [f"pts_{suffix}_{zone_by_code(code).abbrev}.csv" for code in used]
    expected += [f"pts_{suffix}_full.csv", f"pts_{suffix}_README.txt"]
    assert list(members_of(archive)) == expected


def test_the_member_names_are_spelled_as_the_owner_will_see_them(tmp_path):
    """Literal, so a change to the naming scheme is a conscious one."""
    _, archive = auto_job(tmp_path, TAGS_83, "SPCS 83", NAD83_2011)
    assert list(members_of(archive)) == [
        "pts_AUTO-SPCS83_MI-N.csv",
        "pts_AUTO-SPCS83_MI-C.csv",
        "pts_AUTO-SPCS83_MI-S.csv",
        "pts_AUTO-SPCS83_full.csv",
        "pts_AUTO-SPCS83_README.txt",
    ]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("tags, system, frame, suffix", SYSTEMS)
def test_each_clean_file_is_the_fixed_zone_jobs_lines_for_ncats_points(
    tmp_path, tags, system, frame, suffix
):
    """Each zone's file is byte for byte the fixed-zone job's clean file of
    that zone over the same input, restricted to the points NCAT puts in the
    zone, in file order. A point in the wrong file, a point missing, a file
    for an unused zone, or a coordinate from another zone all fail here."""
    result, archive = auto_job(tmp_path, tags, system, frame)
    files = members_of(archive)
    zones = expected_zones(tags, system)
    for code in sorted(set(zones), key=int):
        zone = zone_by_code(code)
        fixed_dir = tmp_path / f"fixed_{code}"
        fixed_dir.mkdir()
        fixed = run(
            dataclasses.replace(
                result.settings, target_zone=zone, auto_frame=None, output_directory=fixed_dir
            )
        )
        fixed_files = members_of(exports.write_all(fixed)["archive"])
        fixed_lines = fixed_files[f"pts_{zone.abbrev}.csv"].split("\r\n")
        wanted_ids = {str(i + 1) for i, z in enumerate(zones) if z == code}
        expected = [line for line in fixed_lines if line.split(",")[0] in wanted_ids]
        assert len(expected) == len(wanted_ids)
        auto_text = files[f"pts_{suffix}_{zone.abbrev}.csv"]
        assert auto_text == "\r\n".join(expected) + "\r\n"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("tags, system, frame, suffix", SYSTEMS)
def test_a_single_zone_auto_job_writes_one_clean_file(tmp_path, tags, system, frame, suffix):

"""Work package Z2: Auto zone in ``job.run`` (docs/PLAN-options-autozone.md).

What is pinned here, each against a value that does not come from the code
under test:

1. **The settings.** ``AUTO_ZONE`` is a typed singleton, legal only as the
   target of ``GEODETIC_TO_ZONE``, and requires ``auto_frame`` (the registry's
   NAD83(2011) or NATRF2022 record, by identity); a stray ``auto_frame`` is
   refused by name. Every refusal is pinned at its message.
2. **The frame rule (L2).** Every pairing of input frame and Auto frame that
   has no registered path is refused with ``frames.require_frame_path``'s OWN
   message - compared character for character, so the job cannot have grown a
   second, weaker check.
3. **The unit gate** checks the output unit against EVERY zone the Auto frame
   can choose, before the file is read.
4. **Per point.** The zone is NCAT's (the frozen Z0 anchors, both systems, in
   one mixed-zone file per system), the projected point is the fixed-zone
   job's point of that zone FIELD FOR FIELD (bit-identical, ``repr`` equal)
   except that an Auto point carries no OUTSIDE_ZONE_EXTENT warning; a file
   with ANY point in no county refuses the WHOLE job, naming every such
   point; and a WGS 84 position's zone is chosen at its NAD 83 pivot -
   including at a constructed point where the two positions straddle a zone
   line.
5. **The observation date source** (L10): stray refused, TODAY held to the
   LOCAL date (``today_local``), never the UTC one.
6. **The write guard's successors**: Z2's guard refused to write an Auto
   job; Z3 removed it, and an Auto job now writes (its outputs are pinned in
   tests/test_autozone_outputs.py).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

NEG = LongitudeConvention.NEGATIVE_WEST


def settings(**fields) -> JobSettings:
    base = dict(
        input_path=None,
        output_directory=None,
        direction=Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE,
        source_zone=None,
        target_zone=AUTO_ZONE,
        input_unit=METERS,
        output_unit=METERS,
        longitude_convention=NEG,
        auto_frame=NAD83_2011,
    )
    base.update(fields)
    return JobSettings(**base)


def source(*rows: tuple) -> pnezd.PnezdFile:
    """A parsed PNEZD file from (id, latitude, longitude, elevation-or-None)."""
    lines = []
    for point_id, lat, lon, z in rows:
        lines.append(f"{point_id},{lat!r},{lon!r},{'' if z is None else repr(z)},D\n")
    return pnezd.parse_lines(lines)


EXTENT = WarningCode.OUTSIDE_ZONE_EXTENT


def _no_extent(warnings):
    return tuple(w for w in warnings if w.code is not EXTENT)


def assert_bit_identical(auto_point, fixed_point) -> bool:
    """The precise claim: every ConvertedPoint field identical - coordinates,
    factors, the whole conversion record - field for field and through repr
    (so -0.0 and the last bit both count), EXCEPT that the Auto point carries
    no OUTSIDE_ZONE_EXTENT warning, in ``warnings`` or in
    ``conversion.warnings``, where the fixed-zone point may (the lead's
    ruling on Z2 finding 6). Returns whether the fixed point had one, so a
    caller can prove the exception was exercised."""
    assert fixed_point.auto_zone is None
    assert not any(w.code is EXTENT for w in auto_point.warnings)
    assert not any(w.code is EXTENT for w in auto_point.conversion.warnings)
    expected = dataclasses.replace(
        fixed_point,
        conversion=dataclasses.replace(
            fixed_point.conversion,
            warnings=_no_extent(fixed_point.conversion.warnings),
        ),
        warnings=_no_extent(fixed_point.warnings),
    )
    for f in dataclasses.fields(type(auto_point)):
        if f.name == "auto_zone":
            continue
        a, b = getattr(auto_point, f.name), getattr(expected, f.name)
        assert a == b, f.name
        assert repr(a) == repr(b), f.name
    return len(fixed_point.warnings) != len(expected.warnings)

def test_auto_zone_is_a_typed_singleton_and_neither_none_nor_a_zone():

# -*- mode: python ; coding: utf-8 -*-
"""PyInstaller specification for the Michigan SPC Zone Converter.

Committed, not generated. ``pyinstaller`` can write a spec for you; a written
one drifts from the program the moment anything moves, and this file carries
four decisions that have to be reviewable rather than rediscovered.

Build it through the gated script, never by hand for a release::

    py tools/build_release.py

**One folder, not one file.** One-file is the friendlier thing to *hand* to
someone — but nothing is handed over here. The deliverable is an Inno Setup
installer (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #13), and the surveyor launches the program
from the Start menu, so the folder is never seen. What one-folder buys is worth
having: the program starts immediately instead of unpacking a ~200 MB bundle to
a temporary directory on every launch, and — the reason that decides it — the
bundled GEOID18 tile sits on disk as an ordinary file that can be listed,
checksummed and compared against NGS's published SHA-256 by anyone who wants to
audit what the program is computing with. A one-file build hides that inside the
executable until the moment it runs. For a tool whose output supports a sealed
survey, a verifiable grid beats a tidy folder.

**The version is read, never restated.** ``michspc/__init__.py`` holds the only
version literal in the project (docs/method/TOOLING.md); this file imports it
and builds the Windows version resource from it, so the file properties Explorer
shows and the number the release gate checks cannot disagree.

**The icon is derived here, from the committed master.** ``tools/make_icon.py``
runs as part of the build rather than a hand-made ``.ico`` being committed, so
the artwork has exactly one authoritative representation (docs/DESIGN.md
amendment #15 note 1).

**Nothing is excluded.** ``excludes`` is deliberately empty. Excluding a module
that a dependency imports lazily produces a bundle that builds cleanly and dies
the first time a user reaches that feature — the numpy-under-ezdxf trap recorded
in docs/method/TOOLING.md. The disk this saves is not worth the failure mode it
buys, and the self-test below is what proves the bundle is complete.
"""

import sys
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(SPECPATH).resolve()  # noqa: F821 — PyInstaller injects SPECPATH

# The spec runs in PyInstaller's interpreter, whose sys.path does not
# necessarily include the project.
if str(REPO_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT))

from michspc import APP_FULL_NAME, APP_NAME, APP_PUBLISHER, __version__  # noqa: E402
from michspc.fileio.counties import COUNTIES_FILENAME  # noqa: E402
from michspc.fileio.geoid import ALL_GEOID_MODELS  # noqa: E402
from michspc.fileio.vertcon import VERTCON3_ERR_TILE, VERTCON3_TRN_TILE  # noqa: E402
from michspc.selftest import LAZY_IMPORTS  # noqa: E402
from tools import make_icon  # noqa: E402

EXECUTABLE_NAME = "mcx"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Data files. Destination paths are load-bearing: the running program looks for
# each of these at a specific place under sys._MEIPASS, and
# tests/test_selftest.py pins this file's destinations to those lookups.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

DATA_DESTINATION = "data"
"""``michspc.fileio.ngs_grid.shipped_data_directory`` reads sys._MEIPASS/data;
both the geoid and VERTCON policy layers resolve their grids through it."""

# Every NGS grid the bundle carries, each under NGS's own filename so an auditor
# can list it, hash it and compare it against NGS's published file without
# unpacking anything (see the one-folder decision in the module docstring).
#
# DERIVED from the program's own registries, never restated (WP-V5): the geoid
# filenames come from geoid.ALL_GEOID_MODELS, so a third geoid model cannot be
# added to the registry without the bundle following - the spec refuses below
# if the tile is missing from data/, and the suite refuses if data/ carries a
# grid this derivation does not name. The two VERTCON 3.0 grids come from the
# vertcon module's own tile constants for the same reason.
#
# Each digest is pinned in code and checked by the suite, not merely recorded
# in the plan: GeoidModel.sha256 on each registry record (authenticated at
# every load), vertcon.VERTCON3_TRN_SHA256 and vertcon.VERTCON3_ERR_SHA256.
# This list is names only - a name is what PyInstaller needs - so nothing here
# can stand in for that.
NGS_GRID_FILENAMES = tuple(model.tile_filename for model in ALL_GEOID_MODELS) + (
    VERTCON3_TRN_TILE.name,  # the shift
    VERTCON3_ERR_TILE.name,  # its uncertainty
)

NGS_GRID_SOURCES = [REPO_ROOT / "data" / name for name in NGS_GRID_FILENAMES]

# The Auto zone county polygons (docs/PLAN-options-autozone.md, work package
# Z5): Michigan's 83 TIGER 2025 counties, US Census data rather than an NGS
# grid, so listed apart from the grids. The name comes from the file's one
# reader, michspc.fileio.counties, which resolves it through
# ngs_grid.shipped_data_directory like the grids and authenticates it against
# counties.COUNTIES_SHA256 at every load. It is a DERIVED serialization, never
# a copy of any captured file in the repository (gate 6 refuses one).
COUNTY_SOURCES = [REPO_ROOT / "data" / COUNTIES_FILENAME]

SHIPPED_DATA_SOURCES = NGS_GRID_SOURCES + COUNTY_SOURCES
"""Everything the bundle carries under data/: exactly the files data/ holds."""

ICON_DESTINATION = "assets/icon"
"""``michspc.gui.icon.icon_candidates`` reads
sys._MEIPASS/assets/icon/coord-convert.ico."""

missing = [str(path) for path in SHIPPED_DATA_SOURCES if not path.is_file()]
if missing:
    raise SystemExit(
        "Data files are missing from the source tree: "
        + ", ".join(missing)
        + ". These ship with the program: without the geoid tile the bundle "
        "cannot compute an elevation or combined factor for any point, "
        "without the VERTCON pair it cannot convert a height between vertical "
        "datums, and without the county file no Auto zone job can choose a "
        "zone. The self-test would refuse the build anyway."
    )

# Derived here so a bundle can never ship yesterday's artwork.
ICON_FILE = make_icon.generate()

datas = [(str(path), DATA_DESTINATION) for path in SHIPPED_DATA_SOURCES]
datas.append((str(ICON_FILE), ICON_DESTINATION))

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Deferred imports. PyInstaller's analysis does follow imports inside function
# bodies, but every one of these is reached only from inside one, so a change in
# its analyser would silently drop them. They are declared rather than trusted,
# and the list is michspc.selftest's own — the same names the frozen bundle then
# proves it can actually import.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

hiddenimports = list(LAZY_IMPORTS)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Windows version resource, built from the one version literal.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _version_tuple(text):
    """``"1.2.3"`` -> ``(1, 2, 3, 0)``. A Windows version is four integers.

    A pre-release marker (``1.2.3-dev``) is stripped for the numeric tuple,
    which has nowhere to put it; the full string still goes into the text
    fields, so a developer build says what it is. The release gate refuses to
    build at all while the marker is present (docs/method/METHOD.md section 6),
    so a shipped executable never relies on this.
    """
    numbers = []
    for part in text.split("-")[0].split(".")[:4]:
        numbers.append(int(part) if part.isdigit() else 0)
    while len(numbers) < 4:
    "michspc.fileio.ngs_grid",
    "michspc.fileio.vertcon",
    "michspc.spc.vertical",
    # The three projection engines. michspc.spc.projection is statically
    # reachable (convert.py and report.py import it at module level), but the
    # engines themselves are imported ONLY from inside that module's dispatch
    # table builder, which PyInstaller's static analysis cannot see - so
    # without these three declarations the bundle would carry a dispatcher and
    # no mathematics, and every conversion in a frozen build would die on an
    # ImportError. Exactly the shape of the WP-V2 defect recorded at
    # docs/DESIGN.md #38, where the VERTCON data shipped with no reader.
    "michspc.spc.lambert",
    "michspc.spc.tm",
    "michspc.spc.omerc",
    # NGS HTDP v3.6.0, transcribed (the WGS 84 build, DESIGN.md #70). Both are
    # statically reachable today - frames.py imports them at module level -
    # and declared for the reason the vertical-datum modules are: this list is
    # the names the bundle then PROVES it can import, and a transcription that
    # later moved behind a function-body import would otherwise go missing
    # with nothing noticing. check_wgs84_conversion is what proves the
    # arithmetic, and the velocity grid inside htdp_data, are intact.
    "michspc.spc.htdp",
    "michspc.spc.htdp_data",
    # The GUI, which the self-test itself never opens — so nothing else in this
    # process would notice if Qt were missing from the bundle.
    "PySide6.QtCore",
    "PySide6.QtGui",
    "PySide6.QtWidgets",
    "michspc.gui.app",
    "michspc.gui.icon",
    "michspc.gui.window",
    "michspc.gui.results_model",
    # The single-point tab's four modules. They reach the frozen bundle
    # transitively, because window.py imports them at module level - but this
    # list's stated contract is the names the bundle then PROVES it can import,
    # and satisfying it only indirectly is how a lazily imported module goes
    # missing later without anything noticing (closing review gate).
    "michspc.gui.controls",
    "michspc.gui.single_point",
    "michspc.gui.result_panel",
    # The copy glyph. It is drawn with QPainter rather than loaded from a file
    # precisely so the bundle carries no new asset (amendment #27) - but the
    # module still has to be IN the bundle, and a missing one would show as a
    # row of buttons with no picture on them rather than as an error.
    "michspc.gui.copy_icon",
    # Degrees / minutes / seconds entry (amendment #28): the four boxes and the
    # composition behind them. fileio.dms is the one that matters most here -
    # it is the only module in the program that turns typed text into an angle.
    "michspc.gui.dms_entry",
    "michspc.fileio.dms",
    # Auto zone and the Options (DESIGN.md #71, work package Z5). All
    # statically reachable today - job.py imports the county reader and the
    # zone lookup, autozone.py its two county tables, window.py and
    # single_point.py the three Options modules - and declared on the
    # single-point tab's precedent: this list is what the bundle PROVES it can
    # import. check_autozone below is what proves the county file is present
    # and authenticates; the Options modules have no check of their own, and
    # a missing one would show as a window that never opens.
    "michspc.spc.autozone",
    "michspc.spc.autozone_spcs83",
    "michspc.spc.autozone_spcs2022",
    "michspc.fileio.counties",
    "michspc.gui.options",
    "michspc.gui.options_dialog",
    "michspc.gui.settings_store",
    # Standard-library modules the shipped code paths reach for. Cheap to check
    # and they have been dropped from bundles before by an over-eager exclude.
    "csv",
    "zipfile",
    "hashlib",
    "struct",
    "zlib",
)


def check_lazy_imports() -> str:
    """Every deferred dependency actually imports, out of this bundle."""
    import importlib

    for name in LAZY_IMPORTS:
        try:
            importlib.import_module(name)
        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 — any failure is fatal here
            raise SelfTestError(
                f"{name} is not importable from this bundle: "
                f"{type(error).__name__}: {error}. It is imported lazily, so "
                f"nothing would have failed until a surveyor reached the "
                f"feature that needs it. Add it to hiddenimports in "
                f"michspc.spec, or stop excluding it."
            ) from error
