tests\test_vertcon.py:470:def test_a_trailing_byte_is_refused(tmp_path):
tests\test_vertcon.py:535:def test_a_checksum_mismatch_is_refused_and_names_both_digests(tmp_path):
tests\test_vertical_disclosure.py:288:def test_a_vertical_record_carries_both_grid_files_and_both_digests(tmp_path):
    never passes it.
    """
    path = path or DATA_DIR / COUNTIES_FILENAME
    try:
        raw = path.read_bytes()
    except OSError as error:
        raise CountyDataError(
            f"Could not read the county file at {path}: {error}. This file "
            f"ships with the program; if it is missing the installation is "
            f"incomplete."
        ) from error

    if verify_checksum:
        digest = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
        if digest != COUNTIES_SHA256:
            raise CountyDataError(
                f"{path} does not match the county file this program was "
                f"built against.\n  expected SHA-256 {COUNTIES_SHA256} "
                f"({COUNTIES_BYTES} bytes)\n  found    SHA-256 {digest} "
                f"({len(raw)} bytes)\nZones chosen from unverified county "
                f"lines could be wrong in ways nothing downstream would "
                f"notice, so it is refused."
            )

    try:
        document = json.loads(raw.decode("utf-8"))
    except (UnicodeDecodeError, ValueError) as error:
        raise _refuse(path, f"it is not valid UTF-8 JSON ({error})") from error
    if not isinstance(document, dict) or document.get("format") != COUNTIES_FORMAT:
        raise _refuse(path, f"its format tag is not {COUNTIES_FORMAT!r}")
    records = document.get("counties")
    if not isinstance(records, list):
        raise _refuse(path, "it has no county list")
    if len(records) != COUNTY_COUNT:
        raise _refuse(path, f"it holds {len(records)} counties, not Michigan's {COUNTY_COUNT}")

    counties = [_parse_county(path, i, record) for i, record in enumerate(records)]
    for field_name in ("fips", "name"):
        values = [getattr(c, field_name) for c in counties]
        repeated = sorted({v for v in values if values.count(v) > 1})
        if repeated:
            raise _refuse(path, f"more than one county has the {field_name} {repeated}")

    try:
        county_set = CountySet(counties, source=COUNTIES_SOURCE)
        require_tables_cover(county_set)
    except AutoZoneDataError as error:
        raise _refuse(path, str(error)) from error
    return county_set


@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
def default_counties() -> CountySet:
    """The shipped county set, read and authenticated once per process."""
    return load_counties()
    """Every computed quantity for every point, with a header row.

    This is the file that answers "how was this coordinate derived" without
    anyone having to re-run the program.
    """
    settings = result.settings
    out_unit = settings.output_unit
    in_unit = settings.input_unit
    geodetic_source = _geodetic_source_columns(settings)
    geodetic_target = _geodetic_coordinate_columns(settings)
    vertical = settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations
    path = applied_frame_transformation(settings)

    header = audit_columns(result)
    rows: list[list[str]] = [header]

    for point in result.points:
        conversion = point.conversion
        factors = point.factors

        # The source columns hold whatever the file's columns two and three
        # held. For a geodetic input that is a latitude and a longitude in
        # decimal degrees, and formatting them as linear coordinates rounded
        # them to the unit's 3 places: 42.73250000 was recorded as 42.733,
        # about 55 m of latitude, and -84.55550000 as -84.555, about 37 m of
        # longitude - in the one file that exists to say how the number was
        # derived (WP-R2 fix F). The longitude is printed as the file wrote it,
        # in the job's own convention; the signed pivot has its own column.
        if geodetic_source:
            source_northing = fmt.latitude(point.row.northing)
            source_easting = fmt.longitude(point.row.easting)
        else:
            source_northing = fmt.coordinate(point.row.northing, in_unit)
            source_easting = fmt.coordinate(point.row.easting, in_unit)

        # What the "Target zone" cell says the output system is. A
        # vertical-only job HAS no output horizontal system - the coordinate
        # columns beside this cell reproduce the input's - so the honest
        # statement is the direction itself: "vertical only".
        if settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY:
            target_system = settings.direction.value
        elif is_auto_zone_job(settings):
            # The point's OWN zone - the one it was projected into, which
            # clean_members holds equal to its assignment before any write.
            target_system = conversion.target_zone.name
        elif settings.target_zone:
            target_system = settings.target_zone.name
        else:
            target_system = "geodetic"

        row = (
            [
                point.point_id,
                settings.source_zone.name if settings.source_zone else "",
                source_northing,
                source_easting,
                target_system,
                fmt.coordinate(point.output_northing, out_unit)
                if not geodetic_target
                else fmt.latitude(point.output_northing),
                fmt.coordinate(point.output_easting, out_unit)
                if not geodetic_target
                else fmt.longitude(point.output_easting),
                fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, out_unit),
                f"in {in_unit.code}, out {out_unit.code}",
                fmt.latitude(conversion.latitude),
                fmt.longitude(conversion.longitude),
                fmt.latitude_dms_fields(conversion.latitude),
                fmt.longitude_dms_fields(conversion.longitude),
                fmt.geoid_height(factors.geoid_height),
                fmt.geoid_height(factors.ellipsoid_height),
                fmt.factor(conversion.source_scale_factor),
                fmt.angle_dms(conversion.source_convergence),
                fmt.factor(factors.grid_scale_factor),
                fmt.angle_dms(conversion.target_convergence),
                fmt.factor(factors.elevation_factor),
                fmt.factor(factors.combined_factor),
                fmt.signed_parts_per_million(factors.combined_factor),
                "; ".join(w.code.value for w in point.warnings),
                point.row.description,
            ]
        )

        if is_auto_zone_job(settings):
            # First of the insertions, because it sits leftmost: each later
            # one is placed by its index in the finished header, which is
            # right only once every column to its left is in the row.
            at = header.index(AUTO_COUNTY_COLUMNS[0])
            row[at:at] = [point.auto_zone.county_name, point.auto_zone.county_fips]

        if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID:
            row.insert(
                header.index("Input height kind"), settings.input_height_kind.value
            )

        if vertical:
            # Inserted at the vertical header's own indexes, computed from the
            # header this function just built, so the cells cannot land under
            # the wrong headings if either insertion point moves. The vertical
            # block first (it sits earlier), then Geoid model.
            # Which numbers the shift and sigma cells carry is stated once,
            # in vertical_shift_and_sigma_m, and shared with the on-screen
            # table: the datum reading's pair ordinarily, the SWAP shift
            # with no sigma on a geoid-to-geoid point, neither where no
            # reading exists (no elevation, or a coverage refusal - the
            # warnings cell says which). An identity reading carries
            # shift_m=0.0 - a real zero, printed as one; sigma_m is None on
            # an identity (no model ran) and where the error model
            # interpolates below zero (DESIGN.md #36) - both render N/A
            # through the formatter, never the raw figure.
            shift_m, sigma_m = vertical_shift_and_sigma_m(point)
            insert_at = header.index("Source vertical datum")
            # The PRE-shift ORTHOMETRIC height. For an orthometric-input job
            # that is exactly what the file supplied; for an ellipsoid-input
            # job it is the height DERIVED from it, because this column means
            # "what the vertical shift was applied to" and the shift was
            # applied to H, never to h. N/A where no height was derived - a
            # blank Z, or a point off the geoid tile.
            source_elevation = point.row.elevation
            if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID:
                source_elevation = (
                    in_unit.from_meters(point.ellipsoid_height.orthometric_height_m)
                    if point.ellipsoid_height is not None
                    else None
                )
            vertical_cells = [
                settings.source_vertical_datum.code,
                settings.target_vertical_datum.code,
                fmt.coordinate(source_elevation, in_unit),
                # Both cells are converted into IN_UNIT - the same unit
                # object the two headings above were built from, so heading
                # and value cannot claim different units.
                fmt.vertical_quantity(shift_m, in_unit),
                fmt.vertical_quantity(sigma_m, in_unit),
            ]
            if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID:
                vertical_cells.insert(
                    2, fmt.coordinate(point.row.elevation, in_unit)
                )
            row[insert_at:insert_at] = vertical_cells
            if settings.source_geoid_model is not None:
                row.insert(
                    header.index("Source geoid model"),
                    settings.source_geoid_model.name,
                )
            row.insert(
                header.index("Geoid model"),
                # result.geoid_model is the name of the model the factors
                # were computed from, or None for a job that stated no geoid
                # is applied - in which case there is no model to name and
                # the factor columns beside it are N/A too.
                result.geoid_model
                if result.geoid_model is not None
                else fmt.NOT_AVAILABLE,
            )

        if path is not None:
            # After Description, under the headings audit_columns appended.
            row.extend(_frame_transformation_cells(point, path, settings))

        rows.append(row)

    return rows

        ResultValue(

OBSERVATION_DATE_LABEL = "Observation date"
"""The audit CSV's own heading for the job's one date (``exports.
frame_transformation_columns``), shown directly under the WGS 84 or ITRF
position it dates. A position in a time-dependent frame means nothing until
the date it was observed is stated (``frames.FrameEpochKind``), so a Copy all
that carried the position without it would paste half a fact."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _Positions:
    """The geodetic rows of one point: what the panel shows at each end.

    ``input`` and ``output`` are the rows for the position the job READ and
    the one it WROTE, where those are geodetic; ``pivot`` is the NAD83(2011)
    position on a transformed job and empty otherwise.
    """

    input: tuple[ResultValue, ...]
    output: tuple[ResultValue, ...]
    pivot: tuple[ResultValue, ...]


def _positions(point, positive_west: bool) -> _Positions:
    """The panel's geodetic rows for this point, each block from its own record.

    Same-frame job: the pivot is the only position, at either end, under the
    labels the panel has always used, and ``pivot`` is empty (the zone-to-zone
    layout shows it as its own block, unchanged).

    Transformed job (plan decision D5): the NAD83(2011) pivot -
    ``conversion.latitude`` / ``longitude`` - and the WGS 84 or ITRF
    position - ``conversion.external_position`` - are different positions in
    different frames, and each is shown under its own frame's name, never one
    under the other's. Which end each one is comes from the path's own records
    (``exports.external_frame`` / ``pivot_frame``): the external position is
    the INPUT when the path starts in the time-dependent frame, and the OUTPUT
    otherwise - the position ``job.written_geodetic_position`` says a geodetic
    target writes. The observation date follows the external position.
    """
    conversion = point.conversion
    if not conversion.is_transformed:
        same = _geodetic_values(conversion, positive_west)
        return _Positions(input=same, output=same, pivot=())

    path = conversion.frame_transformation
    external = conversion.external_position
    pivot_rows = _position_values(
        conversion.latitude,
        conversion.longitude,
        positive_west,
        pivot_frame(path).code,
    )
    external_rows = (
        *_position_values(
            external.latitude,
            external.longitude,
            positive_west,
            external_frame(path).code,
        ),
        ResultValue(
            OBSERVATION_DATE_LABEL, conversion.observation_epoch.date.isoformat()
        ),
    )
    reads_external = external_frame(path).code == path.source.code
    # The written end, asked of the core's own rule rather than inferred: a
    # mismatch here would be the pivot labelled with the WGS 84 frame.
    writes_external = written_geodetic_position(conversion) == (
        external.latitude,
        external.longitude,
    )
    if reads_external == writes_external:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Point {point.point_id} carries the transformation {path}, but "
            f"its external position is neither the input nor the output "
            f"alone. Refused rather than labelling one frame's position with "
            f"the other's name."
        )
    if reads_external:
        return _Positions(input=external_rows, output=pivot_rows, pivot=pivot_rows)
    return _Positions(input=pivot_rows, output=external_rows, pivot=pivot_rows)


def _grid_values(
    zone, unit, northing, easting, elevation, elevation_label: str = ELEVATION_LABEL
) -> tuple[ResultValue, ...]:
    """Zone, units and the three linear columns, in that order.

    ``elevation_label`` names the vertical datum on a vertical conversion
    (``Elevation (NAVD88)``) and is the plain ``ELEVATION_LABEL`` everywhere
    else - the label moves, the value's formatter never does.
    """

    def convert(self) -> bool:
        """Convert the typed point and display it. True if it landed.

        **No wait cursor and no ``setEnabled(False)``.** That ceremony exists in
        ``MainWindow.convert`` because a file of several thousand points takes
        long enough for a frozen window to look like a crash. One point is
        instantaneous - a single ``_convert_row`` and one geoid lookup - so the
        same ceremony here would be a flicker that says "this is slow" about
        something that is not. Recorded as a decision rather than an omission.

        Nothing is written on this path at all, so there is no overwrite prompt
        and no output folder to open.
        """
        settings = self.settings()
        if settings is None or not self.form_is_complete():
            # Convert is disabled in this state; reaching here would mean the
            # enablement logic and this method disagree, so say so rather than
            # converting a half-specified point.
            self._report_failure(ValueError(INCOMPLETE_FORM))
            return False

        self.result = None
        self.last_failure = None
        self._render_sections(None)

        try:
            first, second = self.typed_coordinates(settings)
            parsed = pnezd.parse_typed_point(
                first,
                second,
                self.elevation_edit.text(),
                source=self.typed_point_source(),
            )
            result = run(settings, source=parsed)
            sections = single_point_sections(result)
            warnings = single_point_warnings(result)
        except AutoZoneUnplacedPointsError as error:
            # NAMED, as HtdpRefusal is below: Auto refuses a point in no
            # Michigan county (plan L5), and the message names the point.
            self._report_failure(error)
            return False
        except htdp.HtdpRefusal as error:
            # NAMED, not left to the clause below (interim gate 2 of the WGS
            # 84 build, finding L4): an HTDP refusal - a position outside the
            # velocity grid, a non-convergence - subclasses neither ValueError
            # nor FrameMismatchError, so a handler narrowed to those one day
            # would let it escape the tab. Shown exactly as raised, like
            # every other refusal; job.run has already named the point and the
            # position's own frame in it.
            self._report_failure(error)
            return False
        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - shown in full, then stopped
            self._report_failure(error)
            return False

        self.result = result
        self._render_sections(sections, warnings)
        self._report_success(result)
        return True

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # The results panel
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _render_sections(
        self,
        sections: tuple[ResultSection, ...] | None,
        warnings: str = NO_RESULT_WARNINGS,
    ) -> None:
        """Show a result, or ``None`` to empty the panel.

        Copy all follows the panel exactly: it is enabled when, and only when,
        there is something on screen to copy.

        The warnings field is driven from here rather than from ``convert``, so
        that it is emptied by the SAME call that empties the panel. Every route
        that discards a result - a control changing, a refusal, a fresh run
        starting - goes through this method, and a warnings field that outlived
        the numbers it described would be the stale-result failure of amendment
        #26 in a new place: the surveyor would read "none" beside a blank panel
        and take it as this point's answer.
        """
        self.panel.render_sections(sections)
        self.copy_all_button.setEnabled(sections is not None)
        self.warnings_label.setText(warnings)

    @property
    def sections(self) -> tuple[ResultSection, ...] | None:
        """The exact sections the panel last rendered, or None if it is empty.

        Read from the panel rather than kept beside it, so Copy all serialises
        what is on screen rather than a second copy of it that could go stale.
        """
        return self.panel.sections

    @property
    def value_labels(self) -> list[QLabel]:
        """The panel's value widgets, in screen order."""
        return self.panel.value_labels

    @property
    def copy_buttons(self) -> list[QToolButton]:
        """The panel's per-value copy buttons, in screen order."""
        return self.panel.copy_buttons

    def displayed_rows(self) -> tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]:
        """What the panel is showing, as (label, value) pairs in screen order."""
        return self.panel.displayed_rows()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Copying
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _set_clipboard(self, text: str) -> None:
        """The one route to the clipboard. Overridden in tests, which run
        headless and must not depend on a platform clipboard."""
        QGuiApplication.clipboard().setText(text)

    def copy_value(self, index: int) -> bool:
        """Put exactly one displayed value on the clipboard.

        The value string alone: no label, no unit, no trailing newline. It is
        pasted straight into a CAD prompt or a spreadsheet cell, and anything
        else in the buffer has to be deleted there.
        """
        if self.sections is None:
            # Nothing has been converted, so there is nothing to copy. Refused
            # rather than copying an empty string, which would silently destroy
            # whatever the user already had on the clipboard.
            return False
        self._set_clipboard(self.panel.values[index].text)
        return True

    def copy_all(self) -> bool:
        """Put the whole result on the clipboard, labels included.

        Serialises the *same* sections tuple the panel rendered, through
        ``results_model.single_point_clipboard_text``, so the screen and the
        clipboard cannot disagree about what the result contains.
        """
        if self.sections is None:
            return False
        self._set_clipboard(single_point_clipboard_text(self.sections))
        return True

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Reporting
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def status_text(self, result: JobResult) -> str:
        """"Converted. 1 warning." — one point, so the count is of warnings.

            f"Options group; every item zone_combo builds belongs to one."
        )
    if is_auto(data):
        if is_source or data.frame is not options.auto_frame:
            return False
    return options.is_group_shown(group)


def visible_rows(options: Options, items, *, is_source: bool) -> tuple[bool, ...]:
    """One shown/hidden flag per dropdown row, separators included.

    ``items`` is the combo's data in index order (``controls.combo_items``),
    a separator being None. A separator is shown exactly when it would sit
    BETWEEN two shown entries - placed before the next shown entry, provided
    an entry other than the placeholder is shown above it - so switching a
    group off never leaves two rules together, a rule at the top, or a rule
    at the bottom.
    """
    flags = [False] * len(items)
    pending: int | None = None
    seen_entry = False
    for index, data in enumerate(items):
        if data is None:
            if seen_entry:
                pending = index
            continue
        shown = is_shown(options, data, is_source=is_source)
        flags[index] = shown
        if shown and data != UNCHOSEN:
            if pending is not None:
                flags[pending] = True
                pending = None
            seen_entry = True
    return tuple(flags)

# Persistence (plan L9). Three kinds of key, each read strictly.
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

DATE_SOURCE_KEY = "observation_date/source"
_TRUE = "true"
_FALSE = "false"


def _reset_message(name: str, raw, default_text: str) -> str:
    return (
        f"Options: the remembered value of \"{name}\" ({raw!r}) is not one MCX "
        f"recognises, so it was reset to its default ({default_text})."
    )
"""The shipped Michigan county polygons: the only reader of the county file.

``data/mi_counties_tiger2025.json`` holds Michigan's 83 counties as the US
Census Bureau's TIGER/Line 2025 county shapefile draws them - water-extended
into the Great Lakes, unsimplified, every ring and vertex in TIGER's own order,
every coordinate the shapefile's own double (owner's decision O1,
docs/PLAN-options-autozone.md). It is a DERIVED serialization, written by
``review/autozone-z0/derive_counties.py`` from the zip that script
authenticates, and it is what Auto zone tests a position against
(``michspc.spc.autozone``).

**Authenticated on every load, as the NGS grids are** (``geoid.py``): the
whole file is hashed against the digest pinned here before a byte of it is
parsed, then its structure is checked - the format tag, 83 counties, unique
FIPS codes and names, every ring closed and made of finite coordinates inside
Michigan's neighbourhood - and finally its counties are joined against both
zone tables, which must name exactly the same FIPS codes with exactly the same
county names. Each check refuses by name. A county file that fails any of them
is never used: a wrong county polygon assigns a wrong zone with nothing
downstream to notice.

File layout (one JSON object; LF line endings, one county per line, marked
``-text`` in ``.gitattributes`` so a Windows checkout cannot move its digest):

    "format":       "mcx-county-polygons/1"
    "source", "selection", "coordinates", "rings", "derived_by":  provenance text
    "counties":     [{"fips": "26001", "name": "Alcona",
                      "namelsad": "Alcona County",
                      "rings": [[[lon, lat], ...], ...]}, ...]   sorted by FIPS

Longitude negative west, NAD83 geographic, as TIGER stores it.

Packaging (work package Z5): this file must ship under ``data/`` beside the
NGS grids, and this module must be importable in the frozen bundle.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import math
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path

from michspc.fileio import ngs_grid
from michspc.spc.autozone import (
    AutoZoneDataError,
    County,
    CountySet,
    require_tables_cover,
)

DATA_DIR = ngs_grid.shipped_data_directory()

COUNTIES_FILENAME = "mi_counties_tiger2025.json"
COUNTIES_BYTES = 1_659_316
COUNTIES_SHA256 = "53664dd816375b407c5269cabde565fc89d76e0a3f3ed320fb264240b49f33c3"
"""SHA-256 of the file ``derive_counties.py`` wrote from
tl_2025_us_county.zip (SHA-256
9c6e9d9076abce2670d1de255de3710c35ecca00a7005d88e012dec52d95f763), 2026-10-22."""

COUNTIES_FORMAT = "mcx-county-polygons/1"
COUNTY_COUNT = 83
"""Michigan's counties: MCL 54.231's 15 + 27 + 41."""

COUNTIES_SOURCE = "US Census TIGER/Line 2025 counties"
"""How a refusal and the job record name the county data."""

# Michigan's neighbourhood, generously: TIGER's Michigan counties span about
# 41.70-48.31 N and 90.42-82.12 W (measured from the file). A vertex outside
# this box is not a Michigan county line, whatever else it is.
_LAT_RANGE = (41.0, 49.0)
_LON_RANGE = (-91.0, -82.0)


class CountyDataError(Exception):
    """The shipped county file could not be read, or is not the file pinned."""


def _refuse(path: Path, problem: str) -> CountyDataError:
    return CountyDataError(
        f"The county file {path} cannot be used: {problem}. Auto zone picks a "
        f"zone from these polygons, so a wrong one would assign a wrong zone "
        f"with nothing downstream to notice; it is refused instead. The file "
        f"ships with the program - reinstall if it has been altered."
    )


def _coordinate(value: object) -> float | None:
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        return None
    number = float(value)
    return number if math.isfinite(number) else None


def _parse_county(path: Path, index: int, record: object) -> County:
    if not isinstance(record, dict):
        raise _refuse(path, f"county entry {index} is not an object")
    fips, name, rings = record.get("fips"), record.get("name"), record.get("rings")
    if not (isinstance(fips, str) and len(fips) == 5 and fips.isdigit() and fips.startswith("26")):
        raise _refuse(path, f"county entry {index} has FIPS {fips!r}, not a Michigan (26xxx) code")
    if not (isinstance(name, str) and name.strip() == name and name):
        raise _refuse(path, f"county {fips} has the name {name!r}")
    if not (isinstance(rings, list) and rings):
        raise _refuse(path, f"{name} County ({fips}) has no rings")
    parsed = []
    for r, ring in enumerate(rings):
        if not (isinstance(ring, list) and len(ring) >= 4):
            raise _refuse(
                path, f"{name} County ({fips}) ring {r} has fewer than 4 vertices"
            )
        vertices = []
        for v, vertex in enumerate(ring):
            if not (isinstance(vertex, list) and len(vertex) == 2):
                raise _refuse(path, f"{name} County ({fips}) ring {r} vertex {v} is not a [lon, lat] pair")
            lon, lat = _coordinate(vertex[0]), _coordinate(vertex[1])
            if lon is None or lat is None:
                raise _refuse(path, f"{name} County ({fips}) ring {r} vertex {v} is not two finite numbers: {vertex!r}")
            if not (_LON_RANGE[0] <= lon <= _LON_RANGE[1] and _LAT_RANGE[0] <= lat <= _LAT_RANGE[1]):
                raise _refuse(
                    path,
                    f"{name} County ({fips}) ring {r} vertex {v} at longitude "
                    f"{lon!r}, latitude {lat!r} is outside Michigan's neighbourhood",
                )
            vertices.append((lon, lat))
        if vertices[0] != vertices[-1]:
            raise _refuse(
                path,
                f"{name} County ({fips}) ring {r} is not closed: it starts at "
                f"{vertices[0]!r} and ends at {vertices[-1]!r}",
            )
        parsed.append(tuple(vertices))
    return County(fips=fips, name=name, rings=tuple(parsed))


def load_counties(path: Path | None = None, verify_checksum: bool = True) -> CountySet:
    """Read, authenticate and check the county file; return the prepared set.

    ``verify_checksum=False`` exists only so the structural checks can be
    exercised against a deliberately altered copy in the suite. The program
    never passes it.
    """
    path = path or DATA_DIR / COUNTIES_FILENAME
    try:
        raw = path.read_bytes()
    except OSError as error:
        raise CountyDataError(
            f"Could not read the county file at {path}: {error}. This file "
            f"ships with the program; if it is missing the installation is "
            f"incomplete."
        ) from error

    if verify_checksum:
        digest = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
        if digest != COUNTIES_SHA256:
            raise CountyDataError(
                f"{path} does not match the county file this program was "
                f"built against.\n  expected SHA-256 {COUNTIES_SHA256} "
                f"({COUNTIES_BYTES} bytes)\n  found    SHA-256 {digest} "
                f"({len(raw)} bytes)\nZones chosen from unverified county "
    if not member.endswith("_README.txt"):
        return body
    text = body.decode("utf-8")
    text = _GENERATED.sub("Generated          <normalised>", text)
    text = record_root_normalised(text, root)
    if "\nConversion         vertical only\n" in text and (
        "No State\nPlane coordinate system is involved in this job." in text
    ):
        text, _found = _respelled_as_w3(text)
        text, _found = _respelled_before_the_closing_gate(text)
    return text.encode("utf-8")


def _written(root: Path) -> dict[str, str]:
    """``{"<job>/<member>": sha256}`` for every member of every job above."""
    digests: dict[str, str] = {}
    for name, rows, fields in _jobs():
        folder = root / name
        folder.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        source = folder / "in.csv"
        # newline="\n": the digests were frozen from an LF input file, and
        # the record certifies the input's SHA-256, so Windows text-mode CRLF
        # would change the record without the program changing at all.
        source.write_text(rows, encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")
        settings = JobSettings(input_path=source, output_directory=folder, **fields)
        written = exports.write_all(run(settings), overwrite=True)
        with zipfile.ZipFile(written["archive"]) as archive:
            for member in sorted(archive.namelist()):
                body = _normalised(member, archive.read(member), root)
                digests[f"{name}/{member}"] = hashlib.sha256(body).hexdigest()
    return digests

def test_the_frozen_list_covers_every_member_of_every_job():
    """Anti-vacuity: 3 members per job, 4 for the one job converting to geodetic."""
    jobs = [name for name, _rows, _fields in _jobs()]
    assert len(FROZEN_AT_W3) == 3 * len(jobs) + 1
    for name in jobs:
        assert any(member.startswith(f"{name}/") for member in FROZEN_AT_W3)


def test_no_same_frame_job_writes_a_different_byte_than_before_w4(tmp_path):
    written = _written(tmp_path)
    assert sorted(written) == sorted(FROZEN_AT_W3)
    differing = sorted(
        member for member in FROZEN_AT_W3 if FROZEN_AT_W3[member] != written[member]
    )
    assert not differing, f"these members moved: {', '.join(differing)}"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("member_suffix", ["_README.txt", "_full.csv"])
def test_the_comparison_would_notice_a_changed_byte(tmp_path, member_suffix):
    """A digest is only a pin if a changed member produces a different one."""
    written = _written(tmp_path)
    key = next(k for k in written if k.endswith(member_suffix))
    name, member = key.split("/", 1)
    archive = next((tmp_path / name).glob("*.zip"))
    with zipfile.ZipFile(archive) as opened:
        body = _normalised(member, opened.read(member), tmp_path)
    assert hashlib.sha256(body + b"\n").hexdigest() != written[key]

    """``{job: record text}``, timestamp and root normalised, NOT respelled."""

"""Every CSV a default job writes, digested against v0.5.0. Frozen.

Closing gate, LOW 3. The ellipsoid-height feature rests on one promise: a job
that does not opt in produces exactly what the released program produced. That
promise was checked by hand at the gate — nine archives written twice, once in
a worktree at tag v0.5.0 and once at HEAD, all members digested — and the
evidence is committed at ``review/wp-e-byte-identity/``. But a captured
artifact does not fail when someone breaks it later, and the unit test that
carried the promise compared HEAD against HEAD: an unconditional regression, a
metre added to every height, left it green.

**These digests were computed by v0.5.0 itself**, not by the current code, so
they are a genuine cross-version pin. If a later change moves any byte of any
clean export or audit CSV on any of these nine ordinary jobs, this fails and
names the member.

Only the CSVs are pinned, and deliberately so: the job record embeds a
generation timestamp and the input and output paths, so its digest differs
between any two runs of identical code. What the record says is pinned by
content elsewhere, in the disclosure suites.

**The audit CSV is compared with its two DMS columns removed** (docs/DESIGN.md
amendment #66, the owner's instruction, 2026-09-03). ``Latitude (DMS)`` and
``Longitude (DMS)`` did not exist when v0.5.0 computed these digests, so the
raw member can no longer match them - and re-freezing the digests at HEAD
would have thrown away the cross-version property this file exists for.
Instead ``_without_columns`` parses the member and re-renders it through the
writer's own ``_render_csv`` with exactly those two headings dropped, and THAT
text is digested: every byte v0.5.0 wrote is still pinned to v0.5.0's own
digest, and the two new columns are pinned separately in
``tests/test_audit_dms.py``. ``test_the_stripping_is_exact`` establishes the
parse-and-render round trip reproduces an unmodified member byte for byte, so
the comparison is not passing through a lossy channel; and
``test_the_raw_member_no_longer_matches_but_the_stripped_one_does``
establishes the columns are really there - the stripped comparison would be
vacuous if nothing were stripped.

#
# * H1 - the four Source / Target degree headings of the audit CSV now name
#   their frame, and the record's FILES WRITTEN sentence says exactly what
#   is pinned true ("Every latitude, longitude and ellipsoid height column
#   names its frame", where it said "Every position and ...");
# * L3 - the Round trip paragraph names the epochs W0 measured over;
# * L2 - on the REVERSE job (NAD83(2011) to G1762, dated outside G1762's
#   period) the realization warning speaks of the OUTPUT realization.
#
# The digests are NOT re-frozen - that would discard the cross-version
# property for every other byte - but, on #66/#68's and W4b's precedent, each
# gate-2 passage, which must occur exactly once where it is expected, is
# respelled back to its W4 text before the digest
# (test_the_gate_2_respelling_is_exact pins the counts).
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

    "    DMS, and the velocity HTDP applied. Every position and ellipsoid height\n"
    "    column names its frame.\n",
)

"""Work package W4 of the WGS 84 build: no same-frame job moves by a byte.

W4 (docs/PLAN-wgs84.md section 3.6) adds the FRAME TRANSFORMATION block to
the job record, conditional columns to the audit CSV, a geodetic-to-geodetic
branch to every output, and frame-aware wording to the sentences a
transformation would otherwise make false. Every one of those is conditional
on a frame transformation having run. This file is the proof that the
condition holds the other way: a job with no transformation writes what it
wrote before W4, every member, every byte.

**The digests below were computed by the code BEFORE W4** - commit 8201968
(the W3 commit), with ``_written`` exactly as it stands here - so this is a
cross-version pin of the kind ``tests/test_orthometric_regression.py`` is
(whose eighteen v0.5.0 digests remain the floor, untouched). That file
covers nine zone-input jobs and no record; this one adds one job of every
direction that has a geodetic end or a record branch W4 touched, and digests
the RECORD too, with its two run-dependent parts normalised: the
``Generated`` timestamp, and the temporary folder every path sits under.

Jobs, and the W4 branch each one holds closed:

* ``z2z`` - zone to zone, with an outside-zone warning: the WARNINGS section
  (job-level warnings are W4's).
* ``g2z`` - NAD83(2011) geodetic to MI-C, positive west, a blank Z: the
  ELEVATIONS "does not depend on elevation" paragraph, and the OUTPUT
  section's Reference frame line.
* ``z2g_ellipsoid`` - MI-S to NAD83(2011) geodetic, ELLIPSOID heights,
  horizontal: the ELLIPSOID HEIGHT CONVERSION block's "exactly as supplied"
  sentence, the DD/DMS members and the audit CSV's ellipsoid headings.
* ``g2z_hv`` - geodetic in, horizontal and vertical NGVD 29 to NAVD 88: the
  vertical blocks beside the geodetic input block.
* ``vo_zone`` and ``vo_geodetic`` - vertical only, on a zone and on
  NAD83(2011) positions with ellipsoid heights: the two vertical-only
  records, which W4 does not alter.
"""

"""The results table's model.

Holds nothing but strings. Every one of them was produced by
``michspc.fileio.formatting`` — the same functions the written report and the
audit CSV call — so the screen and the files on disk cannot disagree about what
a number is (docs/method/METHOD.md section 5, "UI honesty").

The model therefore performs no arithmetic at all. It does not round, it does
not scale a unit, it does not decide what an absent value looks like. Those are
domain decisions and they live one layer down.
"""

def _assignment(zone_code: str, county: str, fips: str):
    from michspc.spc.autozone import ZoneAssignment
    from michspc.spc.zones import zone_by_code

    return ZoneAssignment(
        zone=zone_by_code(zone_code), county_name=county, county_fips=fips
    )


def test_the_autozone_check_fails_when_every_point_gets_one_zone(monkeypatch):
    """A lookup answering Michigan South for everything: Kent is right by
    accident, Marquette is not."""
    from michspc import job

    monkeypatch.setattr(
        job, "zone_for", lambda lat, lon, system, counties: _assignment("2113", "Kent", "26081")
    )
    with pytest.raises(selftest.SelfTestError) as raised:
        selftest.check_autozone()
    message = str(raised.value)
    assert "where NGS NCAT chooses zone 2111, Marquette County" in message
    assert "in zone 2113, Kent County" in message

def _sha256_of(path: Path) -> str:
    digest = hashlib.sha256()
    with path.open("rb") as stream:
        for block in iter(lambda: stream.read(1 << 20), b""):
            digest.update(block)
    return digest.hexdigest()


def foreign_bundle_files(bundle_dir: Path, review_dir: Path = REVIEW_DIR) -> list[str]:
    """Files in a built bundle that must never ship, as bundle-relative paths.

    Three rules, any one of which refuses a file:

    * a Fortran source file anywhere (by suffix, any case);
    * anything under a directory named like one of
      ``NEVER_SHIPPED_DIRECTORIES``;
    * **provenance**: a file whose SHA-256 equals that of any file under
      ``review_dir`` - whatever it is called and wherever it sits. A copy of
      ``review/wgs84-w0/htdp_truth.json`` renamed into ``_internal/data/``
      passes both name rules; it does not pass this one, and its entry says
      which review/ file it is (closing gate of the WGS 84 build, finding L4:
      the claim "nothing from review/" was a check of names only).

    A zero-byte file is exempt from the provenance rule: it is byte-identical
    to every other empty file, so its digest says nothing about where it came
    from. Only bundle files whose size equals some review/ file's are hashed,
    so the review/ folder (well over 100 MB) is not read for a bundle that
    shares nothing with it.

    Empty for a correct bundle. A function of its own so the rule is checked
    by the suite on synthetic trees (``tests/test_selftest.py``), not only on
    the owner's machine.
    """
    if not bundle_dir.exists():
        return []
    review_by_size: dict[int, list[Path]] = {}
    if review_dir.is_dir():
        for path in sorted(review_dir.rglob("*")):
            if path.is_file():
                size = path.stat().st_size
                if size:
                    review_by_size.setdefault(size, []).append(path)
    review_digests: dict[Path, str] = {}

    def review_copy_of(path: Path) -> str | None:
        candidates = review_by_size.get(path.stat().st_size)
        if not candidates:
            return None
        digest = _sha256_of(path)
        for candidate in candidates:
            if candidate not in review_digests:
                review_digests[candidate] = _sha256_of(candidate)
            if review_digests[candidate] == digest:
                return candidate.relative_to(review_dir.parent).as_posix()
        return None

    found: list[str] = []
    for path in sorted(bundle_dir.rglob("*")):
        if not path.is_file():
            continue
        relative = path.relative_to(bundle_dir)
        if path.suffix.lower() in FORTRAN_SUFFIXES or (
            NEVER_SHIPPED_DIRECTORIES & {part.lower() for part in relative.parts[:-1]}
        ):
            found.append(relative.as_posix())
            continue
        copy_of = review_copy_of(path)
        if copy_of is not None:
            found.append(f"{relative.as_posix()} (a copy of {copy_of})")
    return found

"""Widget builders and form rules shared by the two tabs.

Everything here was factored out of ``michspc.gui.window`` unchanged, so that a
second tab can build the *same* controls rather than a lookalike set of its own.
A duplicated zone dropdown that drifted by one item, or a second copy of the
direction rule that disagreed on one combination, would be two views of the same
question giving two answers — which is the failure this feature is forbidden to
create (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #26).

**This module never computes a domain value**, for the same reason
``window`` does not: a number produced here would be a second authoritative
representation of a fact the core already owns.

It deliberately imports nothing from ``michspc.gui.window``. The dependency runs
one way — window imports controls — so that neither tab can reach the other
through this module.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import datetime
import traceback
from dataclasses import dataclass

from PySide6.QtCore import QDate, QEvent, QObject, Qt
from PySide6.QtWidgets import (
    QAbstractSpinBox,
    QButtonGroup,
    QComboBox,
    QDateEdit,
    QMessageBox,
    QRadioButton,
)

UNCHOSEN = "unchosen"
"""Sentinel for a dropdown the user has not answered yet.

Not the same thing as a default. A combo box that opens on a real value has
answered a question the user was never asked, which is precisely the failure the
longitude convention rule exists to prevent, so the zone and convention combos
open on a placeholder and Convert stays disabled until they are answered.
"""

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GeodeticChoice:
    """"This side of the conversion is latitude/longitude, in THIS frame".

    Carried in the same dropdown as the zones because the direction of a job is
    exactly the question "what is it coming from, and what is it going to" — the
    program converts zone to zone, geodetic to zone, and zone to geodetic
    (docs/DESIGN.md section 2), and one pair of dropdowns states all three.

    **It replaces a bare ``"geodetic"`` string, and the frame is the whole
    reason** (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #62, and #58 before it). Until the
    SPCS2022 zones landed there was exactly one frame a geodetic position could
    be in, so a sentinel that carried nothing was carrying everything there was
    to carry. There are two now. NAD83(2011) and NATRF2022 differ by one to two
    metres in Michigan and nothing in a latitude shows which one it is, so a
    geodetic selection that does not name its frame is the #58 failure with a
    second frame instead of WGS 84: the number pastes in cleanly and converts to
    something plausible and wrong.

    A frozen dataclass rather than an enum member so the frame record itself
    travels with the selection — ``settings()`` on both tabs reads
    ``choice.frame`` straight into ``JobSettings.geodetic_frame``, and no
    interface anywhere maps a label back to a frame.

    **The records below are the only ones that exist**, for the reason the zone
    records are singletons: ``QComboBox.findData`` compares stored Python
    objects by identity, not by ``==``, so a second equal instance is not
    findable in a dropdown that holds the first. Build one through
    ``geodetic_choice``; never construct one at a call site.
    """

    frame: ReferenceFrame

    @property
    def label(self) -> str:
        """What the dropdown calls this choice. Derived, never typed."""
        return geodetic_label(self.frame)

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return self.label


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AutoChoice:
    """"Choose each point's zone from its latitude/longitude, in THIS frame".

    The Auto entry of a TO dropdown (docs/PLAN-options-autozone.md L1, L2;
    DESIGN.md #71). ``settings()`` on both tabs turns it into
    ``JobSettings.target_zone=job.AUTO_ZONE`` with ``auto_frame=choice.frame``,
    so the frame travels WITH the selection, exactly as ``GeodeticChoice``
michspc/gui/controls.py:72:class GeodeticChoice:
michspc/gui/controls.py:114:class AutoChoice:
michspc/gui/controls.py:1406:OBSERVATION_DATE_UNANSWERED = QDate(1752, 9, 14)
michspc/gui/controls.py:1425:class _CalendarOpensOnToday(QObject):
michspc/gui/controls.py:1452:class ObservationDateEdit(QDateEdit):
michspc/gui/results_model.py:318:AMBER = QColor(255, 233, 178)
michspc/gui/results_model.py:422:class ResultValue:
michspc/gui/results_model.py:430:class ResultSection:
michspc/gui/results_model.py:839:class _Positions:
michspc/gui/results_model.py:1472:class ResultsModel(QAbstractTableModel):
Chosen light enough that the system's ordinary black text stays readable on it,
because the palette is otherwise the native one.
"""


def row_strings(result: JobResult) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]:
    """Render one job's points as display strings.

    The northing/easting/elevation branch below is deliberately identical to
    ``michspc.fileio.exports.clean_pnezd_rows``: when a job converts to geodetic
    the first two columns hold a latitude and a longitude, not a grid
    coordinate. The elevation is in the OUTPUT unit in every direction,
    including that one - it is the only linear column left on a geodetic
    export and the job record describes it in the output unit (WP-R2 fix A).
    If those two ever diverge, the screen would be describing a different file
    from the one written.
    """
    settings = result.settings
    to_geodetic = _geodetic_display_columns(settings)
    vertical = settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations
    zone_columns = _shows_zone_columns(settings)
    auto = is_auto_zone_job(settings)

    rows: list[tuple[str, ...]] = []

    finally:
        built.close()
    assert settings_store.PRODUCTION_STORES_BUILT == 0


def test_the_production_store_is_named_as_the_plan_states():
    """``HKCU\\Software\\DMARTIN\\MCX - Martin Coordinate Exchange`` (plan L9):
    the organisation is the publisher, the application the window title -
    one derivation, so the key cannot drift from the name the window shows."""
    assert settings_store.ORGANIZATION == APP_PUBLISHER == "DMARTIN"
    assert settings_store.APPLICATION == WINDOW_TITLE
    assert WINDOW_TITLE == "MCX - Martin Coordinate Exchange"


def test_the_qsettings_adapter_round_trips_through_an_ini_file(qapp, tmp_path):
    """The adapter the production store uses, exercised on a temp INI file -
    never the native registry - so what is written is what is read back."""
    path = tmp_path / "mcx.ini"
    store = settings_store.QSettingsStore(ini_path=path)
    chosen = with_time_dependent(date_source=ObservationDateSource.ENTERED)
    options.save_options(store, chosen)
    again = settings_store.QSettingsStore(ini_path=path)
    loaded, messages = options.load_options(again)
    assert messages == ()
    assert loaded == chosen
    assert path.exists()


# ==========================================================================
# 2. The Options value, its defaults, and what is remembered.
# ==========================================================================


def test_the_groups_and_their_labels_are_derived_from_the_registries():
    """The dialog's nine groups, in the brief's order, each named from its own
    record (no second copy of a name): the two zone systems by ``Zone.system``,
    then one latitude/longitude group per offered frame by ``frame.code``."""
    assert [group.label for group in options.LIST_GROUPS] == [
        "SPCS 83 zones",
        "SPCS2022 zones",
        "NAD83(2011) latitude/longitude",
        "NATRF2022 latitude/longitude",
        "WGS84(G1762) latitude/longitude",
        "WGS84(G2139) latitude/longitude",
        "WGS84(G2296) latitude/longitude",
        "ITRF2014 latitude/longitude",
        "ITRF2020 latitude/longitude",
    ]
    for group in options.LIST_GROUPS:
        if group.is_zone_group:
            assert group.label == f"{group.system} zones"
        else:
            assert group.label == f"{group.frame.code} latitude/longitude"


def test_the_defaults_are_all_on_but_the_five_time_dependent_frames():
    """Plan section 1 item 1: every group on except the five WGS 84 / ITRF,
    decided by each frame's own ``is_time_dependent`` - and Auto on
    NAD83(2011), the date "today" (O3)."""
    default = options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS
    for group in options.LIST_GROUPS:
        off = (not group.is_zone_group) and group.frame.is_time_dependent
        assert default.is_group_shown(group) is (not off), group.label
    assert sum(not default.is_group_shown(g) for g in options.LIST_GROUPS) == 5
    assert default.auto_frame is NAD83_2011
    assert default.date_source is ObservationDateSource.TODAY


def test_an_options_value_that_would_empty_a_list_cannot_exist():
    """L8 in the value itself, not only in the dialog."""
    geodetic_only = frozenset(
        g.key for g in options.LIST_GROUPS if not g.is_zone_group
    )
    zones_only = frozenset(g.key for g in options.LIST_GROUPS if g.is_zone_group)
    with pytest.raises(options.OptionsError, match="State Plane zones"):
        replaced(options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS, shown=geodetic_only)
    with pytest.raises(options.OptionsError, match="latitude/longitude group"):
        replaced(options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS, shown=zones_only)
    with pytest.raises(options.OptionsError, match="AUTO_FRAMES"):
        replaced(options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS, auto_frame=WGS84_G2296)


def test_remembered_options_are_read_back_by_the_next_window(qapp, store):
    """O3: the three choices are remembered - a second window built on the
    same store opens on them."""
    first = MainWindow(store=store)
    chosen = replaced(
        with_time_dependent(date_source=ObservationDateSource.ENTERED),
        auto_frame=NATRF2022,
    )
    assert first.apply_options(chosen) is True
    first.close()

    second = MainWindow(store=store)
    try:
        assert second.options == chosen
        assert second.single_point.options == chosen
        assert second.options_status == ""
    finally:
        second.close()


def test_a_date_is_never_remembered(qapp, store):
    """O3: "the choice is remembered; the date is not". A date answered under
    "Enter a date" is in no stored value, and the next window's box opens
    unanswered."""
    first = recorded(MainWindow(store=store))
    first.apply_options(
        with_time_dependent(date_source=ObservationDateSource.ENTERED)
    )
    for page in (first, first.single_point):
        page.observation_date_edit.setDate(QDate(2025, 6, 15))
    first.apply_options(first.options.with_groups(
        options.group_of(TIME_DEPENDENT[0]), shown=False
    ))
    first.close()

    iso = re.compile(r"\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}|2025")
    for key, value in store.values.items():
        assert not iso.search(str(value)), (key, value)
        assert "date" not in key or key == options.DATE_SOURCE_KEY, key
    assert set(store.values) == {
        *(group.key for group in options.LIST_GROUPS),
        options.AUTO_FRAME_KEY,
        options.DATE_SOURCE_KEY,
    }

    second = MainWindow(store=store)
    try:
        for page in (second, second.single_point):
            assert page.observation_date_edit.is_answered() is False
    finally:
        second.close()


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "key, raw, named",
    [
        ("lists/geodetic/WGS84(G2296)", "True", "Show WGS84(G2296) latitude/longitude"),
        ("lists/zones/SPCS 83", "1", "Show SPCS 83 zones"),
        (options.AUTO_FRAME_KEY, "NAD83", "Auto zone frame"),
        (options.DATE_SOURCE_KEY, "yesterday", "Observation date"),
        (options.DATE_SOURCE_KEY, 7, "Observation date"),
    ],
)
def test_an_unrecognised_stored_value_is_reset_and_said(qapp, key, raw, named):
    """L9: a value MCX does not write is never coerced ("True", "1", a short
    frame code, a non-string). It falls back to that key's default, the
    status line names the option, and the default is written back so it is
    said once."""
    store = settings_store.MemoryStore({key: raw})
    win = MainWindow(store=store)
    try:
        assert win.options == options.DEFAULT_OPTIONS
        assert f'"{named}"' in win.options_status
        assert "reset to its default" in win.options_status
        assert win.status_label.text() == win.options_status
        assert win.single_point.status_label.text() == win.options_status
        assert store.values[key] != raw
    finally:
        win.close()
    # Said once: the next window reads the written-back default silently.
    again = MainWindow(store=store)
    try:
        assert again.options_status == ""
    finally:
        again.close()


def test_stored_lists_that_would_be_empty_are_reset_and_said(qapp):
    """L8 at load: every zone group remembered OFF would leave no zone to
    choose, so the lists reset to their defaults and the status says so."""
    store = settings_store.MemoryStore(
        {g.key: "false" for g in options.LIST_GROUPS if g.is_zone_group}
    )
    win = MainWindow(store=store)
    try:
        assert win.options.shown == options.DEFAULT_SHOWN
        assert "nothing to choose" in win.options_status
    finally:
        win.close()

## Built against NGS's beta products

As in 0.7.0 and 0.7.1: NGS's SPCS2022 zone definitions and its NCAT v3
service are pre-release, and this release is built against them
deliberately, at the owner's instruction (`docs/REFREEZE-NSRS.md`). Auto
zone on NATRF2022 adds two more beta-derived artifacts: the county to
SPCS2022 zone table, measured from NGS's beta zone polygons, and the beta
NCAT zone picks it is tested against. The release build acknowledges
**seven** beta-derived artifacts, where 0.7.0 and 0.7.1 acknowledged five.
HTDP is not beta: it is NGS's released program. **When NGS changes HTDP,
every HTDP-derived number in MCX is recaptured** (`docs/REFREEZE-HTDP.md`),
and the monthly tripwire now watches NGS's HTDP repository for that change.

## Verified

- **HTDP's own output, at its printed digit.** NGS's source was compiled and
  run (gfortran, x86_64). MCX reproduces 1,846 of HTDP's printed records,
  every offered frame in both directions and the round trip at every
  calendar-equivalent epoch, to 1e-10 degree and 1 mm, compared component
  by component. A further 382 records were chosen to sit within an ulp or
  so of HTDP's rounding or at the velocity grid's edge: MCX reproduces the
  374 it transforms to the same tolerance, and refuses the other 8, which
  lie just outside the grid's edge, as it must. W0's transcription
  matched 45,408 of 45,408 records, and 720 fresh random records were matched
  when the transcription was built.
- **NGS's own Windows `htdp.exe`**, the binary at the pinned commit with its
  git blob verified, was run on the owner's machine over every anchor input
  (`review/wgs84-w0/ngs-windows-check-2026-10-22.txt`). Verdict: **last digit
  only.** 2,093 of 2,228 records are identical. 6 of the 1,846 ordinary
  anchors and 128 of the records tuned to sit at a rounding tie differ by
  one unit in the last printed place, exactly as predicted from a 32-bit
  stand-in built beforehand. The one other record is one MCX refuses, just
  outside its velocity grid, so it is listed and does not decide anything.
- **The transcription is checked against the Fortran itself**: the suite
  re-reads NGS's committed source and requires every transcribed constant,
  and all 480 velocity-grid nodes, to equal the number NGS wrote.
- **Independent checks.** The first interim reviewer compared 617,164 fresh
  records against its own HTDP build and found no difference at the printed
  digit. The second ran 1,500 random transformed jobs, about 6,700 points,
  through the real file path against its own HTDP build plus the projection,
  geoid and VERTCON steps, and nothing differed.
- **Auto zone against NGS NCAT.** 247 frozen NCAT zone picks, every one
  outside the bands near a zone line where NCAT disagrees with itself: 124
  from production NCAT for SPCS 83 and 123 from beta NCAT for SPCS2022, on
  land, on the Great Lakes, and outside Michigan. Auto agrees with every
  one, and refuses every point NCAT puts outside Michigan. The SPCS 83
  county table was checked against MCL 54.231 as captured, and the SPCS2022
  table re-derived from NGS's native polygon capture, whose lines lie on
  TIGER's county lines to 0.025 m at worst over 11,875 shared edges. The
  on-line rule was proven on all 30,871 shared TIGER county edges. 223
  fixed-zone job configurations, 667 files, were compared byte for byte
  against the build before the Auto zone output work: all identical.
- **A self-test run inside the frozen application**, now fourteen checks: it
  converts one WGS 84 (G2296) position into Michigan Central against HTDP's
  own printed output projected, proves the bare-WGS 84 and
  WGS 84-to-SPCS2022 refusals are alive in the shipped binary, and runs an
  Auto zone job on the shipped county file: two points land in the zones
  NGS NCAT chose for them (Michigan South for Kent County, Michigan North for
  Marquette County), at exactly the fixed-zone coordinates and within 0.3 mm
  of NCAT's printed coordinates (the check allows 2 mm), and a point in
  Toledo, Ohio refuses the whole job.
- **The automated tests on the owner's Windows 11 machine.** The WGS 84 part
  was built in a cloud container, where 4,793 tests passed in both run
  modes. On the owner's machine 30 tests then failed, none for a program
  defect: Windows paths and a Windows line ending in two tests that compare
  job records to frozen digests; three NGS HTDP captures rewritten with
  Windows line endings by git; 19 HTDP records (18 of the records tuned to
  sit at a rounding tie, and one at the velocity grid's edge) that MCX
  prints one unit differently on Windows, because Windows' C library
  computes trigonometry differently from Linux's in the last bit; and one
  list in a test whose order depends on the platform. All were fixed in the
  tests, the 19 records by an exception limited to exactly those records
  (4,968 passing in both modes). With
  the Options and Auto zone work, **5,754 tests pass in both run modes** on
  the owner's machine; the release build runs them again as gate 4.
- **Nine build gates**, unchanged in number. The bundle gate also refuses a
  bundle carrying NGS's HTDP Fortran or anything from the repository's
  `review/` folder, and requires every file in `data/`, now including the
  county file, to be in the bundle.
- **The WGS 84 part's gates ran under an independent Opus reviewer**, blind
  to the build's own reading, because the Codex reviewer the method names
  was not available in the cloud container it was built in (the owner's
  standing fallback). Two interim gates and a closing gate ran. The first
  interim gate returned seven LOW findings; the second two HIGH, one MEDIUM
  and five LOW; the closing gate one MEDIUM and seven LOW. None was a wrong
  number. All are fixed, each pinned at the reviewer's own input, and each
  gate's fixes passed a narrowing re-confirmation. The closing gate's
  reviewer drove the real interface on both tabs, 2,050 Single point
  configurations and 540 Multi point jobs, against its own HTDP build with
  no mismatch.
- **The Options and Auto zone part's interim gate ran under Codex** over the
  county data, the zone lookup and Auto in the conversion
  (`review/gate-autozone-1/`). It found no wrong zone and returned one HIGH
  and one MEDIUM. The HIGH: two public routines that turn a result into file
  text could still produce an Auto job's CAD file, with points from
  different zones in one unlabelled file, getting around the guard that held
  Auto jobs back until their job record existed. The guard was replaced by
  the real Auto output, and the CAD file routine now requires each point's
  zone on an Auto job and refuses a zone the job did not use, so points from
  two zones cannot reach one file. The MEDIUM: the SPCS2022 table's test read a
  copy of NGS's polygons that NGS's server had shifted about 0.9 m, while the
  agreement it cited was measured on the unshifted capture; the test now
  reads the unshifted capture. Both are fixed and pinned at the reviewer's
  own inputs.
